# @graview/core

## 0.1.47

### Patch Changes

- 6d84721: A view's check knows the place of a titled view the host keeps, and `describePlace` describes that place (Roadmap f-check-view-places). Threat Watch, on Graview Cloud, keeps a view of many threats titled "Live threats", drawn at `/places/live-threats`; its home linked there with `<a data-place="live-threats">`, the link worked on the page, and `propose_view`'s check said "It names the place "live-threats", which this app does not have" — a false warning that teaches a chat to stop writing `data-place`. The check was handed the document's places and never the host's views, and `describe_place` said the place was not one the app has. Now `runWorkerViewHeadless` takes `views`, the manifests of the views the host keeps — the list `placesOf` and `describePlace` already take — and each titled view of many is a place at the slug the router gives it, through `viewPlaceOf` on `@graview/core/seat`, the function `placesOf` itself reads, so a `data-place`, a `graview.navigate` or a press on a link naming one is no problem; the view being run is a place of its own when it is titled. A name none of the places is still goes nowhere and says so. A run that is not handed `views` cannot know the host's views' places, so it no longer says such a name for certain: "It names the place "live-threats", which is none of the places this run was told of. … This run was not handed the views the host keeps, so a titled view's place is not among these: if it names one, the link goes there." `graview view check` hands `views: []`, since the app's registered views are its places already, and says what it said before. `describePlace` handed `views` finds a titled view's place by its slug, its title or its address, and says it as it says a lens's: its title, slug and address, drawn by `view:<name>`, one `{ t: "view" }` part with the view's title, whether it flows with the page or keeps a frame of its own (`flows`, only where the host said), and the parts a headless run drew; its text says `[the view "Live threats", flowing with the page]` and what the view drew under it. `graview-view-links` says a titled view's place is a place a link may name, and `graview-host-page` that `describePlace` and `runWorkerViewHeadless` take the views. `core`'s tests describe a titled view's place by slug, title and address, flowing, framed and unsaid, and none where the host names no such view; `guest`'s find a `data-place` and a navigate naming a titled view's place not flagged and a press on it opening it, a slug naming nothing still flagged for certain, a run without the views saying it as a doubt, and the place a run drew described.
  
  Compatibility: additive in the API — `runWorkerViewHeadless`'s `views`, `viewPlaceOf` on `@graview/core/seat`, `DescribedHostView.flows` and `hidden`, and `flows` on a `{ t: "view" }` part. A behavior change in a headless run that is not handed `views`: a `data-place`, a `data-record` or a navigate naming none of its places is said as a doubt rather than as no place of the app, and a titled view's own place is among its places; `describePlace` handed `views` describes a titled view's place it refused before. The manifest, the declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.46

### Patch Changes

- 15f234f: A titled worker view's place flows with the page, and a brand may keep the line under the app's name off the home without blanking the description. Every view opened at `/places/<slug>` was drawn in a frame `min(72vh, 760px)` tall with a border that scrolled inside itself, under the page's own scroll — on a phone, a box that traps the thumb — while the home's view and a place of blocks flowed. `registerWorkerView` now marks the view it registers `flows`, as a place of blocks is, and its place is drawn in the page's column as long as the view draws; a calendar, a board, the columns and the other pictures keep their frame. `graview-worker-view` says a view's place flows with the page and needs no height to fill one. And with a home view the routed face drew the document's `description` above it, so a view with its own hero said everything twice, and the only way to stop it was to delete the description, which took it out of the app's listings too. A document's `brand.subtitle: false` now keeps the line off the home — over a home view, under a home of blocks and on the derived home alike — and the description stays the app's: the brand's `subtitle`, the name's hover on the bar, its listings. `set-brand` writes it (`subtitle: false`; `true` or `null` draws the line again) and says so in words, the diff of a change says it the same way, the compiled brand carries `subtitleDrawn: false`, and `describePlace` leaves the line out of the home's masthead. Left out, the line is drawn as it always was. `graview-brand` documents the key. `guest`'s tests find a titled view's place marked to flow and drawn with no height and nothing scrolling inside it, and `pages`' find a calendar, a board and the columns keeping their frame; `verify-address` opens a kept view's place and the board at 1440 and 390 in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, and finds no box between the view and the page that scrolls while the board's frame does (aTitledViewsPlaceFlowsWithThePageWhileABoardKeepsItsFrame). `core`'s tests find the key validated as false and refused as anything else, written by `set-brand` and taken back by `true` and `null` to the very document, and the masthead without the line; `pages`' find the home drawing no line with it and the line without it, the description still the name's hover. A hosted page carries 0.0 KB more up front (3 bytes, 1 gzipped) and the guest host registering a worker view 6 (1), under raises of those budgets by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-views-place-flows-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the declaration — `brand.subtitle`, optional and only `false`; a document without it draws as it did, and a build that predates it refuses a document that carries it. Additive in the API: `Brand.subtitleDrawn` and the `set-brand` edit's `subtitle`. A titled worker view's place no longer scrolls inside itself; the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 99d25eb: Built-in views look designed: one type scale, one left edge and one heading per page on the place header, blocks lenses, grouped row and card lists, kind lists and record pages (Roadmap f-auto-view-type-polish). Threat Watch's By type page stacked two headings — the place's title and count, then the lens's own larger headline saying the count again — inset its rows 16 pixels past the title's edge, ruled each group label with a one-sided accent, drew 69 row titles in the display face, said "high · resolved · supply-chain · disclosed May 18, 2026" as one string, and pressed its arrow against "All threats as a list→". Now a blocks lens that opens with a headline hands it to its page, which says it as the line under the title in place of the count and draws it once; on its page the lens stands in the page's own column, with no inset of its own; a group's heading is its label and how many it holds, in the text face, over the rows' own hairline; a record's name in a row or on a card is the text face at medium or semibold weight and normal tracking, never the display face; a card's or a row's line of facts with a choice field named bare in it draws each choice as a small chip and the rest as quiet words after them, in the order written, and a kind's list draws its glance's choices the same way; an arrow after a link (→, ↗) stands a space from its words and is not underlined; a place's header, its first block, its blocks and its groups are spaced on a 4px scale, given as tokens (`--graview-space-1` … `--graview-space-7`: 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48); a page's title steps one step down the scale on a phone; and on a desk wide enough for a margin, a kind's list hangs its choose boxes in that margin so its names start on the title's edge. A hosted page carries 0.3 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and their budgets are raised by that; the pages face alone by what it measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; the declaration is unchanged and no check finding is added. New: a resolved text block on a card or a row carries `runs` (each fact, a choice marked by its `field`) where one of its facts is a choice field named bare; describe_place says such a line as it is drawn, a chip a space from what is beside it ("High Contained Supply chain disclosed May 18, 2026") and a grouped list's group carries its `count`, said in the text as "Supply-chain compromises (6):"; the theme writes `--graview-space-1` … `--graview-space-7`; `PlacePageHead` on `@graview/primitives/pages`. What Graview Cloud does: nothing to keep Threat Watch's v19 workaround — a lens may open with a headline again, and its page says it once, under the title.

## 0.1.45

### Patch Changes

- 7069188: A form for a visitor: one act of an app opened to people who never join the workspace — an RSVP, a sign-up — drawn alone, run as a visitor the host mints per answer, its answer landing as a record and nothing else of the app shown. Roadmap f-public-forms, filed by Graview Cloud: "The pieces are in place (people records, link roles, the files route); what is left is a form page per kind for a visitor with no account, a minted author per submission ('form:<token>'), abuse limits, and the plan gate." The framework's half is here; abuse limits, the plan gate and minting the token stay the host's.
  
  The declaration says the form: `forms.<name>` in a document, `forms` on `defineApp` — `act` (one that makes a record or acts on none), `title`, `intro`, `fields` (the arguments asked, in order; unsaid, every one a visitor could fill), `say` (what the visitor reads under "Received — thank you."), `submit` and `role` (`visitor`, unsaid). It is not a second permission system: a visitor is a principal holding that role, so the policy says what they may do and see as it says it for anybody — a grant lets the role run the act, and a sight that never names it keeps every record from it. `formVisitor(app, "rsvp", "form:<token>")` (`@graview/core/forms`) mints `{ kind: "human", id, name: "a visitor via the RSVP form", roles: ["visitor"] }`, so Activity names each answer for the form and never by its token (`nameOfAuthor` says "a visitor via a form" for a `form:` id that carries no name); `visitorForm(app, name)` resolves the act, the fields and the words; `formReceived` is what is said after. `graview check` holds a form to what a visitor can do: `form-unknown-act`, `form-act-on-a-record`, `form-without-sight` (no policy, or one that says nothing of who sees what, so the visitor would be served every record), `form-act-not-granted`, `form-field-unknown`, `form-omits-required` and `form-field-unfillable` (a required picker over records the visitor is not served, or a field their sight keeps from them) are errors; `form-visitor-sees` (a sight serves the visitor a kind — to everybody, or by name where no picker asks for it) and `form-visitor-may-more` (the role may run acts no form opens) are warnings.
  
  `@graview/embed/form`'s `mount(el, { app, form: "rsvp", principal: "form:<token>", remote })` draws the form alone — no bar, no lists, no record, no seat — over the store as the visitor is served it, runs the act as them, says it was received in the declaration's words with the keyboard on what it said, and tells `onSubmitted`; `principal` is the minted id or the visitor whole, and a form the declaration has not got is refused by its name. `@graview/pages/visitor-form`'s `VisitorFormPage` is the same for a React host. The derived form asks the arguments `only` names in that order. `graview preview` serves each form at `/forms/<name>`, a visitor minted per page and handed only what that visitor is served, and the page's head no longer preloads the preview seat's document there. The store, the act and the served seat needed nothing new: the visitor's seat is served nothing, its log withholds every record, and an id it guesses is refused as missing.
  
  `core`'s tests read the form from a document and give it back, resolve its fields and words, mint the visitor, run the act as them and find the record on the whole store and its author named for the form, refuse another act, serve the seat nothing before and after its answer (its answer and its log name no other record, and a guessed id is told the same as an absent one), and say every finding; `pages`' draw the RSVP alone, submit as the visitor and say it was received; `embed`'s mount it and answer it. `pnpm verify preview` opens `/forms/rsvp` over a seeded RSVP at 390 and 1440 in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: the title and its four fields, no bar and no link, nothing sideways, the guest on the store as "a visitor via the RSVP form" with a visitor minted per page, the keyboard on "Received", and none of the records already there drawn on the page or in anything the server sent it. The `graview-visitor-forms` skill says the declaration, the grant and the mount, and `graview-permissions` points at it. A visitor's form alone is a new bundle budget, set at 373_310 / 143_118 (380_000 / 146_000), with none of the bar, the notices or the first look (the frame's store and host scheme are a module of their own, `frame-light.ts`). A hosted page carries 0.2 KB more up front (232 bytes, 87 gzipped: the document's `forms` shape and "a visitor via a form"), and the compiled page 0.1 KB (88 bytes, 45 gzipped), under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-form-for-a-visitor-a-hosted-page*.json`); the pages face alone 93 bytes over its budget minified, none gzipped, under a raise (`a-form-for-a-visitor-pages-face.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive for the declaration and the document format — `forms` is a new optional key, which a build before this refuses in a document as a key it does not know; the `form-*` check codes are new. Additive in the API: `@graview/embed/form` (`mount`, `FormEmbed`, `FormEmbedOptions`, `FormEmbedHandle`), `@graview/pages/visitor-form` (`VisitorFormPage`), `formVisitor`, `visitorForm`, `formReceived`, `visitorSeesKind`, `pickersIn`, `VISITOR_ROLE`, `VisitorForm` on `@graview/core/forms`, `VisitorFormDeclaration` and `GraviewApp.forms`, `themeBaseCss` on `@graview/primitives/pages`. The wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged: a visitor is a seat like any other, said in the seat headers a host already believes.
- 7814c9f: A record page with a page slot and a view of its own says where each is drawn. Threat Watch, on Graview Cloud, gave a kind page blocks ("Read the sources", set with `set-view slot: "page"`) and a record view, and `describe_place` said neither the slot nor any sign of it, while on a kind with no view the slot headed the page. On the routed face the order is one and is now written down: the view, then the slot's blocks, then the facts they did not say — and the facts did say again what the slot said, since the view carried none of the slot's `says`; `registerWorkerView` now hands it on (FR-141), so the record page leaves those out under a view as it does without one. A view whose manifest says `replaces: "page"` is the whole page, as before, and the slot is never drawn. `describePlace` takes the host's views (`DescribePlaceOptions.views`, each a `DescribedHostView`: its manifest's `name`, `title`, `attach`, `cardinality` and `replaces`, and the `parts` a headless run drew), and a record's page whose kind has a view of one says a new part, `{ t: "view" }`, at its head, then the slot, then the facts; where the view replaces the page it says the view alone, is drawn by `view:<name>`, and says a problem at `views.<kind>.page`: the slot is never drawn. `shadowedPageSlots(app, views)` on `@graview/core/describe` says the same problems for a host's list beside `graview check`'s, which does not see the host's views. `graview-view-specs` says where a page slot draws beside a view and what `replaces` does to it, and `graview-worker-view` says the same in a line. `core`'s tests describe a record with a view that sits above the page, with none, and with one that replaces it; `guest`'s draw each on the pages face and find the slot under the view and over the facts, a fact the slot said not said again, and no slot under a view that replaces the page; `verify-address` draws both at 390 and 1440 (aRecordPagesSlotIsDrawnUnderItsViewAndAboveTheFacts, aViewThatReplacesThePageDrawsAloneAndItsSlotIsNot). The guest host registering a worker view carries 71 bytes more (23 gzipped), under a raise of its budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-records-page-slot-is-drawn-under-its-view-guest-host.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the API — `DescribePlaceOptions.views`, `DescribedHostView` and `shadowedPageSlots`; a `DescribedPart` may now be `{ t: "view" }`, said only when the host passes its views, so a description asked for as before reads as before. A behavior change on the record page under a view that sits above the page: a fact the kind's page blocks say is not listed again among the facts. The manifest, the declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.44

### Patch Changes

- 68b7837: A compacted store serves no unseen id (FR-199). Found building FR-198: a store opened compacted, from a checkpoint and the tail after it, as Graview Cloud opens its rooms, never read the ops behind its undo horizon, so a record made and removed there was no record to it. A field that still named one was served as it stood to a seat that may not see its kind, and the hidden record's id, minted from its label, went with it: a `pub` whose `ref` named a seed `secret` removed behind the horizon was served `ref: "secret:s0"` in the seat's graph, its log, its epochs, the live welcome, `/graview/state`, `/graview/since` and `/graview/export`. Over 1,000 random worlds compacted twice and reopened, 43 leaked, and in 348 the welcome a seat opened with was not the one the never-compacted store sent, because a field the seat wrote itself behind the horizon was no longer known to be its own words. Now `store.checkpoint` keeps two more things beside `creators`: `removed`, the kind of every record made or removed behind it that its graph does not hold, and `writers`, who wrote each field of its graph that names a record. A store opened on the checkpoint judges a field naming a removed record as the whole log would, clearing it or withholding the record, and serves a seat its own words as before. Neither is ever served to a seat: a seat's epochs carry their graph and their makers only. A checkpoint written before this has neither, and a store opened on one fails closed: an id it cannot place that begins with a kind the app declares, `secret:s0`, is judged as a removed record of that kind, kept from a seat that may not see every record of it. A store `openRemote` holds, which its host already judged, shows what it holds. A host that keeps the archive mends an old checkpoint by opening the whole history once, taking the checkpoint again at the same seq and storing it in place of the old one. `core`'s test runs the 1,000 worlds through two compactions and a reopening: no unseen id in any seat's graph, log or epochs, the same view the never-compacted store serves, the same for a checkpoint mended over the archive, and no unseen id from an old checkpoint either. `ship`'s runs them over the welcome and the routes, with the same welcome as the never-compacted store. Now 0 leak and 0 differ. A hosted page carries 1.0 KB more up front (1,025 bytes, about 360 gzipped; the compiled page the same), what a checkpoint keeps of the records behind it and the seat view judging from it, under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-compacted-store-serves-no-unseen-id-*.json`), and the scene face's before-draw line is 963 KB. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-199.
  
  Compatibility: ops, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-199. Stored formats: additive. A checkpoint epoch gains the optional `removed` and `writers`, written by `store.checkpoint` and carried by every adapter as part of the epoch. A build before this reads such a checkpoint and ignores them, and this build reads one without them, failing closed as above, so no format number moves. A seat whose sight is limited and whose store was opened on an old checkpoint may now be served without a field naming a declared kind's id that no record ever held.
- f750ed3: A host reads any logo a document gives, and knows the logo for each scheme is shipped (FR-197). Graview Cloud, after 0.1.42: `brand.logo` took a third form, `{ light, dark?, alt?, darkFallback?: "invert" }`, but `capabilities().shipped` named nothing for it, so a host that refuses a document needing a seam its build does not ship could not refuse this one — and a rollback below 0.1.42 would leave an app with such a logo unable to open. And `logoOf` reads only the compiled brand, so a host that drew the logo from the document read the string, `{ src, alt }` and the object itself. Now the object logo is FR-197, and `logoOfDocument(logo)` on `@graview/core/document` reads any of the three forms into `{ light, dark?, alt?, darkFallback? }` (`DocumentLogo`), keeping only what the compile keeps: an empty mark is no logo, and a fallback other than `"invert"` is left out.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-197. Additive on `@graview/core/document` (`logoOfDocument` and the type `DocumentLogo`). What Graview Cloud does: `needsUnshipped` refuses a document whose `brand.logo` is an object with `light` unless `capabilities().shipped` has FR-197 ("This deployment's Graview cannot hold a logo for each scheme yet; give one logo."), and reads a document's logo with `logoOfDocument(document.brand?.logo)`.
- 10ea092: A view the host keeps is a place in the nav, and a kind kept off the places leaves the Pages drop-down. Roadmap q-custom-views-in-nav, filed from the hosted En Dash Social Media Playbook: its "To do" is a script view a chat drew and the host keeps by its manifest, and it showed only as an "Also as" link on the tasks list — `arrange-pages` refused it in `order` ('"to-do" is not a kind this app has') and in `first` ('"To do" is not a place, a kind or "home"'), since the declaration cannot know a host's views. The declaration now names one as `view:<its manifest name>` in `pages.order`, `pages.first` and `pages.primary`. Where the host hands its views' manifests to the edit (`EditOptions.views`, `HostView`: `name`, `title`, `attach`), `arrange-pages` takes the view's title or name and writes `view:<name>` (`order: ["To do"]` is `["view:to-do"]`), and refuses a name the host does not keep, naming the ones it does; unsaid, it takes `view:<name>` on the host's word and says "(once the host registers it)". `graview check` and the checked `compileDocument` (`CheckOptions.views`, `CompileOptions.views`) say a `view:` name no view registered here has is a note, `pages-view-not-drawn-here` — not drawn here yet, it stands where it is named once the host registers it — and, told the host's views, a name none of them is a warning, `pages-view-unknown`. On the page, the bar's places list a registered view `pages.order` names among the Lists at its place in the order (`arrangedEntries`), standing on a wide bar unless `pages.primary` says others; `pages.first` opens on it (`openingOf` reads `view:<name>` by the registration's name); the home's gallery puts it first. `placesOf(app, { views })` lists the host's titled pictures of a kind, each with `view: <name>`, where the order names them and `first` where `pages.first` does — what a host lists when an app is opened, and the places a drawn view's `data-place` is judged against.
  
  And a kind `pages.hide` names really leaves the Pages drop-down: it was dropped from the home only and folded into More, so the playbook's eleven reference lists made the drop-down "insanely long". It is listed only while it is the place you are on, so the bar still says where you are, and it still opens at its address, in Find and from wherever it is linked. `set-lens-hidden` on a kind's name or plural (`title: "source"`) writes `pages.hide` where no lens has that title, refused where `pages.primary` stands the kind; the place menu's Hide on a kind's list, for whoever may change the declaration, hands the host's `keptViews.lens` that edit, and the drop-down's Hidden lists each kept-off list with Show. `arrange-pages` and the preview of a change say "the places and the front page leave off …". `core`'s tests name a view by title and by `view:<name>`, with and without the host's views, refuse the rest in words, check the note and the warning, list and open the view, and hide and show a kind's list; `pages`' render the drop-down with the view first among the lists and without the hidden kinds, and the hidden list at its address; `embed`'s hide a list from the place menu and show one from Hidden. `pnpm verify address` mounts the tasks as Cloud's shell does with a script view registered as `to-do` and `pages: { order: ["view:to-do", "task"], first: "view:to-do", hide: ["person"] }`: the bare address opens on it, it stands first after Home, and the people are not listed and still open at `/people`. A hosted page carries 0.9 KB more up front (903 bytes, about 340 gzipped; the compiled page the same), the bar's arranged list and the hidden kinds' Show, under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-kept-view-is-a-place-in-the-nav-*.json`); the pages face alone 1.4 KB more (1_479, 572 gzipped), and the scene face's before-draw line is 964 KB.
  
  Compatibility: additive for the declaration — `pages.order`, `pages.first` and `pages.primary` may name `view:<name>`, which a build before 0.1.44 reads as a kind or place it does not have and passes over (`graview check` warned of it), and `arrange-pages` takes a title or `view:<name>` in `order` where it took only kinds. A behavior change for `pages.hide`: a kind it names is kept off the bar's places as well as the home. `set-lens-hidden` takes a kind where no lens has the title, where it refused. Additive in the API: `HostView`, `arrangedEntries`, `viewReference`, `ArrangedEntry`, `EditOptions.views`, `CompileOptions.views`, `CheckOptions` and `checkApp`'s second argument, `placesOf`'s `views`, `AppPlace.view`, `BarPlace.hidden`; `arrangementFindings` takes `{ views }` where it took a list of places. The wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 2f524d0: An archive is served as the seat may see it (FR-198). Roadmap f-archive-seat-view, from Graview Cloud: to answer `HistoryHost.archived(from, to)` for a seat, Cloud judged each slice with `logSeenBy` over a store rebuilt from the whole history, because a compacted store's own seat view does not know a record made and removed behind its horizon and would serve that record's id, minted from its label, to a seat that may not see it. It had no way to hand such a seat an epoch judged for it, so in an app with sights every seat, the owner's included, folded the past from seq 0, and on a plan with limited retention the chunks older than the cutoff were refused for them. Now `archivedSeenBy(store, archive, principal, { from, to })` on `@graview/core/seat` answers with both. `store` is the app as it stands, compacted or not; `archive` is what `store.compact` returned, every compaction's ops and epochs together, reaching back to seq 0. It answers `{ ops, epochs }` for seqs `from` up to but not including `to`. The ops are every op of the range in its place, redacted exactly as `logSeenBy` redacts the log of the store never compacted. The epochs are those in the range, each graph holding only the records the seat is served, as its sight shows them: its kinds, `own`, `via` and the fields a sight hides, a field that names a record it may not see cleared or the record withheld, the links between those records, and who made each one, named only for them. The answer is a valid `HistoryHost.archived` answer, and each epoch is the graph the seat's ops fold to at its seq, so playback can fold from the nearest epoch and draws only what the seat may see. An archive with a gap behind the horizon is refused, since what the gap held could not be judged. `core`'s test ports the 1,000-world property to compacted histories: records made and removed behind the horizon, links to hidden records, required fields naming them. No unseen id appears in any range's ops or epochs, the ops equal what the never-compacted store serves, and each epoch equals the fold of those ops at its seq. A second test lets a seat that may not see one kind scrub a compacted app to seq 1 from the seed's epoch it is served, and `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-198. Nothing is added to the main entry or to what a hosted page loads up front.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-198. Additive on `@graview/core/seat` (`archivedSeenBy`, and its `ArchivedSeen` answer type).

## 0.1.43

### Patch Changes

- 3c6b0fa: An op carries its value once less: op format 2 keeps no inverse. Roadmap f-ops-without-inverse-on-the-wire: an op wrote what it changed in the call's arguments, in its primitives' `before` and `after`, and again in an `inverse` that was nothing but those primitives turned round — so a 19 KB picture changed in a text field was a 95 KB op, and most of why a heavy hosted app's log ran to 4.9 MB. The inverse is now worked out from the primitives where it is read, `inverseOf(op)`, remembered per op, and read by undo, a selective undo, a rebase's rollback, playback, verifying a fold and the seat's view of a withheld op alike; every primitive's inverse is another primitive, so nothing it needs was missing. `FORMATS.op` is 2. On the Beatles app with a 19 KB cover on each of its 18 releases, each cover changed once and 200 plain edits (`node scripts/op-bytes.mjs`), a cover's change is 57.4 KB where it was 95.5 KB, an added cover 38.4 KB where it was 57.5 KB, an undo of a change 38.4 KB where it was 76.5 KB, and the whole log 1.96 MB where it was 3.17 MB (38% less); with 479 covers, 72.6 MB comes to 45.5 MB. What is left twice is the value in the call and in the patch, which a rebase plays again and a sync sends on, and the value it replaced, which undo puts back.
  
  A format-1 log opens on this build as it did, and nothing on disk is rewritten to open it: a store drops an inverse that is its primitives' own from every op it opens on, receives or rebases onto (`bareOp`, the `upgradeOp` step from 1), keeps one that is not (a host could once hand `append` its own), and its file adapter's compaction writes what it keeps in format 2. An export is stamped `op: 2` and its ops carry no inverse; a bundle from before imports and undoes the same. The live wire carries format 2 as an addition to protocol 1: `openRemote` says it reads it — `opFormat` in its hello, `?opFormat=2` on `/graview/state` and `/graview/since`, `opFormat` in the body it posts — and is sent ops bare; a client that says nothing, as every one before 0.1.43, is sent each op with its inverse written out (`opsFor`), so an open tab on the last build goes on undoing and playing back against this server. A host that routes its own requests passes `WireAsked.opFormat` (`opFormatOf(url)` reads the query). `core`'s tests fold, undo one batch at a time, take back an early batch with what depends on it and redo it, play back at every seq, verify a fold and find where it parts, rebase and receive with the same log in both formats and get the same graph each time, and open 0.1.0's format-1 fixture and go on; `ship`'s open a 0.1.42 store on disk, undo, append and reopen it, and serve both readers on every route and the socket. A hosted page carries 0.5 KB more up front (515 bytes, about 450 gzipped; the compiled page the same), the derived inverse, the format-1 inverse dropped and the format said on the wire, under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/ops-carry-their-value-once-*.json`), and the scene face's before-draw line is 962 KB.
  
  Compatibility: breaking for ops and stored formats, before 1.0 — `Operation.inverse` is optional and this build writes none (read it with `inverseOf`), and `AppendOp.inverse` is gone; op format 2, so a build before 0.1.43 opening a store this one has opened, or importing a bundle it exported, refuses it with `NewerFormatError` ("Upgrade the framework to read it") rather than undo an op it finds no inverse on, and a host rolling a store back writes its log out with `withInverse` under `op: 1`. Additive on the wire: `hello.opFormat`, `?opFormat=` and a post's `opFormat` are fields a server before 0.1.43 ignores, sending format 1, which this build reads; a client that says none is sent format 1 as before. `WIRE_PROTOCOL` and the subprotocol do not move, and the declaration and derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.42

### Patch Changes

- a5bbda0: A brand's logo has one for each scheme. Nick, building the TrueNorth recon app: the brand took an emoji or one mark, a client's real logo (a JPG on white) had nowhere else to go, and on a dark theme it sat as a white slab — the app drew it under `filter: invert(1) hue-rotate(180deg)` and `mix-blend-mode: screen` of its own. `brand.logo` is still a string, or now `{ light, dark? }`: the light one in the light scheme, the dark one in the dark, the light one there too where there is none. Whether a light logo is opaque on white cannot be known from it, so inverting it is asked for rather than guessed: `{ light, darkFallback: "invert" }` draws it inverted and screened onto the ground in the dark scheme alone, and beside a `dark` one it is never used, which `graview check` says (`brand-logo`, a warning at `brand.logo.darkFallback`). In a document the same object takes `alt` (`{ "src", "alt" }` and a string still mean one logo), each mark is judged as the one was (`brand-mark` at `brand.logo.light` or `brand.logo.dark`), and `set-brand` takes `logo: { light, dark }` and says "The logo gets one of its own for the dark scheme." (or "The logo is drawn inverted in the dark scheme.", "The logo is the same in both schemes."), as the preview of a change does. A declared app's logo is checked too now: a pair with no light one, a fallback other than `"invert"` (`brand-logo`), and a mark that could act (`brand-mark`). Contrast checks are on the colors, as before.
  
  The default shell's bar and a design's own draw it with `AppMark` (`@graview/primitives`, handed the brand on `context.brand`): both marks are in the page, each `data-graview-scheme-only="light"` or `"dark"`, and the theme's sheet for the scheme the page is in hides the other's — so the logo switches with the scheme however the page chose it, with no hook — and a light one the brand asked to invert carries `data-graview-logo-fallback="invert"`, which only the dark sheet styles. `logoOf(brand)` and `logoFor(brand, scheme)` (`{ src, invert }`) on `@graview/core` are for a design that draws its own image. The static page's masthead does the same; a worker view is handed the logo its scheme draws. `graview-logo`, a skill of its own, says all of it: `graview-brand` gives its marks to it, split along that seam rather than shaved. A hosted page carries 1.5 KB more up front for it (1_541 bytes, 509 gzipped; the compiled page 1.4 KB, 1_461 / 482), under a raise of each budget by that; the scene face before it draws 1_541 bytes more, the pages face alone 982 / 337, the embed without the studio 987 / 318, every face 4_241 / 1_439, the embed with the studio 4_161 / 1_421, and the guest host 80 bytes before a worker is drawn and on the open kit, each under a raise of its budget by what it measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged. The declaration: `brand.logo` gains a third form, `{ light, dark?, alt?, darkFallback?: "invert" }`, and every document that compiled still compiles the same; the TypeScript `Brand.logo` is `string | BrandLogo`, so code that read it as a string reads `logoOf(brand)?.light`. Check finding codes: new `brand-logo` (an error for a declared app's pair with no light one or a fallback it does not know, a warning for a fallback beside a dark one), and `brand-mark` now also at `brand.logo.light` and `brand.logo.dark`, and for a declared app's logo. New on `@graview/core`: `logoOf`, `logoFor` and the type `BrandLogo`. The `set-brand` edit takes `logo: { light, dark?, alt?, darkFallback? }`. What Graview Cloud does: `add_image` each file as now, then `{"op": "set-brand", "logo": {"light": "/graview/assets/<sha>.jpg", "dark": "/graview/assets/<sha>.png"}}` (or `"darkFallback": "invert"` with a light one alone, when the person says the logo is a picture on white); a page that draws its own masthead uses `AppMark` or `logoFor`, and a stylesheet it writes itself for a scheme hides `[data-graview-scheme-only]` of the other.
- caf67fd: A page opens on the snapshot and a short tail of the log. Measured on Graview Cloud on 2026-10-20, on a phone, the Beatles demo's `/graview/state` was 6.0 MB, 4.9 MB of it the op log (1,267 ops): every op ever made rode into every open, though the page draws the snapshot and reaches the log only for its recent history and its undo. `liveProtocol({ openTail })`, and `createStoreHandler`'s, `serveStore`'s and `servePreview`'s `openTail`, cut what an open carries, `GET /graview/state` and the welcome of a socket that has nothing, to at most that many of the latest ops, said with the `horizon` they begin at, while the host keeps its whole log for its own undo, export and history. The page's store opens as it does on a store compacted behind an undo horizon: it draws the snapshot, undoes back to the horizon and says so past it, and `/graview/since` and a socket's catching up are unchanged. A field written before the tail is no false conflict: `FieldRevisions.of(ops, { horizon })` puts a field no op of a tail wrote at the seq before the horizon, which is what the page claims to have seen, and a claim is stale now only when the field was written after the revision it names (it was any revision but the field's own), so a server holding the whole log takes the claim as seen, and a server reading its own compacted log holds no claim of a field behind its horizon stale. `ship`'s test opens a page on three ops of twelve, renames a record made long before them with no conflict, undoes its own rename and is refused one behind the horizon, and is still refused a rename over somebody else's since. A hosted page carries 0.2 KB more up front for it, handed a compiled app or not (`scripts/budget-raises/a-page-opens-on-a-short-tail-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and the declaration are unchanged. On the wire, a host that sets `openTail` serves a state whose `log` begins at its `horizon`, a shape the wire has carried since compaction came (FR-23 in the changelog); with none, every answer is as before. Additive on `liveProtocol`, `createStoreHandler`, `serveStore` and `servePreview` (`openTail`) and on `FieldRevisions.of` (`{ horizon }`). `FieldRevisions.stale` now calls a claim stale only when the field's revision is later than the claim, where before any other revision was.
- f6b5bb7: Importing the framework builds nothing it does not use (Roadmap f-smaller-worker). Graview Cloud's Worker paid about 50 ms of CPU on every cold start before it answered, and most of what the framework added to that was zod: the document format built some hundred and fifty schemas the moment `@graview/core/document` loaded, the edit ops another sixty, a template's and a connection's shapes more, and nineteen framework modules imported zod's `z` by name, which makes esbuild (and so wrangler) keep every part of zod and its forty-odd locales, 805 KB, whatever was called. The document's shapes (`DocumentSpec`, `KindSpec`, `FieldSpec` and the rest), `TemplateSpec` and `ConnectionSpec` are now `z.lazy` over a set built the first time one parses, the edit ops' shapes are built with the first edit, and the framework imports zod as `import * as z from "zod"`, which a bundler shakes to what is called. Bundled as wrangler bundles it, what Cloud's worker imports from the framework comes to 1.8 MB instead of 2.4 MB, 219 KB of it zod's instead of 805 KB, and loads in 29.5 ms of CPU instead of 52.3 ms; `@graview/core/document` alone in 10.8 ms instead of 34.3 ms, `@graview/core/check` in 13.9 instead of 37.9. `node scripts/import-cost.mjs` measures each entry Cloud imports in a fresh process (`--bundled` as a Worker receives it, `--worker` the names it imports together, `--profile` where the time goes), and `tests/importing-the-framework-costs-little.test.ts` holds each to a budget of CPU, the names Cloud imports to 2 MB and 260 KB of zod, and every framework module to the namespace import. A hosted page spends 1.2 KB more up front on the lazy shapes (0.7 KB handed a compiled app), raised by what they measured (1_197 / 356 and 691 / 143); the scene and the pages faces before they draw are raised to 960 KB.
  
  Merged onto the scheme's logo in the same release, the two together carry 0.0 KB more up front than each alone, on a hosted page and on one handed a compiled app.
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tools are unchanged; `document.schema.json` is byte for byte the same. The document's exported shapes keep their names, their parse and their issues, but each is now a zod lazy schema over the shape it was (`DocumentSpec._zod.innerType` is the object schema), so code that read an object schema's own members off one (`.shape`, `_zod.def.shape`) reads them off `_zod.innerType` instead. `@graview/core` still re-exports `z` for a product.
- 93fccf0: Playback reads the changes a compaction archived (FR-196). Roadmap f-playback-reads-archive, from Graview Cloud: its rooms compact behind the undo horizon, the ops before it archived to R2, and an open carries the checkpoint and a short tail; after a compaction `pastOf` refuses any seq behind the horizon and `HistoryHost` had no way to hand the archive back, so the timeline stopped where the kept tail starts. Now `HistoryHost.archived(from, to)` answers the ops of a range behind the horizon, oldest first and each with its `seq`, and the epochs among them (`ArchivedOps`: `{ ops, epochs? }`, which a `LogArchive` from `store.compact` already is; or the ops alone). It is asked only when the timeline is moved to a moment behind the horizon, in chunks aligned to 256 seqs, newest first until an epoch — a checkpoint an earlier compaction made, a seed's — or seq 0 is reached, and each chunk once while it is the same function; scrubbing through what came back asks nothing more. `pastAt` folds the moment read-only from the nearest epoch (or an empty seq 0, or a point it kept on the way), under the declaration the host says it was made under, and `askArchived` on `@graview/core/past` says whether it can be drawn yet. With `archived` the timeline's scrubber reaches back to the start, a step per seq behind the horizon, captioned with the change that ended each; without it the scrubber begins at the horizon and says "The changes before the timeline's start were archived." While a moment is read the face holds the picture it had, and the playback's `archive` says which moment it is reading; a refusal, a failure or a gap in the answer is said on the timeline ("Could not read the older changes from the archive: …") and the present is untouched. Undo still stops at the horizon: no "Restore to here" is offered behind it. `core`'s test compacts a store twice, hands the host the archives and scrubs to seq 1, every moment behind the horizon drawn as the uncompacted store's; the playback harness does the same in a browser, and `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-196. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (64 bytes, 40 gzipped; the compiled page the same), the moment held in the provider's playback, under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/playback-reads-the-archive-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-196. Additive on the mount options' and the provider's `history` (`HistoryHost.archived`, and the `ArchivedOps` type on `@graview/core/history`), on `@graview/core/past` (`askArchived`, and `pastAt` reading behind the horizon when the host keeps the archive), and on `Playback` (`archive`, `null` unless a moment behind the horizon is being read or was refused), which a host that builds a `Playback` of its own now sets.

## 0.1.41

## 0.1.40

### Patch Changes

- b5c1332: A drop reads when the host says each file was made (FR-195). Roadmap f-drop-reads-upload-at, from Graview Cloud: its `POST /graview/files` answers a photograph with `at`, the instant it read from the file's EXIF, and a view's drop (FR-193) never read it. A required time a drop fills — a moment's `takenAt`, a detection's day — came from the file's `lastModified`, which a copy off the camera, a download or a sync sets to the moment it happened, so three photographs taken on Saturday and dropped on Monday were three moments on Monday. Now `FileValue` takes `at`, an ISO date and time with a `Z` or an offset (`fileAt` from `@graview/core` reads one; `isFileValue` refuses anything else there, and a drop leaves off an `at` it cannot read, EXIF's own `2026:10:17 09:12:00` say, rather than refuse the file), and a drop fills a required time from the stored value's `at` first, then the file's `lastModified`, then the drop's own time in the record a drop makes by default; an instant with an offset fills an argument that takes one as it was said, and one that takes only UTC or a day as that. `event.files` in a view's `drop` carries the stored `at` where the host said one. A record keeps the `at` its host answered, as it keeps a picture's size. The guest-sandbox drop harness proves the three photographs land at Saturday's times. A hosted page's first load carries 0.2 KB more (0.1 KB gzipped), the file value's `at` read where a record's file is judged; the embed's faces and the guest host carry the same. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-195.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored formats are unchanged; a file value that kept no `at` reads as it did, and one with an `at` that is not a date and time is refused where it was ignored before. The declaration is unchanged. The wire: a `dom-event` `drop`'s `files[].at` is the host's stored `at` where it answered one, else the file's last change as before; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-195. Derived tools: a file argument's schema gains an optional `at`. What Graview Cloud does: keep answering `at` from the files route's upload, as an ISO instant with a `Z` or an offset; nothing else.
- b98303b: A face names what its first draw opens, and a hosted page on a phone draws Pages with no chunk asked for after the face's own (FR-194). Graview Cloud names in its page's head every chunk its shell's startup doors open, but a face opens doors of its own as it draws — on the pages the home's lenses, a list's arranging, the bar's Activity and a form's date box; on the scene the relations' ribbons, the city's ground, the districts' states and the way back — each found only once the face had run, a round trip after it on a phone. And what is fetched before it is wanted went out while the place was still drawing: the acts' pane, readied by the keyboard the page put on a title and drawn on every routed page for a selection it draws nothing for there, a date's editor, the other face at a desk. `pnpm verify first-load` counted 41 to 45 scripts asked for after the face on the preview's home and list before the place said `data-graview-drawn`.
  
  They stay doors — a page that never draws a lens never runs one — and `@graview/embed/first-draw`, a new entry for a host's build, names them: `firstDrawChunks(metafile, face)` reads esbuild's metafile and returns the chunks a face's first draw asks for, each door's chunk and every chunk it imports outright, and `FIRST_DRAW_DOORS` is the list it reads, each door `{ package, module }`. `graview preview`'s page asks for them from its head once the face's own chunks have arrived (named beside the face, they took a phone's bandwidth from it and the face drew 0.65 s later). What is fetched early now waits until the place has first drawn and settled (`afterFirstDraw`, `firstDrawn`, `placeWatched` and `placeDrawn` on `@graview/react` and its `/provider`, said by the embed's drawn signal; at once where nothing watches a place), the keyboard put on the page as it draws readies no menu, and the routed face no longer draws the acts' pane for a selection.
  
  Measured by `pnpm verify first-load`, three cold runs on a phone (four times slower CPU, slow 4G), before and after: the roadmap draws and settles in 4.50 s, from 4.96 s, and is interactive in 4.85 s, from 5.30 s; the tasks settle in 4.63 s, from 5.12 s; the first useful paint is unchanged (3.55 s and 3.49 s). Before the home has drawn the roadmap asks for 75 requests and 478 KB, from 78 and 495 KB, and nothing after the face, on both profiles and both apps (`…DrawsPagesWithNoChunkAskedForAfterTheFace`, a new claim of the harness, which also says the time to `data-graview-drawn`). A hosted page carries 0.3 KB more up front for it (307 bytes, 132 gzipped; the compiled page 307 and 143), under a raise of each budget by that, and the scene face 413 bytes more before it draws. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-194.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-194. New: the `@graview/embed/first-draw` entry (`firstDrawChunks`, `FIRST_DRAW_DOORS`, and the types `FirstDrawFace`, `FirstDrawDoor`, `FirstDrawMetafile`), and `afterFirstDraw`, `firstDrawn`, `placeWatched`, `placeDrawn` on `@graview/react` and `@graview/react/provider`. The acts' pane, a date's editor and the other face are fetched once an embed's place has first drawn rather than at the first idle moment. What Graview Cloud does: at build, `firstDrawChunks(metafile, face)` for the face the shell opens on (`"pages"` on a phone, `"graview"` at a desk), less the chunks the head already names, and asks for them once the face's own `modulepreload` links have loaded, as `graview preview`'s head does.
- bdb45b6: A focused record's words get ground of their own. In a product with many records, a position focused in the scene drew "QUESTIONS THAT WOULD SETT…" over the cards of the relation beside it, hung "questions that would settle it: 1 question" and "is a call about: Additive file o…" in open ground, clipped at the edge, ran lines under the cards and the headings, and from altitude stacked "+138" on "+10". Below a crowd the relation band was one grid in id order, so a relation began mid-row and its caption hung in a gutter no deeper than the gap between two cards. Now every band of more than one relation is laid out by relation, crowded or not, with a caption's gutter between its rows, and a relation takes as many slots of the row it starts as its caption's words need, its cards centered in them (`packBand`). A row's captions stand over its tallest card, so "who presents it" spans both speakers rather than the higher one. `bandCaptions` places the captions on the layout's own boxes, as the page does on what it measures (`captionRuns`, moved from `@graview/react` to `@graview/layout`), and a unit test holds that none stands on a card or another caption, at 1440, 1200 and 390. On the ground a tie says its words only for the focused record's own relations that no caption names, and into a stand-in it says how many the relation reaches, not the fourteen ties kept ("their songs: 4 songs" stood beside a door to 790); a line from the record to a door of its band is drawn once, not again by a tie from inside the card's edge; and a road along a gutter passes over a heading, not between the heading and its cards. From altitude a district says what it has no room for once (`districtBlocks` answers one `LaneRest`, with the `rows` it counts). `pnpm verify lines` holds it with two new claims, `aFocusedRecordsWordsStandOnGroundOfTheirOwn` (a gauntlet talk and speaker, a discography song, and an artist of eleven hundred chosen and holding the keyboard, on a desk and a phone) and `aDistrictSaysWhatItHasNoRoomForOnce`. Every face's budget and the studio embed's are raised by the 1.6 KB and 1.5 KB they measured, and a hosted page's scene face by 1.4 KB before it draws; nothing up front changed.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged.
- 686b2e4: A project `graview create --link` makes takes its dev server's aliases from the framework checkout's own exports, as the framework's apps now do. Its `vite.config.ts` used to list one alias per entry point by hand, pinned by a test to the exports of the day, so a project created today broke on the next subpath the framework added: the bare name's alias swallowed the subpath and the page answered 500, which is how seven product apps linked by path broke. It now says `alias: sourceAliases(framework)`, from `@graview/ship/dev`, where `framework` is the linked checkout; the list is read from every package's `exports` each time the server starts. Everything else the linked config does stays: one React, the studio's door and `devFiles`, and the checkout allowed to the dev server. Core still imports nothing of ship; the scaffold only writes the call. `smoke:create` holds it with a new claim, `aLinkedProjectsDevServerResolvesEveryExportFromSource`, which resolves every export of every package a product depends on through the linked project's own Vite. A project linked before this keeps its hand list until it replaces it with `sourceAliases(<its checkout>)`.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. Only the `vite.config.ts` a linked project is created with changes; a project made against the published packages is written as before.
- 2ff7c28: A declaration with a tie to any kind (`to: "*"`) draws its pages again. `supportingKinds`, which works out what folds into the bar's "More", read every tie's `to` as a list, and `"*".filter` threw: the pages face of homeflow, squad and proposal stopped on "(edge.to ?? []).filter is not a function" and drew nothing. Now a tie to `"*"` reaches every kind, so a note about anything is a supporting kind, as a note about two named kinds already was.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. Changed: `supportingKinds` reads a tie to `"*"` as a tie to every kind instead of throwing. What Graview Cloud does: nothing.
- 56b88e1: A headline bound to a long field is warned of, and drawn a step smaller. `{"headline": "{call}"}`, where a call is three sentences, filled a focus card at display size: a headline is a few words, drawn large, and nothing said a long text did not belong there. Now `graview check` warns, `view-headline-long`, where a view's headline block binds a long field whole — a document's `text` field, Markdown among them, or a string declared in code longer than 500 characters, as `view-whole-text` reads them — "incident page heads it with call, a long text, drawn at a headline's size; a headline is a few words.", and the fix says it in a text block, `{"text": "{call}"}`, or heads it with `{call|excerpt: 60}`; an excerpt and a short field say nothing. And a headline past 90 characters says so (`data-long` on `.graview-spec-headline`) and is drawn at a heading's size, 1.125rem whatever its level, clamped to four lines, every word still in the page for a reader that does not see it. The `graview-view-specs` skill says so. A hosted page that compiles its document carries 0.4 KB more up front (0.1 KB gzipped), the check's own words, and its scene face before it draws 0.6 KB more; one handed a compiled app carries nothing more.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; every declaration that compiled still compiles. The check finding codes gain `view-headline-long`, a warning. `SPEC_VIEW_CSS` gains `.graview-spec-headline[data-long][data-level]`. What Graview Cloud does: nothing; a view a chat wrote with a long field as its headline is now drawn smaller, and the warning is there for its own check to show.
- 4aa429f: `graview check` warns when a `describe` written in code returns a template (carried from the twelfth walk, a film society). One sentence of the walk's app said `{startsAt | datetime}` to a person, braces and all: a template is filled in only in a document's strings, and a kind's `describe` or `label`, or an act's `describe`, written in code is shown as it returns. The watch caught it on the page and the checker said nothing. Now it warns, `describe-template-in-code` at `defineNode("<kind>").describe` (or `.label`, or `defineMutation("<act>").describe`): the text of the function's strings is read for `{name}` naming a field, relation or computed value of the kind or an argument of the act, and for any `{name | filter}`, and the fix says the value in code — `momentAsRead(node.startsAt)`, `dayAsRead(…)`, or `${…}` in a template literal. The `graview-view-specs` skill says so. The studio carries the checker, so the embed with the studio handed in measures 1.9 KB more (0.2 KB gzipped) and every face 2.1 KB, and their budgets are raised by that; nothing of it is in a hosted page.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; every declaration that compiled still compiles. The check finding codes gain `describe-template-in-code`, a warning about a describe or label in code whose words show braces to a person; a document's strings are not asked.
- 752da2b: `spatialHref(id, sceneHref = "/")` takes the scene's address, as `placeHref` already did. It answered `/#focus=<id>` whatever the host, so a host whose scene lives at `/scene/places/overview` could not link a page of its own to a record there. Now `spatialHref("plot:north-bed", "/scene/places/overview")` is `/scene/places/overview#focus=plot%3Anorth-bed`, and unsaid it is `/` as before. A project `graview create` writes passes its PagesApp context's `sceneHref` to it. The `@graview/pages` README and the `graview-pages` skill say to set `sceneHref` in the PagesApp context when the scene is not at `/`, and pass it on.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `spatialHref(id)` answers what it did.
- 68ebfd2: The scene's strip tells a watching harness when an act's own input refuses a press there, as the pages do (carried from W-228 and W-238). The strip and the quick ask show what an act would do before they apply it, so an act offered whole whose argument its input refuses was refused in the preview — said in the strip — and the apply that tells the watch never ran: the same offer was a finding on the pages and passed every harness on the scene. `previewAffordance` takes `{ pressed: { author } }` for the preview of a press about to be applied, and then tells the watch of an input refusal (`tellTheWatchOfAPressRefusedByItsInput`, on `@graview/core`); `useApplyAffordance`'s preview and the quick ask pass it. A preview of what an act would do before anything is pressed still tells nothing, and a form a person filled wrongly is still theirs, not the surface's (the watch reads the press's origin). `pnpm verify watch` holds it with a new claim, `aPressTheStripsPreviewRefusesIsCaught`, over the workspace's own store and strip press. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and their budgets are raised by that; the pages face, every face and the studio handed in by what they measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tool names and schemas are unchanged. New, additive: `previewAffordance`'s fourth argument `{ pressed }`, and `tellTheWatchOfAPressRefusedByItsInput` on `@graview/core`.

## 0.1.39

### Patch Changes

- 77a235a: `graview check` says which acts write a status board's column without saying to what (the thirteenth walk, a camera-trap survey). The survey's review queue, a status board of detections with `move: true`, offered "Move to ›" on every card — on a phone the only way to move one — and withheld every column with "Nothing in this app moves a detection to Confirmed.", though "Confirm", "Nothing there" and "Same animal again" each set the status. They were written in code as the scaffold wrote its own close act, with `writes: ["status"]`, which says an act writes the field and not the value it writes, so no column was reached; and the checker said nothing, since a board nothing moves on at all was taken for a picture of where things stand. Now it warns, `lens-column-written-unsaid` at `lenses.<i>.bindings.<kind>.column`: "\"Confirm\", \"Nothing there\" and \"Same animal again\" write status on a detection without saying to what, so \"Review queue\" moves no card into Confirmed, False trigger, Duplicate.", with the fix `sets: { status: "<the value it writes>" }` beside `writes`, which makes each the move to its column. The close act `graview create` writes says `sets: { status: "closed" }`.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; every declaration that compiled still compiles. The check finding codes gain `lens-column-written-unsaid`, a warning about acts in code a status board names by the field they write; a column such an act would have reached was already unreached, and said so.
- f3af490: A project `graview create` writes takes a picture on a record in development (the thirteenth walk, a camera-trap survey). The graview-node-kind skill's first example declares `imageField()`, and a project that followed it drew the field's editor as "This host doesn't take files, so none can be added here.": the scaffold handed neither face any `files`, and its dev server kept none. Now its `vite.config.ts` adds `devFiles()` beside `studioDoor()`, and `main.tsx` hands the scene and the pages `filesAt("/__graview/files", …)` while the dev server runs (pictures, videos, PDFs and plain text, up to 10 MB each, kept in memory); a built page still hands none, and the comment says to hand the host's own when it is deployed.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; only what `graview create` writes for a new project changes.
- 1d365af: A page opens on a view proposed and not kept, so a proposal can be seen before it is kept (FR-187). `propose_view` tries a view headless and answers in words; an agent that wanted to see it at 390 px in dark had to keep it first, because the hosted page drew only the app's views. Now `mount` takes `draftView` (`EmbedDraftView`: a worker view's `manifest` and its `source`, or `worker`, with what else a `WorkerViewDefinition` takes) and draws it for the seat at the keyboard where its manifest attaches — the home, a kind's list at the place its title names, each record's page — under one line, "Draft — <title> · Not kept", in a group named "Draft: <title>" (`[data-graview-draft-view]`), as the seat's drawn panel is marked on `/~draft`. A kept view of the same name is the one it stands in for: whatever the host's `views` registers under that name is left out, so it is drawn nowhere beside the draft. Nothing of it is kept: it goes by no name the place menu or `keptViews` could keep it under, the store is not written, and the next page, handed no draft, has none. A draft that is stopped says why in its place instead of standing the kind's own face there, and playback draws it as proposed. With FR-188's `data-graview-drawn`, a host pictures it once it has drawn. `registerWorkerView` takes `draft: true` on the definition for a host that registers one itself. `graview preview` and `graview shots` take one as `--view <view.js> --manifest <manifest.json>`, handed to the page with the document (`servePreview`'s `draftView`). The worker's host is fetched before the embed draws, and only when a draft is handed: a hosted page carries 0.7 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and their budgets are raised by that; the pages face, the embed, the studio handed in and the guest host registering a view by what they measured, and every face, counted with every chunk, by the worker's host and the open kit it can now fetch. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-187.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-187. New: `draftView` on `mount` and `<Embed>`, `EmbedDraftView` on `@graview/embed`, `WorkerViewDefinition.draft` on `@graview/guest/host/views`, `PreviewOptions.draftView` on `@graview/ship`; `/graview/document` from `graview preview` carries `draftView` when one was given. `@graview/embed` depends on `@graview/guest`. What Graview Cloud does: serve a proposed view once — a one-time draft id on the picture's address, say, that the shell trades for the manifest and source — and pass it to `mount` as `draftView`, then picture the address where it attaches once `[data-graview-drawn]` is on the root.
- 6bff55b: A `pictures: true` view draws a full-size record photo, and a headless run says what the page will leave out (FR-192). Roadmap f-view-picture-src-large: Maryland Family Weekend's journey view drew its moments while they held 9 KB webps; once they held the phone's own JPEGs (187 KB to 1.5 MB), the page fetched every one, handed the view a `data:` image of it, and then refused to draw that very image — a picture is handed at up to 1,500,000 bytes, a view's image was cut off at 512,000 characters of `data:` URL — so the view fell back to the declared home with "The image "data:image/jpeg;base64,…" was not drawn". The page now draws whatever `src` its host handed a view for a record's picture, at any size, by the host's own address for it (`JudgeContext.picture`, which the worker view's drawing asks `GuestPictureHand.drawn`), and only as handed: a `src` the view changed is judged as any image it makes. On a page whose policy refuses `blob:` images, as Graview Cloud's does, the view is handed a short `blob:` address all the same and the page draws the `data:` image behind it, so the view never carries a megabyte of base64 and never has it refused. A JPEG with an EXIF APP1 segment after its JFIF one is drawn as any other. The cap stays on a `data:` image the view makes itself, and its refusal says so: "It is a data: image of 1,234,567 characters, more than the 512,000 a view's own data: image may be. A picture on a record is drawn at any size by the src the host hands the view (pictures in its manifest), used as it was handed." A headless run (`runWorkerViewHeadless`, behind `describe_place` and `propose_view`) hands a picture the page would leave out before fetching it — heavier than the host fetches, or past the view's budget as the page reckons it — no `src` and the page's own sentence in `left`, and says it among its problems at `pictures`; `HeadlessLimits` takes `pictureBytes` and `picturesBytes` as the page does. The guest-sandbox pictures harness draws a 1,200 × 900 JPEG with EXIF on a page that refuses `blob:` images, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox. The graview-view-drawing skill says the cap and that a record's picture is drawn as handed. Drawing a worker view on the open kit carries 1.1 KB more (1,075 bytes, 454 gzipped), under a raise of its own. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-192.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-192. What Graview Cloud does: nothing for the live page — a view whose manifest says `pictures` draws its records' photos at any size on the next version; a headless run's `problems` may now carry `{ at: "pictures", says }` for a picture the page would leave out.
- 99ed9e7: A record a lens draws as a link answers the right-click menu on a page mounted under a base, and a new tab keeps the base (FR-189). On a page mounted with `basePath` (`/a/<app>`), a board's card and a blocks lens's card or row were `<a href="/features/live-documents">`, the base dropped, so the menu read them as a link somewhere else: "Open in a new tab" and nothing more, no Open, no acts, no "Move to ›" — and with the Move button gone in 0.1.38, a phone could not move a card at all — while the new tab opened outside the app. Now every record a list draws as a link (a board's card, a blocks lens's cards and rows, grouped or not, a home's lists) links to its address under the router's base (`/a/<app>/features/live-documents`), the menu reads the record an item is listed as (`data-graview-listed`) wherever in it the press lands, its own stretched link included — a link the card draws to somewhere else stays that link — and "Open in a new tab" opens the record's address under the base. On a status board a finger resting on a card opens its menu, with Move to ›, rather than choosing it; on a phone that menu offers "Choose several", which chooses the card and begins choosing as the long press did, and while several are chosen a tap and a long press choose as before (a card is `data-graview-choose` only then). `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-189. The pages face alone grows 218 / 81 bytes (minified / gzipped), every face 679 / 250 and the embed with the studio handed in 218 / 97, each raised in `scripts/budget-raises/`; a hosted page's first load holds to its budget unraised.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-189. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — a page mounted with `basePath` draws its cards' links under the base and its menu answers them; a host that read a card's `href` as basename-relative now reads it with the base on.
- c9c13d2: A view saves a field as the person types, and hears that it saved (FR-191). A view's code is never handed what a person types where sight is not total, so the Farm Bureau workshop's call sheet could take notes during a call only through a Save button the person had to remember. A field now carries its act and says when it saves: `<textarea name="notes" data-prefill="notes" data-act="save-notes" data-save="input" data-debounce="1500">` inside a `fieldset data-record` is saved by the host once the person pauses for `data-debounce` milliseconds (1 000 when it is left out, held to 250–10 000), at once when they leave the field, and as the view goes, whatever was still waiting; `data-save="change"` saves on the field's `change` alone. A save is a press in every way but the click: the act is the field's own `data-act`, its record and its fieldset's fields are read as a press reads them and held to the same rules (only what the person typed, a prefilled value only back where it came from, under their grants), and a click into the field is not a press. The view hears what became of every act a press or a save asked for as an `acted` event on the bound element — `graview.on("acted", "textarea", (event) => …)`, `event.pressed` holding `as`, `ok`, and `reason` and `message` when it was refused — so it can say "Saving…" on `input` and "Saved" on `acted`. A headless run (`graview view check`, `runWorkerViewHeadless`) says when a field's `data-save` would save nothing: no `data-act` or `name`, no fieldset bound to a record, a `data-save` other than `input` or `change`, or a `data-debounce` it does not read. The graview-worker-view skill teaches it, with `examples/a-call-sheet.js`. The guest host's open-kit drawing carries 1.4 KB more (0.5 KB gzipped), fetched with the press reader when a view first draws; a hosted page's first load is unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-191.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire: a worker view's runtime is sent a `dom-event` whose `event` is `acted`, carrying `pressed`, after every act a press or a field's save asked for; a view that does not listen for it hears nothing new, and `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-191. What Graview Cloud does: teach `data-save`, `data-debounce` and `acted` in its views guide in place of the Save button a call sheet needed; nothing changes in how it mounts a view.
- 817aaba: `add-lens` refuses an option the lens does not take (FR-190). Roadmap f-lens-option-refused: 0.1.38's notes said `add-lens` refuses an unknown option, but it applied an option a board does not take (Graview Cloud sent color in its British spelling), and `move: true` on a calendar, with only the checker's warning, and drew the lens without it, so a misspelled key applied and did nothing. Now `add-lens` refuses such a key at `edits.<i>.options.<key>` with the code `lens-option-unknown` and the checker's own words and fix — "The columns lens takes no option "shade", so "Work" ignores it." / "Use one of: sort, search, filters, move, collapsed, cap, hidden." — as it already refuses a column or a field the board does not have. A lens declared some other way, by a JSON patch (`applyPatch`) or a document sent whole, is judged by `lensOptionsRefused(stored, proposed)` on `@graview/core/document`: an error at `lenses.<i>.options.<key>`, in the same words, for each key a titled shipped lens brings that it does not take and the same lens (its lens, title and kind) did not already carry. A document already carrying such a key still compiles, with the `lens-option-unknown` warning. The checker, `add-lens` and `lensOptionsRefused` say it in one sentence and fix, written once. The graview-lens skill says `add-lens` refuses it. The embed with the studio, and every face, carry 251 bytes more (103 and 116 gzipped), under raises of their own; a hosted page carries no more up front. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-190.
  
  Compatibility: an `add-lens` edit that named an option its lens does not take, which applied with a warning, is refused; every declaration that compiled still compiles, with the same warning, and ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-190. What Graview Cloud does: on a change sent as a JSON patch or a whole document, refuse it when `lensOptionsRefused(stored, proposed)` answers any finding, as it refuses an edit's error; `propose_change` with `add-lens` needs nothing.
- 3bfdf76: The page says when a place has drawn and settled (FR-188). A host picturing a place headless waited on a guess — its opening frame gone, the network quiet, then 600 ms — and a font, an image or a worker view that came later than that was pictured half drawn. Now once the place the reader is on has drawn and settled — its face here, every part drawn on it arrived or said it did not, the fonts and images loaded, each worker view drawn or stopped (what stands in its place drawn), and nothing moving for 200 ms — the embed's root wears `data-graview-drawn`, its value the place's path, and `mount`'s `onDrawn(place)` is told the place as `where()` says it (`EmbedWhere`: face, path, stop). A move to another page, another stop on the scene or another face takes the attribute off in the commit that moves, and the next place puts it back as it settles; a place that never stops moving is said drawn after ten seconds all the same. `DRAWN_ATTRIBUTE` names it on `@graview/embed`, and `graview shots` waits for it before each picture where it waited 0.7 s, or 1.8 s on the scene. A worker view's region says `data-worker-view-drawn` once it has drawn what it was last shown with no picture still being fetched for it; `partsArriving()` on `@graview/react` counts the parts drawn and still on their way, and `partArriving(load)` counts a loader of one's own. `onDrawn` was told the face once it was on the page (FR-57); that is `onFaceDrawn` on `<Embed>` now, and `handle.drawn()` still waits for it. A hosted page carries 2.3 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and their budgets are raised by that, and the pages face, the embed, every face and the open kit's host by what they measured. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-188.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-188. `mount`'s `onDrawn` is handed the place (`EmbedWhere`) where it was handed the face, and is called once the place settles rather than when the face is first committed; a React host that wanted the old call passes `onFaceDrawn` to `<Embed>`. What Graview Cloud does: in `pictures.ts`, wait for `#graview-app [data-graview-drawn]` (or its value, the address pictured) in place of the opening's absence, network idle and the 600 ms settle.
- 328235d: A view takes files dropped on it (FR-193). Roadmap f-views-take-a-drop: Graview Cloud's family weekend wanted photographs and a phone's video dragged from Finder onto a day's card on its journey home view to become moments on that day; a worker view had no way to receive a file, and no act argument could carry one. A view now marks the element — `<section data-drop="moment" data-drop-connect="takenOn:day:saturday">` — and its manifest says which fields files become: `accepts: [{ kind: "moment", field: "photo" }, { kind: "moment", field: "clip" }]`, each a kind and its image, video or file field (`checkManifest` refuses a kind the app lacks, a field that holds no file, and a kind no act makes with that field). The host owns the rest. It offers the element as a drop target only where this seat may make that kind and link it, and the host's files take what its fields hold (`data-graview-drop`, said to assistive technology); while files are dragged over it the host draws it with its own outline and says what dropping there does, in a status of its own; on a coarse pointer the same element is a tap that opens the picker, asking for what the fields take (`image/*,video/*`), and Enter or Space on it does the same anywhere. Each file is held to the field its type fits (a picture to `photo`, a video to `clip`) and to the host's `kinds` and `maxBytes`, stored through the host's `files.upload`, and what was refused is said one sentence a file while the rest go on. A view that does not hear `drop` gets, per file, a record of the kind with its file on its field, linked as `data-drop-connect` says — by an act on the dropped-on record that makes one linked where the app has it, else the act that makes the kind and the derived "Set the day" — all in one change that one undo takes back, said "Added 4 moments to Saturday." A view that hears it (`graview.on("drop", "[data-drop]", (event) => …)`) is told once, with `event.files` as the host stored them — `{ href, name, type, bytes, width, height, poster, duration, at, field }`, never their bytes — and `event.refused`. `graview.act(name, [args, …])` runs several of one act as one change, and an act's argument takes a file only as the host handed it, in a drop or on a record the view was shown, going as the host kept it; any other `href` is refused.
