Where sight is not total, an act from a view's code still applies only from a press, except one that carries a drop's files and nothing the view could have read. `applyAll` takes `{ $made: n }` in an argument, the record call `n` of the same change made (`made(n)` from `@graview/core`), and judges that call once the record is there to name. A headless run (`graview view check`, `runWorkerViewHeadless`) says a `data-drop` of a kind or `data-drop-field` the manifest does not accept, and a `data-drop-connect` that is not `<relation>:<record id>` or names a record the view was not shown. A file field's editor on a host whose files take nothing (`maxBytes` 0) offers no picker and says the host takes no files, never "up to 0 bytes"; an empty video field on a record's page is drawn as its editor where the host takes `video/*`, as it already was, now held by a test. The graview-view-drawing skill teaches the drop, and every event a view hears as `{ type, target, element }` with what it carries: `value` and `checked`, `key`, `pressed`, `files` and `refused`. The guest-sandbox drop harness proves it in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox. A hosted page's first load carries 0.7 KB more (0.3 KB gzipped), the store's `{ $made: n }`; the drop itself is fetched only with a view that draws a drop target. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-193.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored formats are unchanged; an op keeps the record id a `{ $made: n }` was read as. The declaration: a worker view's manifest takes `accepts`. The wire: a worker view's runtime sends `{ type: "hears", events }` the first time its `graview.on` listens for each event, an act request's `args` may be a list of argument objects (1 to 100) for several of one act as one change, and the host sends a `dom-event` whose `event` is `drop`, carrying `files` and `refused`; a view that asks for none of it is sent nothing new. Derived tools are unchanged, and `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-193. What Graview Cloud does: mount views as it does — the page's `files` (`filesAt` under the app's base) is what a drop is stored through, so the files route's upload is the whole seam; let `propose_view` take `accepts` in a view's manifest; and teach `data-drop`, `data-drop-connect`, `accepts` and `graview.act(name, [args, …])` in its views guide.

## 0.1.38

### Patch Changes

- 28a63eb: A status board moves a card without a button under it, and the shipped lenses take search, filter and column options (Roadmap f-board-move-without-buttons, f-lens-search-filter). A Move… button under every card doubled a board's height, and the Recon app's Actions board could not be searched without a hand-drawn view that could not drag. A card is now dragged, carried by the keyboard — Space picks it up, the arrows carry it between and along the columns, Space drops it and Escape puts it back, each step said in a live region ("Public forms, picked up. Planned, 2 of 16.") — or moved from its own menu's "Move to ›", which lists every other column: the ones this seat may move it to, and the withheld ones struck through with the reason a press would meet (`columnChoices` on `@graview/core/describe`). The card stays one stop for Tab and says how it moves. `add-lens` and a declared or TypeScript `columns` lens take `options`: `search` (the fields Find looks in), `filters` (fields and relations standing as chips), `move` (the Move button back), `collapsed` and `hidden` (columns, by value), and `cap` (the newest cards a column keeps, with "Show N more"). A board of more than twenty cards shows Find without being asked; a smaller one shows it when `search` names its fields. A narrowed card moves as before. A calendar and a coverage take `search` and `filters` too. `graview check` says an option the kind cannot honor as `lens-option`, and `add-lens` refuses it. Up front a hosted page carries 0.7 KB more (722 bytes, 0.6 KB handed a compiled app), the options a declared lens is held to.
  
  Compatibility: a declaration gains the `columns` lens's `search`, `filters`, `move`, `collapsed`, `cap` and `hidden` options and the calendar's and coverage's `search` and `filters`, and `graview check` gains the warning `lens-option`; every declaration that compiled still compiles, and ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged.
- 91f5168: A view is hidden, shown, deleted and restored from the place menu. Views pile up as chats draw them, and the person annoyed by the clutter is standing at the places drop-down: now the place you are on in the bar, and each place in its drop-down, open the Pages menu — a right-click, Shift+F10 or the menu key, a finger resting on it — with Hide and Delete for a view. Hide takes it off the places, the scene's places and the home's gallery for everyone, still at its address; the drop-down's foot lists it under Hidden with Show. Delete takes it out of every face and lists it under Recently deleted with Restore, and the day it is gone for good, thirty days on; deleting the place you are on takes you to its kind's own place, else home, on Pages and the scene alike. A script view a chat drew is the host's: `mount`'s new `keptViews` (and the whole-page `Shell`'s, and `GraviewProvider`'s) (`KeptViews` from `@graview/core`: `current()`, `onChange`, `hide`, `show`, `remove`, `restore`, and `lens`) is where the page asks, and `current().may` says what this viewer may — the views a chat drew for an owner or an editor, the declaration's lenses for the owner. A declared lens is hidden with `set-lens-hidden` and deleted with `remove-lens`, handed to `lens` as a change to the declaration the host writes with its rollback. `registerWorkerView` registers a view under its manifest's `name` and `registerDeclaredLenses` marks a lens `declared`, which the menu reads; a view the app's own code registers is offered neither. A served store answers `/graview/views` (`createStoreHandler`'s `views` and `mayTidyViews`, `remoteViews` from `@graview/ship/browser` for the page), `graview serve` keeps what was tidied in `views.json` beside the store, `graview preview` writes a declared lens's change into the document it holds, and `devViews()` from `@graview/ship/dev` is the dev server's door. `memoryViews` and `placeTidying` (whether this viewer may tidy a place, and how) are on `@graview/core`, and `useTidiedViews` — the registry as the host's tidying leaves it — on `@graview/react`. A page carries only what hears the gesture and says the drop-down's foot has something to list: which place, whether a finger rested, what a press on the menu's rows does, the toast, the foot and the tidying are fetched on first use, and the registry's tidying only once the host lists something hidden or deleted. A hosted page carries 1.7 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too); their budgets are raised by that, and the pages face, the embed, every face and the guest host by what they measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire: a new route, `/graview/views` (GET what is hidden and recently deleted and what the asking seat may do; POST `{ hide | show | remove | restore: name }` or `{ lens: set-lens-hidden | remove-lens }`), and `createStoreHandler` takes `views` and `mayTidyViews`; every other route answers as before. New: `KeptViews`, `KeptViewsState`, `KeptViewsMay`, `RemovedView`, `LensTidyEdit`, `memoryViews`, `placeTidying`, `NO_KEPT_VIEWS`, `REMOVED_VIEW_DAYS` and `removedLensName` on `@graview/core`; `ViewMeta`, `ViewRegistration` and `Place` take `name` and `declared`; `keptViews` on the embed, the pages embed, `Shell` and `GraviewProvider`, `GraviewContextValue.keptViews`, `KeptViewsProvider`, `useTidiedViews` and `PLACE_MENU_EVENT` on `@graview/react`; `ViewTidyingSections`, `useViewTidyingSection`, `TidyGroups`, `useTidied`, `placeAfter`, `useKeptViewsState`, `placeGesture`, `openPlaceMenu`, `placeAim` and `PLACE_MENU_EVENT` on `@graview/primitives`; `answerViews`, `keepsTheApp`, `remoteViews`, `fileViews` and `VIEWS_PATH` on `@graview/ship`, `devViews` on `@graview/ship/dev`. What Graview Cloud does: keep each app's hidden view names and its deleted views (with the definition and its versions, thirty days, then gone), answer `KeptViews` for the viewer with `may` from their share (owner and editor: views; owner: lenses), write a lens's edit as a change with its usual undo, keep registering a deleted view's definition while it is kept so Restore brings it back in place, and pass the object as `keptViews` to `mount`.
- 5bd37dc: The first load asks for nothing it does not draw again. The place menu's tidying had put a round trip and six requests in front of the first paint: the preview's page asked `/graview/views` only after the document came, and waited for the answer before it drew; the frame fetched what tidies the registry though nothing was hidden; and the routed face's first chunks carried what a press on Hide or Delete does, the drop-down's foot and the gesture's reading, while the bar's panes carried the hover chips' switch. Now `graview preview` hands the views with the document, as it hands the store's state, and `remoteViews` starts from them (`state`) without asking; `useTidiedViews` fetches `tidyViews` the first time the host lists something hidden or deleted; the menu's rows decide what they offer as the menu opens and fetch what a press does while the pointer rests on them; the foot is fetched when the drop-down opens with something to list, the gesture's reading with the bar's panes; and the person's menu fetches the hover chips' switch only where the host offers chips. On a slow phone the roadmap is useful in 3.5 s where it took 4.1 and interactive in 5.3 s where it took 5.9, on 75 requests where it took 80, as main measured it before the batch; the tasks are useful in 3.4 s where they took 4.1.
  
  `tidyViews` is no longer on `@graview/react`'s main entry, which put it in every chunk that imports that entry: a host hands `keptViews` and `useTidiedViews` does the rest. The rule for whether a viewer may tidy a place is `placeTidying` on `@graview/core` (it was `tidyingOf` on `@graview/primitives`, a name core's suggestions already use), and `PLACE_MENU_EVENT` is on `@graview/react/provider` beside the menu it opens, still re-exported by `@graview/primitives`; `useKeptViews` is gone from `@graview/primitives`. The routed face alone loads 5.4 KB (2.0 KB gzipped) less before it draws; up front, a hosted page carries 0.3 KB more for the frame following the host's views itself, and the place menu's raises are measured again.
  
  Compatibility: the preview's `/graview/document` carries `views`, the answer `/graview/views` gives its seat; `remoteViews` takes an optional `state`. Ops, stored records, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged.
- 9c45f5d: The right-click menu offers a view the app already has before it draws a new one. Asked for a board of tasks where the app has "Tasks by status", the inline AI drew a second board, because the menu offered "Draw a panel" and nothing else. Where it would draw one, the menu now offers "Open Tasks by status" above "Draw a new view": the best of the app's places for the target, matched by what each is attached to, what it reads, its title and what it is (a board, a calendar), in one of the three rows its suggestions had, so the menu is no taller. The quick ask, which has the words, offers up to three ahead of its "Show …" panels — "a searchable board" finds Recon's "Actions by status". A view that fits only in part (it reads the kind but pictures another, or a word asked is not said by it) is offered as "Open and adjust …", which opens it with the quick ask on it to say what to change (from the quick ask, with the words already asked of it). No model is asked: `existingViewsOf` on `@graview/core/catalog` reads the places alone, and none is offered, with no row or heading, when nothing fits, for a question, or on the home with nothing asked. A declared or kept lens says which shipped lens it is (`Place.lens`), and a worker view registered with `registerWorkerView` says it is a view and the kinds its manifest reads (`Place.reads`), so a view added from a chat is matched as a declared one is. A hosted page carries 77 bytes more up front, for what a place is and reads; the matching and its rows are fetched with the inline AI.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. `ViewMeta` and `Place` take the optional `lens` and `reads`, and `CatalogPlace` takes `over`, `lens`, `reads` and `hidden`; a registration that says none of them registers as before.

## 0.1.37

### Patch Changes

- a130326: The date editor reads an instant as a record keeps one. A film festival's screening is kept as `2026-10-20T15:00:00-05:00`; pasted into its own "Starts" box, on the act's form or the record, it said "Not a date this reads", so the form would not send and "Schedule a screening" could not be done that way on either face. `readWhenTyped` now reads a stored instant whole — with its seconds, a `Z` or an offset — on the reader's clock, as the value is said, and a wall time with its seconds (`2026-10-20T15:00:00`) as the day and time it names.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; text the editor refused before it may now read.
- 7fa2dbc: A provisional link of a relation that only its derived connect and disconnect make or break can be kept. "Keep this link" was permitted through declared acts that connect or sever its relation, so once every relation came with a derived pair, a link of a relation with no declared act was kept by nobody a policy names; it is now permitted to whoever its derived connect is, the seats that may make or write the kind the relation leaves.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; a policy that named no act for such a relation now lets the kind's writers keep its links.
- cf691f4: A record can hold a video. A photo gallery built on Graview Cloud with three iPhone clips could show only a play glyph over a still: no field took a video, the host's files took none, and a view may not draw `<video>`. A document now says `{"type": "video"}` (or `{"type": "list", "of": "video"}`), a declaration `videoField()`, and the value is the `FileValue` the host's upload answered — `{ href, name, type, bytes, width?, height?, caption? }` — with what a player wants before it loads a byte: `poster` (a picture on the host), `duration` in seconds and `captions` (a WebVTT file on the host), each a path on the host or a length above 0, or refused. A video field takes only `video/*`, a picture field no video. The editor asks for videos where the host's `kinds` take `video/*` (`accept="video/*"`, "Choose a video", "This host doesn't take videos" where they do not) and sends one as it was picked: making a phone's HEVC `.mov` an MP4 every browser plays, its poster and its length are the host's to answer. The face plays a record's video — its poster first, the browser's own controls, `preload="metadata"`, captions where the value has them, sized from the value — drawn and fetched where the host serves files (`fileSrc`), its length read from what the player loaded where the value does not say it; a card or a row shows its poster (or its first frame) with a play glyph and its length. A record's facts, an image block and a headless run say it as "a 45-second video" (`videoWords`); the act's form and its argument's shape say `video: true`. A worker view whose manifest says `pictures` places `<graview-video data-record data-field>` and the host plays that record's video in it, from the records the view was shown, never handing the view a path or drawing it a `<video>`; and a viewer's press on a `data-lightbox` element opens the host's full-screen viewer for a picture or a video. The dev server's files door takes videos and serves them a range at a time. The `graview-view-drawing`, `graview-worker-view` and `graview-node-kind` skills say so. A hosted page carries 0.6 KB more up front for a video field's schema; the player, the card and the view's player are behind the doors already there, and the guest host's first load grows by under 1 KB for the press and the door.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged; a stored file value is read as before, and a value may now also carry `poster`, `duration` and `captions`. The declaration takes the field type `video` and `of: "video"`; a declaration that compiled still compiles. Derived tool input schemas for a file argument list the optional `poster`, `duration` and `captions`, and a video field's form control and argument shape say `video: true`. The guest wire is unchanged in shape: a video on a record is handed to a view that asks for `pictures` as its facts (`GuestFile.duration` beside `width`, `height` and `caption`) where it was handed its name, type and size. The open kit draws one element more, `graview-video`. `DEV_FILES_KINDS` takes `video/*`.
- d689fbc: A record page of a project's own draws its facts as the derived one does. The page `graview create` writes drew every fact as text: a festival's trailer was its file name, and a date was changed only through "Change the film", where the derived record page plays a video, shows a picture where it is kept, links a phone and edits each value where it stands. `RecordField` from `@graview/pages` is one fact as the derived page draws it, the derived page draws with it, and the scaffold's record page does too.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; `@graview/pages` exports `RecordField` and `RecordFieldProps`.
- 9121a9c: A relation can say no act makes or breaks it (`acts: "none"` on an edge declaration), and is then given no derived connect or disconnect: it is read from what was declared. The studio's own meta-schema says it of what an act creates, connects and severs and of the kind a computed field is on, which its named acts and the opened declaration set; once every relation came with a derived pair, "Link a kind (creates)" had taken a kind's strip past "Add a field".
  
  Compatibility: `EdgeDeclaration` takes `acts?: "none"`; the studio's acts are as they were.
- 862ab8c: A repair says what it still asks for, whether or not its rule said so. A festival's "Every selected film is screened" named `schedule` with the film and left the venue and the time to choose, as graview-invariant teaches, without writing `missing`; the problems page offered "Schedule Under Weather" as one press and answered it "Not yet: Venue id — invalid input: expected string, received undefined", so the repair could not be taken on either face. The store's violations now carry, on every repair whose rule did not say, `missing`: the arguments its act's input requires and the repair does not give. Every surface that asks before it applies asks for them. And a press that reaches the store and is refused by the act's own input is told to a watching harness (`refusedInput`), as a refusal by the policy is, so the harnesses hear an act offered whole that its input refuses. A hosted page carries 0.8 KB more up front for it, the compiled page too.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; a violation's repairs may now carry `missing` where their rule left it out.
- f860e41: A rule's today() is the app's day, not UTC's. Datetimes were said in the app's zone, and `today()` — what a rule, a computed field, a template and a label read — was still the UTC date, so an app in New York turned its day at 8 PM: at 01:30 UTC on Oct 17 a chore due Oct 16 was already overdue over dinner. With `brand.timeZone` declared, today() is the date there, and the day turns at the app's midnight: a document's rules, computed fields, guards and labels read it from the document's own brand, and a store told the zone (`new Store({ timeZone })`, which `openStore`, `openRemote`, the embed and `graview mcp` pass from the app's brand) is judged on that day, hands it to a rule written as a function in its context, as `openStore` already did, and ticks to it: `tick(now)` takes `now`'s day in that zone, and `tickEachDay` fires at that midnight, 23 hours after the last on the day clocks spring forward and 25 on the day they fall back, at the day's first moment where midnight never happens. A page reads the day of the app it draws, never the reader's zone and never the runtime's, so a server and a page agree on the day for the same app. Without a declared zone, today() is the UTC date, as before, and `openStore` still judges on the host's own date. A host that keeps a store on its own alarm (a Durable Object) passes `timeZone: brand.timeZone` to its store and sets the alarm `untilTheDayTurns(now, store.timeZone)` ahead; a tick at the turn of the day now says what the turn moved, judging what stood on the day the store was last ticked to. `isoToday(timeZone?, at?)` and `dayZoneFor` are exported, `dayOf` and `untilTheDayTurns` take a zone, and `rules-read-the-clock`'s fix says which midnight. The `graview-invariant` skill says so. A hosted page carries 0.7 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too); their budgets are raised by that.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and the declaration are unchanged. An app that declares `brand.timeZone` reads today() as the date in that zone, where it read the UTC date, on every surface and in `graview serve`. `StoreOptions` takes `timeZone` and `Store` has `timeZone`; `dayOf(now, timeZone?)` and `untilTheDayTurns(now, timeZone?)` take a zone; `tickEachDay` reads the store's `timeZone`. A store told its zone hands its day to its rules' context as `today` where neither the call nor its options carry one.
- 7d6f521: Datetimes are said in the app's time zone, and a template can say a time of day. An app declares its zone once, `brand.timeZone` (an IANA name, "America/New_York") on `defineApp`'s brand or a document's `brand`, and every instant kept with a zone (`2026-10-16T14:47:00Z`, `…-04:00`) is said on that clock: in lists, cards, record pages, a rule's sentence, an act's sentence and its echo to an agent, Activity's digest, the static page and the date editor, which reads and keeps an instant on the same clock. A person's zone wins over it where a host passes one — `timeZone` on `GraviewProvider`, the embed and the pages face, beside `locale` — and each reader's own clock is said when neither says, as before. A moment kept without a zone is still the wall time written. Describe templates take `{at | time}` ("10:47 AM") and `{at | datetime}` ("Oct 16, 2026, 10:47 AM EDT"), and a field block takes `as: "time"` and `as: "datetime"`; `{at | date}` and `relative` count an instant's day on the app's clock. An agent's act given `2026-10-16T10:47:00-04:00` echoed "2:47 PM", the server's UTC; a tool runtime handed the app now says it in the app's zone, and a host answering away from any page says its own words in it with `sayingIn({ appTimeZone }, run)`. A view is handed the zone rather than formatted strings: every props push carries `locale` and `timeZone`, for `Intl.DateTimeFormat(props.locale, { timeZone: props.timeZone, … })`, so no view hard-codes a zone again. `graview check` and the document's checker refuse a zone the runtime does not know, or an offset, by name (`brand-time-zone`); `set-brand` sets one (`timeZone`, null clears it), and a diff says "Times are said in America/New_York." `describeApp` says the zone. The `graview-brand`, `graview-node-kind` and `graview-worker-view` skills say so. A hosted page carries 2.0 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and the guest host about 0.5 KB; their budgets are raised by that.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged; the declaration takes an optional `brand.timeZone`, and a declaration without it says every time as before. The check finding `brand-time-zone` is new. The guest wire's props carry `locale` and `timeZone`. `Money` (a block's and a template's `money`) takes `timeZone`; `readableFields` takes `timeZone`; `momentAsRead`, `timeAsRead` and `whenAsRead` take a zone after the locale. `sayIn` takes `readerTimeZone` and `appTimeZone` beside `reader` and `app`. A derived edit's sentence and Activity's digest say an instant's day and time on the page's zone, where they said them on the runtime's clock.
- f97a149: Every relation comes with its connect and disconnect, as every kind comes with its edit, remove and archive. After an `add-relation` — a moment taken on a day, a moment that features a traveler — nothing could link two records until somebody also wrote an act that `connects`. Now each kind's relation is given `connect-<kind>-<relation>` and `disconnect-<kind>-<relation>` (the relation in kebab-case, `connect-moment-taken-on`), on the record it leaves as `id` and the record it reaches as `to`. A cardinality-one relation is set and cleared ("Set the day", "Clear the day") and connecting another replaces the link it had; a many one is linked and unlinked ("Link a traveler", "Unlink a traveler"); each reads from the other end too ("Link a moment"), its description carries the relation's own, and a kind with two relations to the same kind tells them apart by the relation's words ("Set the person (reviewer)"). Any act the app declares that `connects` a relation replaces the derived connect, any that `severs` it the derived disconnect; an `appendOnly` relation is given no disconnect, and a relation to another app's kind is given neither, because it is a field the derived edit already sets. They are permitted as a derived edit is — to whoever may make or change the kind the relation leaves, or a grant naming them — and refuse a record the seat may not see. Because they declare what they connect and sever, a line's acts, the far record's page, the inline AI's fills, an import's links and an agent's tools offer them with nothing more; `graview describe` says, per relation, which acts make and break it. A grant or a repair in a document may name one, and it follows a renamed kind or relation. `edge-without-severer` is gone: a relation some act makes and none breaks is now given its disconnect, so the asymmetry it warned of cannot be declared.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas gain `connect-<kind>-<relation>` and `disconnect-<kind>-<relation>` ({ id, to }, strict) for every relation no act connects or severs — in this repository's apps, only the desk's `uses` relation (`connect-app-uses`, `disconnect-app-uses`); the conformance kit announces `connect-vendor-fills` and `disconnect-vendor-fills` on the 0.1.10 shortlist. The check no longer gives `edge-without-severer`. A hosted page carries 2.6 KB more up front, handed a compiled app or not: the derivation the store registers and a document's grants are checked by.
- d689fbc: `graview create --link` run by npm writes dependencies npm installs. It wrote pnpm's `link:` whichever package manager made the project, and npm refuses `link:` outright ("Unsupported URL Type"), so a project linked to a checkout and made with npm could not be installed. Under npm the framework is now `file:` to each package's directory, which npm links; under pnpm it is `link:` as before.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 34fa7b9: No import cycle is left in any package's source, in types or otherwise. `seenBy` asks a store only for what a seat's view reads of it (`Viewed`) and hands back the store's own type, so the seat's module needs nothing of the store's; what judges a seat's sight (`Judged`) is the sight's; what `describePlace` says a place shows (`PlaceDescription`, `DescribedPart` and the rest) is a module of its own, which the coverage grid in words reads; `ListFields` is the arrangement's, beside `listFieldsOf`, and `parseArrangement` is reached from its own module; the context menu's shapes (`MenuTarget`, `MenuItem`, `MenuSection`, `MenuSectionSource`) are a module the provider names them from; `ToolDefinition` and `ToolAnnotations` are a module the subscription tools declare theirs with; and an app link's, a first look's and Notify me's props live on the side of the door that the drawn part reads them from. Every name is exported from the same entry as before.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, and tool names and input schemas are unchanged — only where a type is declared moved, and every entry exports what it did. `seenBy` is generic over the store it is handed rather than its schema, so a call that named the schema (`seenBy<S>(…)`) names the store instead. The hosted page's first load is 7 bytes smaller.
- 0d440e2: The hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front than the 0.1.37 batch's three raises add up to: the app time zone, every relation's connect and disconnect, and the video field each measured alone, and together they share less of what they bring than each assumed.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- e56c351: Two records of one name are never told apart by their id. A festival with three films called "Small Harbor" offered "Small Harbor · film:small-harbor" in every picker, the search page and Find, because no one fact told all three apart and `tellApart` fell back to the id. It now says two facts together ("Ana Smith · Shorts"), else the one fact a record holds alone, else which of them it is in the order they were made ("2 of 3").
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; a picker's, an ask's and a search hit's words for namesakes change where they said an id.

## 0.1.36

### Patch Changes

- 9f3e1d1: A record's pictures and documents load where the host serves them, on every face. A record keeps its file at the path the host's upload answered (`/graview/files/…`), without the base the app is served under, and the face drew `<img src>` and every link and download at that path; Graview Cloud serves an app at `/a/<app>/` and its files only under it, so every thumbnail, gallery, picture full size, inline PDF and download asked the workspace's root and broke. `HostFiles` now says where it serves a file (`src(href)`), and every place the framework draws, links or downloads a record's file goes through it (`fileSrc(files, href)` from `@graview/core`), the href itself where the host does not say. `filesAt(url)` derives it from its own url: an href under `/graview/files/` is drawn under `url`, so a host that already hands `filesAt(base + "/graview/files")` gets its pictures under its base with nothing else changed; the dev door's `/__graview/files/…` is drawn as it is. A worker or frame view the framework mounts as a kind's view takes its `file` from the page's `files.src` unless its definition gives one. What the page says of a file (`data-graview-thumb`, `data-graview-full`, `data-graview-removed-later`) and what it asks `kept` still name it by the href the record keeps. The `graview-ship` and `graview-view-drawing` skills say so. The guest host's first load grows by under 200 bytes for it, and its two budgets are raised by that.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records, the declaration and the guest wire are unchanged. `HostFiles` takes an optional `src?(href: string): string`; a host without it draws each file at its href as before. `filesAt` answers `src`, which draws an href under `/graview/files/` under its url — a host that handed `filesAt` a url elsewhere than its files' own address now draws its files at that url. `WorkerViewDefinition.file` and `guestView`'s `file` default to the page's `files.src`.

## 0.1.35

### Patch Changes

- d33bc19: A host may say later whether it still keeps a file (the Roadmap's f-files-kept-async). `HostFiles.kept(href)` answered only at once, so a host whose bytes are elsewhere — Graview Cloud's are in R2 — passed none, and every picture a record held in the past was drawn as kept while somebody scrubbed back, though the host had let it go. `kept` may now answer `boolean | Promise<boolean>`: the picture is drawn as itself until the promise settles and as "removed later" if it settles to `false`, on a card's thumbnail, an `image` block and a record's gallery alike. The page asks once per href for as long as it holds that `files`, so a scrub, a second card or the record's page reads the answer already given rather than asking again, and a promise that rejects is taken as kept rather than thrown. A synchronous `kept` is asked as before, every time a picture is drawn.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; a host's `kept` that answers a boolean works as it did.
- ac91d40: An `add-relation` edit can name another app's kind. Since 0.1.30 a whole document could declare `"forHouse": { "to": ["@nick/house/house"], "cardinality": "one" }`, but the edit refused the same relation (`"@nick/house/house" is not a kind this app has`), so a host that changes an app by edits — graview.cloud's builder, `propose_change`, the studio — could not link one app to another. `editDocument` now takes `{"op": "add-relation", "kind": "scenario", "relation": "forHouse", "to": ["@nick/house/house"], "cardinality": "one"}` and gives a document that checks clean, judged by the rules `graview check` applies: a `to` that mixes this app's kinds with another's, reaches two apps, says `inverse` or `appendOnly` is refused at `edits.<n>` with the checker's own finding (`linked-mixed`, `linked-apps`, `linked-target`, `linked-inverse`, `linked-append-only`). It says `scenario gains a relation, "forHouse", to one house in the app @nick/house, another app, read there as whoever is looking.` `rename-relation` and `remove-relation` work on one. `planMigration` dropped a far relation's values on every migration, since it carried only declared fields; it now keeps them, moves them with a rename and clears them with the relation, said like a relation's links. The studio read a far relation as a text field; it now reads it as a relation (`toAnotherApp` on the edge), makes one with `add-edge-to-another-app` (`to: "@nick/house/house"`), says it as `add-relation`, and writes it back to a checkout as `linkedRecordField([...]).optional()`.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration is unchanged — a document that compiled compiles the same — and the edits take what they refused, with the checker's finding codes at `edits.<n>`; a stored graph migrates keeping what it dropped; ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged. The studio's own tools gain `add-edge-to-another-app`.
- f2666ed: Playback asks the host for a past declaration when it reaches one, instead of needing every version handed up front. To draw the past under the declaration then in force, `HistoryHost.declarations` had to carry each version's compiled declaration before the page opened — 27 of them on Cloud's Roadmap app — so a host that sent only the sentences had its past drawn under today's declaration. `HistoryHost.declarationAt(seq)` now answers a mark handed without its `declaration`, called with that mark's `seq` the first time playback reaches a moment under it and asked once per mark (per `declarationAt` function, so hand the same one), returning the declaration or a promise of it. While it is on its way the face holds the picture it had rather than flash the moment under today's declaration, and draws it under today's after 400ms if the answer is slow; an answer of `undefined`, or one that fails, is as if the mark had no declaration — the last one handed before it, else today's. Marks handed with a declaration are never asked about and work as before, and a page that never opens the past asks nothing: the asking lives on `@graview/core/past` as `askDeclaration(host, seq)`, which answers `{ declaration }` or `{ waiting }`, and `pastAt` reads it. It threads wherever `history` is handed — `GraviewProvider`, `mount`/`FrameOptions` and `PagesApp`'s `PageContext`. The holding is a few hooks in the provider's playback, 0.5 KB more up front on a hosted page.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: keep sending the marks with their sentences only, and add `declarationAt: (seq) => fetchVersionDeclaration(seq)` to the `history` it hands, answering with the compiled `{ schema, invariants?, policy?, modules? }` of the version that began at that seq.

## 0.1.34

### Patch Changes

- 5fc86f5: Opening a context menu spends no thinking-model call (FR-185). The menu's AI section asked for suggestions every time it opened, and `suggestHere` goes to the Thinking slot when there is no Fast one, so a host that gave only `ai.thinking` paid one language-model call per right-click, and opening and closing a menu cost as much as asking. Now a menu asks as it opens only with a Fast slot; with only a Thinking slot (or `complete`, or `turn`) it offers a "Suggest…" row (`[data-menu-item="ai-suggest"]`) that asks when pressed, keeps the menu open, and draws what was suggested in it, with the keyboard on the first row that arrived. `HostAi.suggestOnOpen` lets a host say it: `false` makes it the row with a Fast slot too; unsaid, a menu with a Fast slot asks on opening as before. `suggestsOnOpen(ai)` from `@graview/tools/inline-ai` answers whether a menu would. A menu drawn again while open no longer asks again: it asked once per drawing of its target object, which the menu makes anew on every draw, so a Fast slot was asked twice for one right-click. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-185.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-185. What Graview Cloud does: nothing to stop paying for a right-click with only a Thinking slot; to give its readers suggestions as a menu opens, fill `ai.fast` (Jev, `jevFast`), and pass `suggestOnOpen: false` where a Fast call per menu is still too many.
- f48b7d1: A panel drawn from the right-click menu opens in the face it was asked on (FR-184). On Pages, "Draw a panel" did nothing: the menu at the pointer was drawn outside the routed face's way of going (`GoToContext`, which Pages gives by address), so the inline AI went the scene's way, set the seat's draft with nowhere on the page to show it, and the draft waited for the scene. The routed face now gives its way of going to everything it draws, the menu and the quick ask's card included, so "Draw a panel", a panel the menu suggests and a panel or a place taken from the quick ask's card land at `/~draft` with "Keep as a lens" and "Discard", or at the place's page, and never switch face. Over the scene a drawn panel is framed on the scene's surface — the panel's color, an edge, rounded corners, a little in from the picture's edges — instead of a bare list on the ground, and the panel beside the map (a district's records, a record's links) is put away while it stands and comes back when it is kept or discarded. An ask the menu makes stays in the menu until what it asked for lands: "Asking…" while it is on its way, then the menu is put away as the ghost, the panel or the page arrives; an ask that fails is said in the menu, where the person who asked is looking, as well as in Activity, and the quick ask's card says a failed offer on the card. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-184.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-184. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — a host that mounts the pages face gets the panel at `/~draft` on the next version.
- 8f09e52: A reader who may do nothing is told how to get in, not shown every act struck through (FR-183). A signed-out visitor on a public app right-clicked an era and the menu said "Nothing you may do with an era — 7 actions withheld" over seven disabled buttons, each with the policy's sentence; the list's foot said "~~Add an era~~ — Not permitted: …"; nothing said how to get the right, and "Notify me…" was offered to `anonymous` and kept a subscription for nobody. A host now says what a signed-out reader could gain: `signIn: { label, run, roles? }`, handed only while the reader is a visitor, on `mount` from `@graview/embed` and `@graview/embed/pages`, on `GraviewProvider` (which the Shell draws under, and a provider inside another takes from the one around it) and on the pages' context. Where every act withheld from the reader is one a member could take — one some role could, or one of `roles`, the roles signing in gives, when the host names them — the context menu and the Inspector on either face, the list's foot and a record's acts say `label` as one press that calls `run` (`data-testid="sign-in"`), in place of the struck acts; the face's own rows (Open, Show in the scene) stay. With `signIn` given, "Notify me" is not offered on the menu, a record's page or after an act, and the seat's chat keeps no subscription. A member lacking one role is still told each act they may not take and who could (FR-16), and so is a visitor where one act is one no role can take. `signInInstead(withheld, signIn)` and the `SignIn` type are exported from `@graview/core`, the same judgment for a page of an app's own. A hosted page, compiled or not, carries 0.2 KB more up front for it, under a raise of 0.3 KB more up front on each of the two budgets. The embed's README and the graview-embed skill say the option. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-183.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire is unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-183. What Graview Cloud does: pass `signIn: { label: "Sign in to make changes", run }` to `mount` while nobody is signed in, and drop its interim `visitorMenuSections` row and the CSS hiding `[data-testid=withheld]`, `[data-testid=no-affordances]` and `[data-menu-section=notify]`.
- b320b36: Find offers a past record the words name when no current record is named by them, only mentions them (the eleventh walk). An animal shelter with 62 animals in its care and 3,000 gone home searched for "1539", the number in "Moose #1539": the adopter whose phone ends 1539 answered, and since a current record had answered, the horizon held and Moose, adopted in 2022, was offered nowhere — on the scene's Find or on `/search`, at either width. A current record that carries the words only in a field no longer keeps the past out: where no current record is named by the words and a past one is, the past ones named are in the answer, said as past, ranked by how they matched (the name before a field), and `searched.past` says it looked there. A current record named by the words keeps the horizon as before.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged.
- 9c79c96: The record page `graview create` writes names the record in its heading as the derived page does (the eleventh walk). The name in the heading was a button called "Name: Daisy #0071" where the derived record page's is called "Daisy #0071" — its heading already says what it is — so a person looking for the name to press found "Change the animal" instead, a press more on the pages at either width; the discography's song page, copied from it, did the same. The scaffold's `src/ui/pages.tsx` passes `said={facts.label}` to the heading's `EditableValue`, and a test holds it to the derived page's.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; a project made before keeps its own page until it changes the line.
- 55ab33c: The right-click menu is ready before the click (FR-186). The menu derived its acts and sections as it opened, fetched its AI section behind Suspense, and asked for suggestions afresh every time, so it filled in while the reader watched: "Thinking…", then rows arriving. Now when the pointer rests on a record, a card, a row or a kind for 150 ms — in the scene and on the routed face; a pass-over across a list does nothing — or the keyboard lands on one (so Shift+F10 opens at once), or a finger goes down on one (so a long press does), the menu that would open there is got ready: the pane and its act forms fetched, its acts derived and kept, and each section's new `prepare` called, which fetches the inline AI's chunk and the sheet "Notify me…" opens. Nothing is fetched before that, so a hosted page's first load is unchanged. The acts derived for a target are kept per store, by the selection, the seat, the providers, the pins and the focus, until the graph changes (`prepareAffordances` and `AffordancesAsked` from `@graview/react`; `useAffordances` reads the same kept derivation). Suggestions are kept per target, by the seat, the declaration and the host's `ai`, until the record changes (for a list, a lens or a place, until the graph does): a menu opened again draws what was suggested in its first frame and asks nothing, and a failure is not kept. Hovering asks no model, ever. A host's own menu section may give `prepare` too (`MenuSection.prepare`); `useMenuPrepare()` from `@graview/react/drawing` calls every section's for a target, and the routed face's `Inspector` takes `aimAt`, what a menu opened at an element would be about. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-186.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-186. What Graview Cloud does: nothing for its menus to be ready before the click; a section it adds through `menuSections` that fetches what it draws can say so in `prepare`.
- 9c79c96: The test `graview create` writes still holds once the app says who may do what (the eleventh walk). The shelter followed graview-permissions and its `pnpm verify` went red on the scaffold's own `tests/domain.test.ts`: "expected [] to include 'add-animal'" — the derivation was asked for nobody, and every act in it was applied with no author, so a store under a policy refused them all and nothing said why. The test now takes every act, and asks the derivation, as somebody holding every role the app declares (`app.policy?.roles`), and says so where a reader meets it; with no policy it reads as before.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; a project made before keeps its own test.

## 0.1.33

### Patch Changes

- 01fc2ce: A read connection takes its own calendar's changes under a declared policy, and a change coming in that the store refuses is said as refused, not as a calendar it could not reach (FR-181). `SyncEngine.guard()` answers the store's `permits` for every surface, and it had no exemption for the mapping's own system: with a policy declared, the engine's own create and `edit-<kind>`, authored by `google-calendar`, met the read-only sentence, and the pass reported "could not reach the calendar; will try again", because the engine read every throw in its inbound pass as the remote being offline. `permits` now lets the mapping's own system through as `admit` already did, while a person, an agent or a view is still refused with the sentence. A change coming in that the store refuses — a policy, an invariant, a host's own `admit` — is caught where it lands and said in `SyncReport.refused`, which now holds `SyncRefusal`s (`{ localId, error, resource?, id? }`, the remote's resource and record id for a change coming in, and `localId` the id a new record would have had), and the pass goes on; only the remote failing is `offline`. `sayReport` names such a refusal by the calendar's event. Choosing a side with `resolve` still throws a refusal to whoever chose. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-181.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire is unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-181. `SyncReport.refused` entries may carry `resource` and `id`, and `SyncRefusal` is exported from `@graview/core/sync`. What Graview Cloud does: drop its interim stand-in for the connection's system past `permits`, and read a refused inbound change from `report.refused` where `offline` is false.
- a583b18: `mount` takes the host's subscriptions, and a page that keeps them on a served store carries no rule parser (FR-182). `GraviewProvider` and the pages' context took `subscriptions`, but the embed's options had none, so on a hosted page the sheet "Notify me…" and Activity's list kept a reader's subscriptions in the page's memory. `subscriptions` (any `SubscriptionStore`) is now one of the embed's options, on `mount` from `@graview/embed` and from `@graview/embed/pages`, and on `<Embed>`, handed to the provider on both faces and to the pages' context. `remoteSubscriptions` moved out of the module that answers `/graview/subscriptions` into one of its own, so asking the route no longer brings the expression and template parsers, which the answer judges a subscription with, into a hosted page's first chunk: the compiled shell handing `mount` a `remoteSubscriptions` store was 10,360 bytes (10.1 KB) heavier up front than the shell without one, and is now 767 bytes heavier, 9.4 KB less; the page that compiles its document, which carries the parsers anyway, is 1.9 KB less. Both are still exported from `@graview/ship`, `@graview/ship/browser` and `@graview/ship/runtime` as they were. The embed's README and the graview-embed skill say the option. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-182.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire is unchanged: `/graview/subscriptions` answers as before, and `answerSubscriptions`, `remoteSubscriptions` and `SUBSCRIPTIONS_PATH` keep their entries; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-182. What Graview Cloud does: pass `subscriptions: remoteSubscriptions({ url, principal })` to `mount` and drop its interim stand-in for the store.

## 0.1.32

### Patch Changes

- 7d1c3e1: A project `graview create` writes names an icon in its `index.html` and `embed.html` (an empty one, until the Shell draws the brand's mark). Google Chrome asks for `/favicon.ico` before any script runs, and the new project had none, so its first page load put a 404 on the console. The smoke rehearsal caught it once the nightly's GPU leg ran in Google Chrome rather than Playwright's bundled build.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.

## 0.1.31

### Patch Changes

- 6009469: An app says which calendar it keeps in step with in its own declaration, rather than in code. A document's `connections.<name>` names the system (`google-calendar`), the calendar (or the environment variable holding its id), the kind its events are and which field holds each part of one, a filter (the whole calendar, one of Google's colors by name, or a title tag such as `#graview`, kept in the title or not), the direction (`read`, where the calendar owns the kind and an act here that would change one is refused with a sentence saying so, or `both`) and the conflict policy. `graview check` says each wrong shape at its place: a kind or field the app lacks, a field of the wrong type, a color Google has no name for, a tag that is not one word, and an app with no act to land an inbound change through. A `date` field is an all-day event's day, its end inclusive here; a `datetime` field a timed event's instant. Filters apply as events arrive, since Google refuses a sync token beside a search: an event that leaves the filter is gone here, and one made here carries the color or tag that keeps it in, and a private property naming the app. `graview serve` and `graview preview` run the connections beside the store, with credentials from the environment (`GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`, `GOOGLE_REFRESH_TOKEN`, or the prefix and file `auth` names), each pass credited to `google-calendar` in Activity and the cursor kept in `connections.json` beside the data; `--no-sync` or a declared `paused` leaves them off. A host runs them with `runConnections` from `@graview/ship` or `@graview/ship/runtime`, handing its own `token({ connection, refresh })` and a place to keep their state. The studio carries an app's connections through an edit, the calendar following a renamed kind.
  
  A page that draws an app reads `connections` loosely and carries none of this, so a hosted page is unchanged; the studio's checker judges them, which adds 5.6 KB (2.1 KB gzipped) to every face and to the embed with the studio handed in, and those raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: a document may carry `connections` (until now an unknown key), and `graview check` adds `connection-kind`, `connection-field`, `connection-field-type`, `connection-when`, `connection-filter`, `connection-color`, `connection-tag`, `connection-creates`, `connection-edit` and the warning `connection-remove`; a document without `connections` compiles and checks as before. New: `GraviewApp.connections`, `connections` in `document.schema.json`, and on `@graview/core/sync` `connection`, `connectionMapping`, `creatingAct`, `inConnectionFilter`, `isDayField`, `GOOGLE_COLORS`, `CONNECTION_TAG`, `MADE_BY_GRAVIEW`, `Connection`, `ConnectionDeclaration` and `ConnectionRun`, with `GoogleCalendarOptions.keep` and `.madeHere`; on `@graview/ship` and `/runtime` `runConnections`, `sayReport`, `GOOGLE_TOKEN_URL` and their types, and on `@graview/ship` `fileConnectionState`, `readEnvFile` and `connectionsFileOf`; `PreviewOptions.connections` and `Preview.connections`; `graview serve` and `graview preview` take `--no-sync`.
- 4784ba8: A date and a date and time are given as people give them (Roadmap f-date-inputs). Every place a person gives one — a record's value changed in place on its page or its card, an act's form on the pages, the scene's ask — draws one editor (`WhenInput`, on `@graview/primitives` and `/pages`): a text box that reads "tomorrow 3pm", "Fri 10:30", "17 Oct", "in 2 weeks", "next Mon" or "10/17" (in the reader's order of digits), says back in words the day it read and the zone it reads it in, and refuses words that name no day rather than guessing, so the form it is in will not send them. Beside it a button (or Alt with the down arrow) opens a month to pick from, a grid the keyboard walks — arrows, Page Up and Page Down, Home and End, Enter, Escape back to the button — with each day named in full; it sits under the box, so on a phone it never runs off the screen. The editor and its month are fetched when first asked for, and a date that can be changed fetches its editor while the page is idle. A value opened to change is chosen whole, so what is typed replaces it.
  
  A document's `datetime` was asked for as a bare text box that wanted `2026-10-17T15:00:00Z`; `describeArg` now reads it, and `z.iso.datetime()`, as a date with a time that names an instant (`{ type: "date", time: true, instant: true }`, and `instant` on a form's date field). Such a moment is typed on the reader's clock and kept in UTC with its `Z`; a field whose own pattern takes a bare `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM` keeps the wall time as written. An instant is said on the reader's clock with its zone ("Oct 17, 2026, 3:00 PM EDT", `momentAsRead`), and a calendar places it on the reader's day and hour rather than UTC's.
  
  An end moves with its start: where a kind (or an act) has a start and an end — its `start` and `end` roles, else fields named for them (`starts`/`ends`, `from`/`until`, …) — a start moved in place carries the end as far, in one change with one undo; in a form or the scene's ask, a first start puts an empty end an hour after it, and a start moved carries the end it had. `@graview/core/words` gains `readWhenTyped`, `storedOfWall`, `wallOfStored`, `wallNow`, `namesInstant`, `zoneSaid`, `endAfterStartMoved` and `startAndEndOf`. `pnpm verify date-inputs` holds it on both faces and a 390-pixel phone.
  
  Nothing up front changes on a hosted page but 42 bytes, and with the rest of 0.1.31 merged the page is 0.1 KB more up front (raised in `scripts/budget-raises/dates-and-the-quick-ask-together-*.json`); the scene face before it draws is 7.2 KB larger (954,856 bytes), and the embed's budgets are raised in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: derived tool names and input schemas — no tool name or input schema changes; a form's date field (`ScalarField`) gains an optional `instant`, and a document's `datetime` argument describes itself as a date with a time instead of as text, so a form and the scene ask for it with the date editor.
- 938721b: The Google Calendar transport has now met Google itself, and keeps step with what it found. An access token Google refuses (401: an hour old, or revoked) is asked for again once with `token({ refresh: true })` and the request goes through; refused a second time, it throws a sentence naming the token. A pull follows `nextPageToken` to the end, so a calendar longer than one page is read whole and its sync token kept, and `pageSize` sets how many events a page holds. A deletion Google answers with no body is acknowledged rather than reported as refused. An all-day event arrives as its day, `start.date` and `end.date`, with no time invented for it. And a first full pull no longer offers `createInbound` the canceled events Google still lists. `scripts/verify-google-calendar.mjs` proves each of these against a real test calendar, with its credentials kept outside the checkout.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; `GoogleCalendarOptions.token` takes an optional `GoogleTokenRequest` (`{ refresh?: boolean }`), `GoogleCalendarOptions.pageSize` is new, and `GoogleTokenRequest` is exported from `@graview/core/sync`.
- ff88015: A person can ask to be told when something happens in an app, and the host is told when it does (Roadmap f-notify-subscriptions). A subscription is `{ who, app, when }`, where `when` is an act running (any, one by name, and/or one on a record), a record of a kind added, a kind's records or one record changed, or a record reaching a condition written in the app's own rule language (`done == true`, `amount > 100`); it is told only of changes somebody else made unless it says `alsoMine`. `matchSubscriptions(store, ops, subscriptions)` on the new `@graview/core/subscriptions` judges a commit batch by batch as each subscriber's seat sees it — a change to a record they cannot see never matches, a field kept from them is neither said nor read by a condition, a change withheld from them is said as "Someone" — and answers at most one `SubscriptionMatch` per batch per subscription, with who made it, its sentence, the acts it ran and each record it touched with the fields it changed. `watchSubscriptions(store, { subscriptions, onSubscriptionMatch })` calls a host's hook on every commit; `subscriptionWords` says a subscription in words and `subscriptionProblem` says why one could never be told anything. The shapes and `memorySubscriptions()` are on the main entry.
  
  `@graview/ship`'s handler keeps them (`subscriptions`, a `SubscriptionStore` of the host's; in memory when unsaid), serves each seat its own at `/graview/subscriptions`, and calls `onSubscriptionMatch` once per matching batch; `remoteSubscriptions` is the page's half, `fileSubscriptions` the file `graview serve` keeps beside the store and prints each match from. A chat subscribes its person with three new tools, `subscribe`, `unsubscribe` and `list_subscriptions`, listed where the host keeps subscriptions (`ToolRuntimeOptions.subscriptions`, `createMcpHttpHandler`'s, and always under `graview mcp`, which shares the file with `graview serve`).
  
  On both faces the context menu offers "Notify me…" on a record, a kind's list and an act (a menu target now says the `act` it was opened on), a record's page offers "Notify me when this changes", and an act's form when it is done offers "Notify me when someone else does this": each opens one sheet already filled in, so from a right-click a person subscribes in two presses. What a person is told of is listed in Activity, each with one press to stop. The provider keeps them where its host says (`subscriptions`), else in the page.
  
  It adds 0.8 KB more up front to a hosted page (864 bytes, 330 gzipped), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: the wire: a new route, `/graview/subscriptions` (GET the seat's own; POST `{ add: { when, alsoMine? } }` or `{ remove: id }`), and `createStoreHandler` takes `subscriptions` and `onSubscriptionMatch`; every other route answers as before. Derived tool names and input schemas: unchanged for a runtime given no `subscriptions`; given one, `subscribe`, `unsubscribe` and `list_subscriptions` are listed beside the read tools, and an act named any of them is listed as `<name>_2`. New: `@graview/core/subscriptions`, `Subscription`, `SubscriptionWhen`, `SubscriptionMatch`, `SubscriptionStore`, `OnSubscriptionMatch`, `memorySubscriptions` and `subscriberOf` on `@graview/core`, `MenuTarget.act`, `PointerMenu.act`, `GraviewContextValue.subscriptions`, and `NotifySections`, `NotifyDoor`, `NotifyButton`, `NotifiedDoor`, `useNotifySection`, `useNotifiedCount` and `askToNotify` on `@graview/primitives`.
- 294f181: The inline AI picks from what the app already knows, previews before it keeps, and leaves an entry in Activity (Roadmap f-ai-candidate-catalog, f-activity-ai-entries). A host that hands the seat a model now gets an AI section in the context menu on both faces: a few suggestions the model picked for what was right-clicked, "Fill in with AI" and "Draw a panel". The model never invents: `catalogOf(store, target, { principal })` on the new `@graview/core/catalog` derives, for a record, a selection, a field, a list or a lens, an act or a place, the candidates with an id and a line each — the acts the viewer may run there, the panels its kind can be drawn as, the places and records next to it, the values its fields may take — and `aiContextOf` the compact state a model reads beside them (where the viewer is, the target, the selection, the records one link away, what was done lately, a digest of the declaration cached per declaration), both under the viewer's sight: nothing they may not see or run is ever offered or said.
  
  `HostAi` gains two provider slots: `fast`, a typed-decision model in Jev's shape (`{ model, decide }`; `jevFast()` fills it with Jev), and `thinking`, a language model (`{ model, think({ system, prompt, state, catalog }) }`); unsaid, `decide` and `complete` serve them. Every call reports its tier, model, tokens and cost to the interaction and to `ai.meter`. `@graview/tools/inline-ai` holds the engine (`suggestHere`, `fillHere`, `panelHere`), the ghost (`ghostOf`, `acceptGhost`) and stub providers (`stubFast`, `stubThinking`) for tests and a dev app; the todo example takes them at `?ai=stub`. A proposed change waits as a ghost with Accept, Accept some and Dismiss, is kept as one batch whose ops carry lineage `interaction` and come through `ai:<interaction>`, and is taken back with one undo; a panel is drawn where the seat draws a view, and "Keep as a lens" makes it a real one.
  
  Each interaction is one entry in Activity (`AiInteraction`: who, when, the target, what was asked, offered and taken or dismissed, the batches it made with their undo, the models and their cost), and the change it made is drawn in it rather than again beside it. Activity filters All, Changes or AI and "Just mine"; a record's page lists what AI did there (`RecordInteractionsDoor`); an entry can ask again or show its panel again. A host keeps them with `ai.interactions` (`{ load, keep }`). `ai: { off: true }` hides every AI part on both faces — the menu's section, the ghost, the ask field, the entries and the filter — rather than disabling it.
  
  It adds 1.3 KB more up front to a hosted page (1,305 bytes, 460 gzipped), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives: an op's `lineage` may carry `interaction`, the id of the AI interaction it came from; a reader that does not know it reads the lineage as before. New: `@graview/core/catalog`, `@graview/tools/inline-ai`, `HostAi.off`, `.fast`, `.thinking`, `.interactions` and `.meter`, `GraviewContextValue.aiDesk`, `GoTo.draft`, and `GhostDoor`, `InlineAiSections`, `useInlineAiSection` and `RecordInteractionsDoor` on `@graview/primitives`.
- 9a5e067: The quick ask replaces the Ask box (Roadmap f-quick-ask-framework). No face draws a field at its foot that grows into a chat thread any more: `SeatField`, its panel and Pages' `PageAsk` are gone, and so are their settings, the Shell's `ask` and `chat`, a routed face's `context.ask`, the embed's `seat` option and `handle.setAskField`. In their place, where the host's `ai` gives a model and does not say `off`, ⌘K (Ctrl+K) opens a command bar beside what the reader is on — the selected card on the scene, the record or list a page is about — that completes the acts on it, "Show …" for a panel the catalog can draw of it, its places and its records. Words that complete nothing are asked, and the answer is a card beside the record or the list: a few sentences, the places, records and panels that act on it, and the changes it proposes as a ghost on the card, accepted all or some as one batch with its one undo. A follow-up is asked on the card and replaces its answer; it is not a thread. The context menu's "Ask about this…", Find's last row and the search page's "Ask: ‘…’" open the same card, Activity keeps each ask as an AI interaction (`ask.kind` `"ask"`, with its `answer`) and "Ask again" asks it on the card again. Where the quick ask is drawn ⌘K is its and `/` stays Find's — Pages' Find hears `/` too, and the bar says `/` over its box; where it is not, both are Find's.
  
  `quickAsk` (a boolean, on by default) on `Shell`, a routed face's context and `mount`/`<Embed>`, and `handle.setQuickAsk(on)`, say whether it is drawn; `quickAskOf(setting)` reads a setting a host kept from the Ask box — `"field"` is on, `"hidden"` is off — and the embed reads a kept string the same way. The answer is the host's: `HostAi.answer` (`{ model, answer({ said, target, targetLabel, state, catalog, follows }) → { text, pick?, usage? } }`) answers it, else the Thinking slot, else the Fast slot's pick among the candidates; `answerHere` on `@graview/tools/inline-ai` is the engine, and `stubThinking` answers it for a test. A provider drawn inside another now takes the outer one's `ai` unless given its own, so an embed's routed face draws the inline AI and the quick ask too.
  
  The conversation stays the app's (`seatTalk`, the responders, `ai.turn`): no face draws it, and `ChatPanel` is there for a product that places one in its own layout. The strip the scene kept clear at its foot for the field stays, always, as the foot's (where the zoom, the way back, a notice and a proposal stand), so the city keeps its fit; `useSeatDrawn` and the conversation's `open`, `side`, `drawn`, `setOpen` and `setSide` went with the field. The four harnesses that existed for the box (`chat`, `phone-seat`, `seat-guide`, `seat-goes-and-draws`) are retired, and `quick-ask` holds the new surface on both faces in three engines.
  
  It adds 0.1 KB more up front to a hosted page (100 bytes, 60 gzipped, about the 19 its first load grew by and a margin), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. Removed: `SeatField`, `SeatFieldProps`, `SeatStart`, `SEAT_WIDTH`, `SEAT_PHONE_BELOW`, `PageAsk`, `useSeatDrawn`, `SeatSide`, the Shell's `ask` and `chat`, `PageContext.ask`, `EmbedOptions.seat` and `EmbedHandle.setAskField`. New: `QuickAskDoor`, `openQuickAsk`, `useQuickAskDrawn`, `quickAskOf`, `QuickAskOpening` on `@graview/primitives`, `PageQuickAsk` on `@graview/pages`, `quickAsk` on `ShellProps`, `PageContext` and `EmbedOptions`, `EmbedHandle.setQuickAsk`, `HostAi.answer` with `AnswerProvider`, `AnswerRequest` and `AnswerReply`, `answerHere`, `answerOf` and `QuickAsk` on `@graview/tools/inline-ai`, `AiInteraction.answer`, and `HeldGhost.onCard`.

## 0.1.30

### Patch Changes

- 6cbecc0: A card says its kind's glance and no more, the bar names a place once, and a drawn home stays Home beside `pages.first` (the same review app). A finding card read "Critical · Security · High" — the third its confidence, though a severity can be high too — and still did after `confidence` was taken out of the kind's `glance`: the glance chose the order and the rest of the kind followed it. A glance a kind declares is now said exactly, and a choice another field shown beside it shares says its field ("Confidence: high", "Likelihood: low"), on a card, a row, a describe line and a table alike. A default card no longer draws a long or Markdown text as a chip cut after a couple of dozen characters: it says the text's start on a line of its own under the chips. With a home declared and `pages.first: "This week"`, the app opened on This week, and a press on Home from the scene drew the pages at their first entry and went to This week again, so the home the author drew was nowhere: `pages.first` now picks where a new visit lands, once, and after that `/` is the home on both faces. `graview check` says so when both are set (`pages-first-beside-home`, a note). And a kind whose plural is "root causes" beside a blocks place titled "Root causes" put two "Root causes" on the bar: the place keeps the name and stands, as `pages.first` and `pages.primary` already read the word, and the kind's list folds into More as "All root causes", still at `/root-causes`; `graview check` warns of it (`pages-place-named-as-a-list`).   Compatibility: ops, stored records and wire messages are unchanged. New check finding codes `pages-first-beside-home` (a note) and `pages-place-named-as-a-list` (a warning). `readableFields` with `glance: true` says only a kind's declared `display.glance` when it has one, and a choice's `alone` may carry its field's label. `barPlaces` labels a kind's list "All <plural>" and folds it when a place has its name (`allLabel` from `@graview/core`). `@graview/react/provider` exports `hasLanded(views)` and `markLanded(views)`, which the pages and the embed read `pages.first` once by. - 6862f3a: A calendar of years spans its records, from the first to the last. A career calendar opened on 1970-04-10 with a horizon of thirty years drew 1970–1999, and a discography's releases of 1962–1969 and 2000–2023 were nowhere. Its years now reach back to the first record and on to the last as well as across the horizon from the day it opens on — "The career · 1962–2023" — and a planning calendar whose records stop short still looks out as far as its horizon. A horizon says its ends outright with `from` and `to` (years), which win; a horizon written as a number is its years, called "Years"; and one that is not a span of years is a warning by its path (`lens-horizon`) and offers none. The graview-lens skill says the options, and `verify-declared` holds the span in a browser.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged. A calendar lens's `horizon` takes `from` and `to` beside `years` and `title`, and a number; a document's lens `options.horizon` is held to that shape by its reader. `spanOf` takes `fit` (the records' first and last years). A new warning code, `lens-horizon`.
- ed2a75d: A change is said in words. The derived edit's sentence read `Change Billing across apps: stage → "considering"` — the field's key, an arrow and the value as it is kept — and that is what a person reads in Take back and in Activity, and hears when another seat's change is said aloud. It says "Changed the stage of Billing across apps to Considering" now: each field by its label, each value as the record page says it (a choice by its option's words, money and days in the app's currency and locale, a length of time as one, a file by its name, prose by its start in quotes, a record in another app as "a linked record", a date that repeats as "to repeat every week"), and several fields in one sentence — "Changed the price of Model Y to $49,900, the condition to Certified pre-owned and the VIN to “8C9D”". A test holds it over the derived edits of every example app and every document fixture: no sentence has a key, an arrow or a value in a program's quotes.

- 6862f3a: A coverage with nothing to cross says so instead of listing its rows. "Songs by genre" — songs against genres through `inGenre` — with no genre yet drew forty songs as a plain list with no columns, which read as the answer to a question it could not yet ask. With no columns it says "No genres yet: link songs to genres.", and with columns and not one link over its relation "No song is linked to a genre yet: link songs to genres."; it draws no row, and a glance at it says "nothing linked". `describePlace` says the same words. - 648fc05: A role's grant to make a kind no longer lets it remove, archive, bring back or merge somebody else's record of that kind. The derived remove, archive, unarchive and merge acts ride the acts that make the kind, and since the ninth walk (W-201) riding a grant to everybody reached only what the seat made, but riding a role's own grant still reached every record: a member who may log an inspection could remove anybody's. Riding the making of a kind, whoever's grant it is, they now reach only the records this principal made, and a refusal says "only on what you made" and who could do the rest. A derived edit is unchanged: a role's grant on the acts that write or make a kind still reaches every record's edit. The hosted page carries 36 bytes more up front, raised by 0.1 KB more up front (and 0.1 KB more up front handed a compiled app) in `scripts/budget-raises/a-grant-to-make-a-kind-takes-out-only-what-you-made-*.json`.
  
  Compatibility: an app whose roles removed, archived or merged records other people made only through a grant on the act that makes the kind is refused those acts on others' records; name them in the grant to keep them. No API changes.

- 5b8ac1f: A lens can be kept off the lists (Roadmap f-hidden-lenses). A kind could be left off the home, but a lens could not be left off anything, so an app whose custom home links to lenses as targets ended with a long page list of them. In a document `set-lens-hidden` sets it — `{ "op": "set-lens-hidden", "title": "Building now", "hidden": true }`, with `on` where two lenses share a title — and says so ("The lens "Building now" is kept off the bar and the page list; a link or navigate still reaches it.", and "… is listed on the bar and the page list again."), as `diffDocuments` does; a lens drawn anew in a hidden one's place (`add-lens` with `replace`) stays hidden, and a lens `pages.primary` stands on the bar is refused, in a sentence, until it is taken out of `primary`. `graview check` says a `hidden` that is not true or false (`lens-hidden-not-a-boolean`, a warning), a hidden lens with no title to be a place (`lens-hidden-untitled`, a note), and `pages.primary` naming a hidden lens (`pages-primary-hidden`, a warning). New check finding codes `lens-hidden-not-a-boolean` (a warning), `lens-hidden-untitled` (a note) and `pages-primary-hidden` (a warning). `DocumentDiff`'s sentences may say "The lens "…" is kept off the bar and the page list; a link or navigate still reaches it." `LensDeclaration.hidden`, `DrawnLens.hidden`, `Place.hidden`, `ViewMeta.hidden`, `ViewRegistration.hidden` and `AppPlace.hidden` (now a lens's too) are `boolean | undefined`. - 6862f3a: A list shows more past a page, and groups by a linked record. A list now holds up to its `limit` (1,000 at most, the most it holds unsaid), draws a page of 100 and a "Show 58 more" button that draws the next page — under their headings, where it is grouped — and a list cut past what it holds still says "See all" or "and N more". `group` takes a relation as a kind's list writes a field: `"inGenre"`, or `"out('inGenre')"` and `"in('writtenBy')"` to say which way, a heading per linked record in the order of their names, a record linked to two under both, and one linked to none last, under "No genre". A document's kinds, acts, rules and roles are kebab-case (`tool-note`) and its fields and relations camelCase (`supersededBy`), and the checker said one rule at a time, so the first write met the trap twice. Now a refusal of either says both, and its fix names the spelling it wants: `kinds.toolNote` is told `Call it "tool-note"`, a field `superseded-on` `Call it "supersededOn"`, and a value naming a kind (`"creates": "toolNote"`) the same. - e46f304: A headline inside a group is a section heading, not the page's title. The home's first headline is the page's heading, and a headline at the top of a column inside a row group was the first of its own blocks, so it was said at that level too: a home with two such columns drew two more titles at page-title size, each with the app's description repeated under it. A headline in a group or a condition is said at the level of the view's sections now (on the routed face's home, an `h3` under the page's `h2`), and the description is said once, under the real title. "Change" beside it, a form and the scene's ask pick another from what the host lists.   Compatibility: no surface changes. `@graview/core/forms` exports `editStart(mutation, graph, args)`, the record's values for the fields its derived edit asks, and `changedArgs(args, start)`, what a form that started there sends. Derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged.
- 412abf0: A document's rule may be repaired by an act from the other end of its relation. A rule over findings that requires `exists(in('resolves'))` (a serious finding has an initiative) is naturally repaired by `resolve-finding`, an act on the initiative that says `fromTheOtherEnd: "Name the work that fixes it"` and which a finding's page already offers; `graview check` refused it as `repair-wrong-kind`, because the act's subject is not the kind the rule judges. An act that connects or severs a relation between the judged kind and its own subject, takes the judged kind as an argument and says how it reads from that end is now accepted, and the rule hands it the judged record as the far end (`to`), labels the repair with the far end's words and leaves the subject for the person to choose (`missing: ["id"]`), as the record page does. Without `fromTheOtherEnd`, or without such a relation, it is still `repair-wrong-kind`, and the fix now says this third way out. An `expressionRule` in TypeScript still hands every repair `{ id }`, so it is held as before.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: a document that compiled still compiles, and one refused only for `repair-wrong-kind` on such an act now compiles; a violation's repair for that act carries the far end's argument and `missing: ["id"]` where it carried `id`. Ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tool schemas are unchanged.
- 6cbecc0: A template says related records by their labels, and a count in words can start a sentence (a review app built as a document on 0.1.29). A finding's page said `{out('affects') | and}` as "module:files, module:app-code and module:tsc" while `{out('dependsOn') | and}` beside it said task titles: `and`, and a set or a record said bare, looked for a field called name, title or label and fell back to the id, so a module labeled `{path}` was said by its id. A record is now said by its kind's declared label — in a block, in a rule's sentence, and in a computed field shown as words — and by those fields only where no label is declared. And `{count(all('finding')) | words} findings` read "fifty-seven findings" at the start of a headline: the new `capital` formatter upper-cases the first letter of what was said, after another formatter or alone — `{count(all('finding')) | words | capital} findings` is "Fifty-seven findings". It is the one formatter that may follow another; two others in a row (`{n | words | money}`) are refused in a sentence rather than read as a join of sets. The graview-node-kind and graview-lists skills teach it.
