  Compatibility: ops, stored records and wire messages are unchanged. A template's formatters take `capital`, alone or after one other (`{n | words | capital}`), within `graview-document@1`; a build that predates it refuses a document that says it (`template`). `TemplatePart` may carry `capital: true`; `formatValue` takes an optional sixth argument, how a record is said, and `RenderContext` an optional `name`, which unsaid reads the labels of the schema the graph carries. A rule's sentence, a block and a describe line that listed records by id now list them by label.
- e46f304: A view is held to 40 blocks at its top, a group or a condition counting as one, and to 120 blocks in all. The cap counted every nested block, so a home of four stat tiles (a figure and a line each), four summary lines, five meters and three lists came to 56 and was refused, though a reader sees sixteen things. What a reader scans is the top of the view, and a group is one thing on the page, so that is what the 40 counts; the ceiling of 120 in all stays because every block is worked out on every draw, and a view nobody could read should still be refused. `view-size` says which of the two was passed: "has 41 blocks at its top; a view has at most 40 there, a group or a condition counting as one", or "has 121 blocks counting the ones inside its groups and conditions; a view has at most 120 in all". `MAX_VIEW_BLOCKS_IN_ALL` is exported beside `MAX_VIEW_BLOCKS`. The `graview-pages` skill says the counts.
  
  A hosted page's first load carries 1.0 KB more up front for the check and the document's new keys, raised in `scripts/budget-raises/blocks-lay-out-as-written-a-hosted-page.json`, and 0.4 KB more when it is handed a compiled app (`…-a-hosted-page-handed-a-compiled-app.json`).
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: a view that was refused for more than 40 blocks counted inside its groups now compiles when it has at most 40 at its top and 120 in all; a view of more than 120 blocks in all, which needed more than 40 at its top before, is still refused. `view-size` keeps its code with new words. Ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tool schemas are unchanged.
- 5b8ac1f: A view's `navigate` goes to a kind's list or a lens narrowed, and to a section of a page (Roadmap f-navigate-filters). It took a place's slug and nothing else, so a custom home could not open "features where stage is building" or jump to a heading, and every filtered link needed a lens of its own: the Graview Roadmap's home needed six. Now `graview.navigate({ place: "features", where: { stage: "building" }, section: "Shipping soon" })` goes to the features' list narrowed, at the heading that says "Shipping soon"; `place` is a lens's slug first, then a kind's list by its plural or its name, and a link says the same with `<a data-place="features" data-filter="stage:building" data-section="shipping-soon">`. The filter is held to what the kind can be filtered by — the offers the list's own arranging line makes — and written in the words that line already writes, so the routed face's address is `/features?filter=stage%3Abuilding#shipping-soon` and can be shared: opened from a link it lands on the list narrowed, the section scrolled to and given the keyboard. A lens is gone to narrowed the same way (`/places/<as>?filter=`, the records it is handed narrowed and said so over it, "Only those where stage is building. Show all"); a lens drawn from blocks lists what its own blocks say. On the scene a list is the kind's stop narrowed (`in.filter`, which its arranging line reads) and a lens its picture narrowed; a stop is a picture, not a page of headings, so the scene leaves a section unsought. A field the kind does not have, a choice it does not offer, a filter on one record or a section that names nothing goes nowhere and says why to the view's console and the host's problems ("The filter on "features" goes nowhere: Features have no field "stag" to filter by; they filter by name, stage, area, summary, became, became.status and is."), as a place that is not there already did; a headless run says it too. The graview-worker-view skill teaches it, and `verify-growing` holds it in a browser.
  
  A hosted page's first load carries 0.2 KB more up front for it and for keeping a lens off the lists, raised in `scripts/budget-raises/navigate-filters-and-hidden-lenses-a-hosted-page.json` and `…-a-hosted-page-handed-a-compiled-app.json`; the embed's pages face, the embed with the studio handed in, a worker view's runtime and the guest host's four budgets (the links that judge a navigate, fetched with the first one where a frame is drawn) are raised in their own files beside them.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and the declaration are unchanged. The guest wire takes more on its existing `navigate` message: `{ type: "navigate", nonce, place, where?, section? }` and `{ type: "navigate", nonce, to, section? }`, where `where` is `Record<string, string | number | boolean>` (`GuestWhere`); a host that predates them ignores them and goes unnarrowed. `graview.navigate` and a frame guest's `navigate` take `GuestNavigateTo`: a string, `{ record, section? }`, or `{ place, where?, section? }` (`navigateRequest` from `@graview/guest` spells the message; `GuestNavigateTo` and `GuestWhere` are its types). `GoTo` (`useGoTo`) is now `record(id, also?)`, `place(as, also?)` and a new `list(kind, also?)`, `also` a `GoToAlso`: `{ filter?: string, section?: string }`, the filter in the address's words; a face that provides `GoToContext` writes `list` too. `mountWorkerView`'s `onNavigate` hears an `Arrival` — `{ record, section? }`, `{ place, filter?, section? }` or `{ list, filter?, section? }` — and it and `mountGuestView` take `goTo` (the face's `GoTo`) in its place; `createGuestHost` takes `onArrive` and `onLost`. New subpath `@graview/core/navigate`: `narrowedPath(path, { filter?, section? })` and `sectionSlug(words)`. `PagesApp`'s `onNavigate` path ends with the section it went to (`#shipping-soon`), never a scene's fragment.
- f7c1a9e: A record named in Activity goes to that record when pressed, and nothing else there looks pressable. The records under each change and each call were chips carrying a pick target that only the scene's own pointer answered, so on Pages, and from the keyboard on either face, they looked pressable and did nothing. A record still in the graph is now a button that goes to its page on Pages, or focuses and chooses it in the scene, and closes Activity as it goes. A record the change removed is named from the removal, where it used to show its id ("i2"), and is a plain label, as is an argument that is a word rather than a record. A design that replaces the routed face's shell under the bar (todo's) no longer shows two "Activity" buttons once there is a change: the derived bar drew Activity in its tools and also handed its own slot to the design's face controls, which drew it there too. It is now drawn once, in the slot.
  
  The every-face and studio budgets are raised by 1_169 / 470 bytes in their files.
  
  A change's preview no longer says a text field turned to Markdown "is now said as in undefined — 12 reads 12". `diffDocuments` said the number-format sentence for any change of format, and `formatWords` took `markdown` for a number's unit; the preview now says only that the field is written in Markdown, and adding a Markdown text field says "(text, written in Markdown)".
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. Activity's records carry `data-testid="activity-record"` and `data-record`, not `data-graview-pick`.
- 1a1f996: An act costs what it touches in the store too. The graph diffed only what a change named, and the store around it diffed the whole graph again after every act, undo, host-made op and received op, so seeding a team of 1,700 records and 15,000 links through its own acts took three minutes and a quarter. The store's diff is of the records and links the primitives name, and says what the whole comparison said; the same seed takes 37 seconds. With the tenth walk's other fixes the hosted page carries 0.2 KB more up front (and 0.2 KB more up front handed a compiled app).
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 6770f65: `graview create` in a directory under a pnpm workspace that does not list it makes the app a workspace of its own and installs it. pnpm installs the nearest workspace going up, so the install said "Done" having installed the workspace above, and the new app had no `node_modules`: the skills were "not written" and every import was a type error. Where the install leaves nothing in the app, create writes a `pnpm-workspace.yaml` there, says why, and installs again; a workspace that lists the app installs it as before.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- cf85240: An app can name one standard emoji as its page icon and, where it has no logo, its mark. A brand's icon was an SVG or a path, so an app somebody made in a chat had a blank tab and an empty corner of the bar until somebody drew it a picture. `brand.favicon` now also takes `{ emoji: "🏡" }`, in a TypeScript `Brand` and in a document alike: exactly one emoji as the reader sees it (Intl.Segmenter), and one of the standard emoji every emoji set draws (`RGI_Emoji`), so a skin tone, a joined emoji and a flag — 🏡, 👩🏽‍🌾, 🇺🇸 — are each one, and two emoji, letters or nothing are refused in words: `"🏡🏡" cannot be the app's icon, because it is 2 emoji, and a mark is one` (`brand-emoji`, at `brand.favicon.emoji`), from `compileDocument`, `graview check` on a declared app, and `editDocument`. `set-brand` takes `favicon: { emoji }` and says "The app's page icon and mark become 🏡." (or "The app's page icon becomes 🏡." when the app has a logo), and the preview of a changed document says the same sentence. Where the brand has no logo, `AppMark` draws the emoji as text on the app bar of both faces and in an embed, and the routed face's static pages put it in the masthead; with a logo, the logo is the mark and the emoji is only the icon. `faviconHref` makes the icon an SVG with the emoji as text, so a self-hosted app's tab shows it in the reader's own emoji font; a host that wants it alike on every system renders it from a bundled emoji set into icon PNGs and serves those instead, as Graview Cloud does. New from `@graview/core`: `emojiProblem`, `emojiMark`, `emojiOf`, `emojiSvg` and the `FaviconSpec` type. The graview-brand skill says it in two lines. The every-face and studio-handed-in budgets are raised by 2_385 / 795 and 2_409 / 920 bytes (scripts/budget-raises/an-emoji-as-the-apps-mark-every-face.json, scripts/budget-raises/an-emoji-as-the-apps-mark-studio.json); a hosted page's first load stays within its budget.
  
  Compatibility: the document format's `brand.favicon` gains a second form, `{ "emoji": "<one standard emoji>" }`, within `graview-document@1`; a document with a string `favicon` compiles to the same app. The TypeScript `Brand.favicon` is now `FaviconSpec` (`string | { emoji: string }`), so code reading it as a string narrows first. `set-brand`'s `favicon` takes the same object. Check finding codes gain `brand-emoji`. Ops, stored formats, the wire and tool names are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: render `brand.favicon.emoji` from its bundled emoji set into the icon PNGs it serves, and serve those rather than the framework's text SVG.
- 56dbbe3: Find reaches the past when nothing current answers. A search kept to the records on the horizon unless it said `is:any`, so a rescue team looking for last March's callout by its number found nothing and was told "add is:any for the past". Where the words find no current record, the past records they find are the answer, each marked past, and `searched.past` says the search looked there; where a current record answers, or the words say `is:`, the search keeps to what it was asked.
  
  Compatibility: `search` (and every face's Find, `/search` and `search_graph`) answers a query that matches only past records with those records, marked `current: false`, where it answered with nothing.
- 412abf0: The graview command line is one line from PATH in a framework checkout. On a machine with the checkout and no published install there was no `graview` command, and every use was `node <checkout>/packages/graview/dist/cli.js`. The checkout's `pnpm graview:link` is `pnpm link --global` for `packages/graview` (after `pnpm setup` once, where pnpm has no global bin folder), written in the README's commands and AGENTS.md's; and `graview doctor`, run from a checkout with no `graview` on PATH, says so with that line and the full path to use until then — even in a folder with no package.json, which is where a document-only app lives.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. `graview doctor` may print one more note; its exit code is unchanged.
- 91716ae: The past and the app's own actor's suggestions leave the store for entries of their own, so a hosted page carries neither before somebody looks back or something waits. Each of 0.1.30's changes fit the hosted page's budget alone, and together they were 10.0 KB over it. `store.at(when, options)` and `store.seqAt(when)` are now `pastOf(store, when, options)` and `seqAt(store, when)` on `@graview/core/past`, beside `pastAt(store, when, host)` and `declarationAt`, which leave `@graview/core/history` for it; the provider fetches the entry the first time somebody asks to see the past, and the timeline's helpers stay on `/history`. `store.suggest`, `store.suggestions`, `store.acceptSuggestion`, `store.declineSuggestion` and `store.declined` are now `suggest(store, calls, options)`, `suggestions(store, principal?)`, `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options?)`, `declineSuggestion(store, id, options?)` and `declined(store, calls)` on `@graview/core/tidying`; the bar fetches the Suggested tray, and with it what reads which suggestions still wait, once the log holds a suggestion. Asked of a seat's view (`seenBy`), each reads the whole store and answers as that seat, as the methods did. What they do is unchanged.
  
  That took 3.7 KB (1.2 KB gzipped) off what the hosted page loads first. What stays up front is what the page draws or judges with before anything is asked — the store's holding of the app's own actor to what people said, its refusal of a change to the past and the seam every face reads the moment from, a relation to another app compiled as its field and walked by the rule language, an emoji drawn as the app's mark, and the first look's door — and the hosted page's budget is raised by 7.4 KB (2.5 KB gzipped) up front to 544.9 KB, and the page handed a compiled app's by 5.3 KB (1.4 KB gzipped) to 501.3 KB (`scripts/budget-raises/the-0-1-30-wave-*.json`); every face's budget is raised by the 1.8 KB the changes measure together over their own raises.
  
  Compatibility: `Store.at` and `Store.seqAt` are removed for `pastOf(store, when: number | string | Date, options?: { declaration?: PastDeclaration }): Store` and `seqAt(store, when: string | Date): number` on the new `@graview/core/past`, which also takes `pastAt(store, when, host?: HistoryHost): Store` and `declarationAt(marks, seq)` from `@graview/core/history`; `Store.pastEvery` reads `StoreOptions.pastEvery`. `Store.suggest`, `Store.suggestions`, `Store.acceptSuggestion`, `Store.declineSuggestion` and `Store.declined` are removed for `suggest(store, calls, options: SuggestOptions): Operation | undefined`, `suggestions(store, principal?): readonly Suggestion[]`, `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options?: ApplyOptions): ApplyResult`, `declineSuggestion(store, id, options?: ApplyOptions): ApplyResult` and `declined(store, calls): Operation | undefined` on `@graview/core/tidying`, which now exports `SuggestOptions` and `Suggestion` too (`Suggestion` stays a type of `@graview/core`; `SuggestOptions` leaves it). Ops, the wire and stored formats are unchanged.
- c70440c: A person can scrub back through an app's history and watch it being built (Roadmap f-playback). `pastOf(store, seq)` and `pastOf(store, time)`, on the new `@graview/core/past`, are the app as it stood then — its records, its links, its log and its problems — read, never changed: it offers no act, declared or derived, `permits` says "Nothing changes while you look back.", and every change asked of it is refused with a `PastError`. A seek folds from the nearest point it can, an epoch or a point kept in memory for that store every `pastEvery` ops (64) as an earlier seek passed it, so scrubbing never folds the whole log twice; a store reopened with its log but not the epoch its seed began (a page's browser adapter keeps none) works back from the graph it holds with each op's inverse, so the past begins at the seed rather than at nothing. `pastOf` asked of a seat's view of a store answers under that seat's sight, so a seat sees the past only as it could see it then. `seqAt(store, time)` says where a time falls, and `pastAt(store, when, host)` is the past under the declaration a host says the moment was made under (`declarationAt`). The past is an entry of its own, apart from the store, because a page needs none of it until somebody looks back: the provider fetches it then. `@graview/core/history`, fetched with the timeline, holds what the timeline reads around it: `ticksOf(store, { me, declarations })` is a tick per gesture marked by who made it (`you`, `person`, `agent`, `app`), with a bigger tick where the declaration changed and the sentence it said, from an epoch's `change` or a host's `DeclarationMark`; `declarationSentence` ("Added a Budget kind"), `makerOf`, `makerWords` and `restoreBatches`.
  
  On both faces, every row of Activity has "Show the app as it was here": the face draws the app as it stood just after that change, from the one store every surface reads, with a timeline over the scene and at the head of the routed face's page — "As of" the moment, what was done there and by whom, a tick per change, Play at 1× to 8×, and Back to now. While the past is shown nothing is offered to change it: no act, no undo on Activity, no ask field, no way back after an act, and a design's own control that asks `permits` is disabled. "Restore to here" takes back every change since as one undo, itself undone as one, offered only when this seat may make every one of those changes and the log lets them go together. An undo whose target also came after the earliest op being taken back no longer blocks it, since the two leave the graph as if neither was made.
  
  What the log does not hold is the host's: `GraviewProvider`, `mount` and `PageContext` take `history: { declarations, viewAt }`, the declaration changes it knows (each with the declaration the past is drawn under from its seq) and `viewAt(name, seq)`, a script view's code at a moment, `null` where the view did not exist then. `HostFiles.kept(href)` says whether the host still keeps a file's bytes, and one it has let go of is drawn as "removed later", as is a picture that does not load while somebody looks back. `verify playback` drives it on the todo example in three engines. Up front, the hosted page carries only the seam the faces read the moment from and a store's refusal of a change to the past; the fold, its kept points and the timeline are fetched when somebody first looks back (what the 0.1.30 wave adds up front together is said in `.changeset/the-0-1-30-wave-leaves-the-past-and-the-suggestions-off-the-first-load.md`). The every-face, pages-face, studio and registering-a-worker-view budgets grow by 13_243 / 5_442, 4_630 / 1_928, 5_790 / 2_173 and 482 / 154 bytes in their raise files.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives fold as before (`OperationLog.fold` lands each op through the new `foldOp`, unchanged); `checkUndo` no longer counts as blocking a live undo whose target is itself after the earliest op being undone, so an undo that was refused with "Include batch …" over such a pair now runs, and nothing it was allowed before is refused. New API: the `@graview/core/past` entry — `pastOf(store, when: number | string | Date, options?: { declaration?: PastDeclaration }): Store`, `pastAt(store, when, host?: HistoryHost): Store`, `seqAt(store, when: string | Date): number` and `declarationAt(marks, seq)` — and `Store.past`, `Store.pastEvery`, `StoreOptions.pastEvery`, `PastError`, `PastPoint`, `PastDeclaration`, the `@graview/core/history` entry (`ticksOf`, `declarationSentence`, `makerOf`, `makerWords`, `restoreBatches`), `HostFiles.kept`, `history` on `GraviewProvider`, `FrameOptions` and `PageContext`, `usePlayback`, `useLiveStore`, `useOwnPlayback`, `PlaybackContext`, `PastBar` and `prefetchTimeline`.
- c3564eb: The app's own actor suggests what it is not sure of, is held to what people said, and its run of changes is one row of Activity. A background actor that links and prunes records on its own ("Graview, tidying") could apply a change with its lineage (FR-172) and nothing else: what it was unsure of had nowhere to wait, a person could not say "leave this one alone", an app could not say "never auto-link Vendors", and a pass of thirty links buried a person's own changes in Activity. An author may now say `automated: true` (a `system` or `agent` author: the app's own actor; `isAutomated`).
  
  A suggestion that waits is `suggest(store, calls, { author, lineage, via?, intent? })`, on `@graview/core/tidying`: the calls are judged as `applyAll` would judge them for that author, on a rehearsal, and none of it is applied — the log keeps one op that `suggests` them, with no primitives and its lineage, so it persists, travels and is withheld from a seat exactly as any op is. The same suggestion asked again answers the one that waits, and one that would change nothing is not suggested. `suggestions(store, principal?)` lists what waits, under the seat's sight (or the seat whose view `store` is); `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options)` applies the calls as the person, carrying the suggestion's lineage with `lineage.suggestion` naming it; `declineSuggestion(store, id, options)` is an undo of the suggestion, which keeps its lineage as `undoneLineage`. `declined(store, calls)` answers the op that declined these calls, or any one of them — a declined suggestion, or an actor's confident change a person took back — and the store refuses the app's own actor the same change or suggestion again, in a sentence ("Graview, tidying may not make this again: a person declined it on 2026-10-14."). The log is where suggestions live, rather than a seam a host fills, because accepting and declining are then a `call` and an `undo` like any other, and a host persists nothing new. Both faces show what waits in a quiet Suggested tray beside Activity on the bar — what it would do, who suggested it, its basis and how sure, Accept and Decline — fetched, with what reads which suggestions wait, only once the log holds one, so a hosted page carries up front only what holds the actor to what people said (what the 0.1.30 wave adds up front together is said in `.changeset/the-0-1-30-wave-leaves-the-past-and-the-suggestions-off-the-first-load.md`).
  
  Leave this one alone: every kind is given `leave-alone-<kind>` ("Leave this one alone") and `allow-tidying-<kind>` ("Allow tidying again"), ordinary undoable acts that set and clear `$leftAlone` on the record as archive sets `$archived`, offered on the record's page and the scene's record card beside archive, and said at the head of the record's page and in its card. `isLeftAlone(store, id)` reads it. The store refuses the app's own actor any change that touches a record left alone — a patch, a removal, a link at either end — and a suggestion about one, naming the record. A declaration says what is never tidied: `tidying: "never" | "no-links"` on `defineNode` (a document's kind) and `tidying: "never"` on a relation; the actor is refused a change to a kind never tidied, a link to or from a kind never linked, and a link of a relation never tidied, each in a sentence, and may still suggest them for a person to decide. The document edit `set-tidying` (`{ kind, relation?, tidying: "never" | "no-links" | "allowed" }`) says it in a sentence, `diffDocuments` says the same sentence, and `graview check` refuses a grant naming a leave-alone act of a kind never tidied (`grant-act`) and warns of a relation that says what its kind already says (`tidying-redundant`).
  
  A digest in Activity: consecutive batches by one automated author fold into one row, "Graview, tidying Tidied 6 things today", which opens to each change with its own lineage and its own undo; a person's change and a chat seat's never fold, and a suggestion and its declining are the tray's, not rows. The agent's tools gain `list_suggestions` (the actor's basis handed over as somebody else's words), `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion`. `@graview/core/tidying` makes, settles and reads it all for a host and a face: `suggest`, `suggestions`, `acceptSuggestion`, `declineSuggestion`, `declined`, `suggestionsIn`, `declinesIn`, `declinedIn`, `suggestionKey`, `tidyingOf`, `tidyingRefusal`, `foldDigests`, `digestWords`, `isSuggestionBatch` and `LEFT_ALONE`. The embed's budgets for the pages face alone, every face and the studio handed in are raised by what they grew (`scripts/budget-raises/the-apps-own-actor-*.json`). The graview-lists skill teaches it.
  
  Compatibility: ops gain two optional fields, `suggests` (on an op with no primitives, which folds to nothing) and, on a lineage, `suggestion`; authors gain the optional `automated`; a record may carry `$leftAlone: true`, written by an ordinary `patch-node` — additive within op format 1, and a log folds to the same graph on an earlier version. The wire is unchanged: accepting is a `call` whose lineage names the suggestion, declining an `undo`. The document format gains the optional `kinds.<kind>.tidying` and `kinds.<kind>.edges.<relation>.tidying`, the edit `set-tidying`, and the check finding `tidying-redundant` (a warning); `grant-act` also names a leave-alone act of a kind never tidied. Derived tools: every kind gains `leave-alone-<kind>` and `allow-tidying-<kind>` (`{ id }`) unless it is never tidied, and the agent's surface gains `list_suggestions`, `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion` (`{ suggestion }`). New APIs: `isLeftAlone`, `isAutomated` and the type `Suggestion` on `@graview/core`, and `@graview/core/tidying` with `suggest(store, calls, options: SuggestOptions): Operation | undefined`, `suggestions(store, principal?): readonly Suggestion[]`, `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options?): ApplyResult`, `declineSuggestion(store, id, options?): ApplyResult`, `declined(store, calls): Operation | undefined` and `SuggestOptions`. Stored formats are unchanged.
- 412abf0: The document's JSON Schema types its lenses and its views' blocks. `lenses` items were any object and a view's blocks any value, so an editor reading `document.schema.json` offered nothing for a lens's name, its bindings, `options.blocks` or a block's keys, and a misspelling surfaced only at `graview check`. Each lens is now held to a name `SHIPPED_LENSES` lists (`timeline`, `calendar`, `coverage`, `board`, `plan`, `reach`, `blocks`, `columns`), the options that lens takes, its bindings as roles bound to fields where it binds fields, and the keys a lens declaration has; every block — in a kind's card, row and page, the home view and a blocks lens's `options.blocks` — is held to the closed set `BLOCK_KEYS` names, each block with what it may say besides and the values each takes (tones, formats, a list's sort and grouping, a group's direction). Both are generated where the build writes the schema, from the tables the checker judges by, and a block or a block key added there without a shape stops the build. Every example document and fixture in the tree validates.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: the compiler reads documents as before. `document.schema.json` is stricter than it was on lenses and blocks, so an editor now underlines a lens Graview does not ship, an option or key a lens does not take, and a block or block key the checker refuses, where the compiler warns (`lens-not-shipped`, `lens-option-unknown`) or refuses (`view-block`, `unknown-key`). Ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tool schemas are unchanged.
- c3e789b: The record page `graview create` writes offers every act the derivation offers, and the record's name is changed where it is read. It offered only the acts that tie two records, so "Change the callout", "Close it" and every act of a record's own were on no page of the routed face. The name is an `EditableValue` under the face's provider, every affordance but a repair is offered (a repair stays beside its rule), and what takes the record away comes last.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; a project made before keeps the page it was given, and can take these lines from a new scaffold.

## 0.1.29

### Patch Changes

- 523149b: A change costs what it touches, not what the graph holds (the ninth walk, W-197). Every change diffed the whole graph before and after, reading every field of every record through JSON — about 30 milliseconds an act on a beekeeping co-op of 8,500 records and 16,000 links, so importing 200 inspections took 9.8 seconds and `store.append` of 100 records 3.1. `Graph.applyPrimitives` now diffs only the records and links its primitives name, with the links a removed record takes, and `diffSnapshots` does not compare a record held unchanged: the 200-row import takes 240 milliseconds and the 100 appends 114.
  
  Compatibility: the diff a change emits and returns is the same as before, in the same order; nothing stored or sent changes.
- 7966f5d: A change says what it was based on, and a link made less than sure is drawn as such until a person keeps it (FR-172). An author said who moved and nothing said why one change was made or how sure its maker was, so a background actor that links and prunes on its own ("Graview, tidying") could not show its reasons or be told apart from a person's work. An op may now carry `lineage: { basis, confidence?, model? }` — given as `ApplyOptions.lineage` to `apply`, `applyAll`, `applyPrimitives` and `undo`, or on an `AppendOp` — checked before anything is kept (a basis of 1–500 characters, a confidence from 0 to 1, a model of 1–120) and kept in the log, on the batch and on the wire. On the op rather than its author, because an author is the same for every change a seat makes and a basis is this change's own. It is said where the change is: "based on “both name 12 Harbor Street” · 60% sure · jev-1" on a record page's history line and in the scene's recent changes, and a record page says what the record was made on. A link whose making op was less than sure, by anyone but a person, is provisional: the scene draws it in short dashes and quieter, its basis on hover and in its acts when it is pressed or long-pressed, and a record page marks it "unsure", the mark saying the basis and offering "Keep this link". Keeping it is `keep-link` (`KEEP_LINK`), an act every store answers to and no list offers, which takes the link away and puts it back as the person's op; any person's op that takes the link away does too, and undoing the keep makes it provisional again. An undo of a change with a lineage keeps it as `undoneLineage`, so a host can read "do not make this again" — a merge (FR-173) as much as a link. `@graview/core/provenance` is the one answer every face and host reads — `linkProvenance(store, edge)`, `isProvisional`, `provisionalLinks`, `provenanceWords` — fetched with what draws a link, so the hosted page's first load stays under its budget. Nothing in the framework decides what to link.
  
  Compatibility: ops gain two optional fields, `lineage` and `undoneLineage`, additive within op format 1: an op without them reads and folds as before, and a build that predates them keeps them on an op it receives. `Batch` gains an optional `lineage`. The wire's `call` and `undo`, on the socket and over `POST /graview/ops`, may carry `lineage`; a change whose lineage is not one is refused as `invalid`, and a server that predates it ignores the field. Every store answers to `keep-link` (`{ kind, from, to }`), which no list of acts — `allMutations`, `permittedMutations`, an agent's tools — offers; an app that declares its own `keep-link` keeps it. Stored formats, the document format, check finding codes and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-172. What a host does: a background actor passes `lineage` with each change it makes, reads `linkProvenance` or `provisionalLinks` for what a person has not yet kept, and reads an undo's `undoneLineage` for what a person declined.
- fcf84de: A computed field is said as a length of time because its parsed expression works one out, not because its words looked like it. The face read the expression's text — split at its `+` and `-`, matched against patterns — so `budget - sum(in('sessions'), length)` was said "7 h 45 min" when the sessions point the other way, and a guess about a TypeScript declaration's words decided what a reader was shown. A document's compile now reads each kind's computed fields from their parse, where the parser already is (`durationsOf`, `document/durations.ts`): a duration field of its kind, another computed field that is one, a field across a relation or a sum, least or most of one over `out(...)` or `in(...)` whose far kinds hold a duration in it, two of these added or taken apart, or one negated. The compiled app names them (`KindPlan.durations`), the built definition says `as: "duration"` on each, and a face reads `as` and nothing of the expression — no parser on a page, and the kinds-beside-it index the guess needed is gone from `@graview/core`. A TypeScript declaration has no compile step to read, so it says `as: "duration"` itself; one that does not is said as a number. A definition written back as a document (`toDocument`) leaves `as` out.
  
  Compatibility: `graview-compiled@1` gains an optional `durations` (computed field names) on a kind's plan; a plan without it builds as before and says those fields as numbers, and a build that predates it ignores it. A TypeScript declaration's computed sum of durations without `as: "duration"` is now said as a number. The document format, check finding codes, ops, stored formats, the wire and tool schemas are unchanged.
- a43f829: A count is said in the page's locale, and the map's district panel counts what its head counts (the ninth walk, W-198). `counted` — the sentence every face counts a kind with — wrote the number bare, so a beekeeping co-op's home said "7540 inspections" and its district panel "7347 inspections → 299 hives"; it now says the figure as FR-178 says every other ("7,540 inspections", "7.540" in de-DE). The panel's link counts are said the same way, and its relations are counted over the records its head counts — "280 hives → 14 apiaries" under "280 hives", where it said 299 by counting the dead-outs past the horizon.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and wire messages are unchanged. The words a person reads change: a count of a thousand or more is said with the locale's separators wherever `counted` is used (the home, a list's head, the map, a static page, the seat's lines), and the district panel's relations leave out records past the horizon unless the view shows the past.
- 55ec52b: A date can repeat: a weekly practice, a monthly bill or a yearly renewal is one record, not one per occurrence made by hand (the Roadmap's f-recurring-dates). A document says `"repeats": true` on a `date` or `datetime` field; a declaration puts `startsAtRepeat: repeatField()` beside `startsAt`, and `toDocument` writes it as `repeats`. A record keeps its first occurrence in the date and, beside it under the date's name and `Repeat`, how it repeats: `{ "every": "week", "on": ["tue"], "until": "2026-12-15" }` — `every` day, week, month or year, an `interval`, `on` weekdays for a week or days of the month for a month (`-1` the last), `until`, `count` and `except` days — or `null` once it no longer does. The store refuses anything else (`validRepeat`). Occurrences are worked out and never stored (`occurrences`, `nextOccurrence`, `dateOn`): a rule, a template, `today()`'s comparisons, a list's sort, filter and date groups, and a kind's stop read the next occurrence on the day they are judged on, so the tick that turns the day (FR-176) moves it with nothing written. A day a month lacks falls on the month's last (Jan 31 monthly is Feb 28, Feb 29 yearly is Feb 28 off a leap year), a moment's time of day is kept as written so six in the evening stays six across a clock change, and `count` counts skipped days. The calendar draws every occurrence in the days it shows, marked ↻ and "· repeats" and not dragged, and counts a series once, at its next, where it is a glance; a kind's stop opens on a calendar by a series' next occurrence and counts it in "in the next 7 days"; a record's facts, a row and a card say "Every Tuesday, next Oct 27" in the app's locale (`repeatAsRead`, `repeatingDateAsRead` on `@graview/core/words`). Three derived acts on a kind with a date that repeats, permitted as its edit is, each one undo with a sentence: `skip-<kind>` ("Skip this one", `{ id, on }`) adds the day to `except`; `detach-<kind>` ("Change just this one") makes that occurrence a record of its own, with the series' fields, the links it makes and what was changed, and an exception on the series; `split-<kind>` ("Change from here") ends the series the day before and starts a new one that day, `continues` naming the old. A record's page has a "Repeat…" control beside the date: a picker (does not repeat, daily, weekly, monthly, yearly; every how many; which weekdays; the month's day or its last; until) that says the rule back in words as it is chosen, every control labeled, kept by the edit act; a form asks for a repeat with the same picker (`FormField` gains `control: "repeat"`). `editDocument` gains `set-repeats`, the studio offers "Say whether it repeats" and writes `repeatField()` back, a migration moves a repeat with its renamed date, `graview types` writes `<date>Repeat?: Repeat | null`, and an act's tool schema describes it with `"x-graview-repeat": true`. Time zones beyond the one a moment says, and the rest of RFC 5545 (by-week-number, by-set-position, hourly), are left out. A hosted page's first load is 5 797 bytes larger minified, 2 381 gzipped, than the batch it joined (549 531 / 192 258 against 543 734 / 189 877, under its budgets of 550 912 / 192 512), and 5 425 / 2 274 handed a compiled app (507 320 / 177 411 against 509 440 / 177 664): the occurrence math, the repeat's check and the three acts, which a rule and the store need as the page loads; the words, the picker and the calendar's drawing are fetched with what draws them. The embed's every face, the studio handed in and the pages face alone are raised by files of their own. A document's `datetime` field now reads back as a date and time (`fieldSpecOf`), so a kind's stop finds it for its calendar and its dates, and the stop says its date by the field's label ("in the next 7 days by starts"). verify-repeats holds it at desk width in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The declaration and check finding codes: the document format gains `repeats` on a date or datetime field, within `graview-document@1` and `graview-compiled@1`, the edit `set-repeats` and the finding `repeat-clash`; a build that predates them refuses the key as `unknown-key`. Derived tool names and input schemas: a kind with a date that repeats gains `skip-<kind>`, `detach-<kind>` and `split-<kind>`, and its `edit-<kind>` takes `<date>Repeat`; a kind with none is unchanged. `ReadableField` gains `repeats`, `PlacedEntry` gains `repeats`, `DerivedAct` gains `skip`, `detach` and `split`. Ops and primitives, the stored formats and the wire are unchanged. What a host does: serve 0.1.29 or later before writing `repeats`; a record's repeat travels as any other field value.
- dbe0a28: A document can say a kind is never archived (FR-173). `defineNode({ archives: false })` gave a TypeScript kind no `archive-`, `unarchive-` or `merge-<kind>`, and a document had no way to say it, so a hosted app offered all three on every kind — a checklist's steps, an app's settings — whether setting one aside meant anything or not. `"archives": false` on a kind now says it: the compiled app gives that kind its edit and remove and none of the three, `toDocument` writes it from a declaration that says it, `describeApp` says "Steps are kept or removed, never archived or merged", and a grant naming one of the three on that kind is `grant-act`. `editDocument` gains `set-archives` (`{ kind, archives }`), which says what the app stops or starts offering — "Categories are kept or removed, never archived: the app no longer offers archive-category, unarchive-category or merge-category, …" — says the agent's tools moved, and is refused as `grant-unknown-mutation` while a grant names one of the three, naming the `remove-grant` to send first; the preview of a changed document says the same sentence. The studio holds it on a kind as "never archived", hands it back as `set-archives`, and writes it into the declaration it writes back. A record of the kind already archived is not left where nothing can bring it back: `planMigration` brings each back in the same op that lands the change and says so ("2 archived category records brought back, since category is now kept or removed, never archived.", `counts.broughtBack`), and `set-archives` says it will. Every other migration now keeps an archived record archived — it cleared the mark on every record it planned, and said nothing. The grant check is the checker's (`compileDocument`), not the page's: a hosted page's first load is 20 bytes gzipped more for all three of this wave's follow-ups, and the every-face and studio-handed-in budgets are raised by 2_431 / 760 and 2_431 / 740 bytes (scripts/budget-raises/what-the-0-1-29-wave-left-every-face.json, scripts/budget-raises/what-the-0-1-29-wave-left-studio.json).
  
  Compatibility: the document format gains an optional `kinds.<kind>.archives` (only `false`) within `graview-document@1`; a document without it compiles to the same app. The edit vocabulary gains `set-archives`. Derived tools change only for a kind that says it: it has no `archive-`, `unarchive-` or `merge-<kind>`. `MigrationPlan.counts` gains `broughtBack`, and a migration keeps `$archived` on a record (it cleared it before). Check finding codes are unchanged (`grant-act` and `grant-unknown-mutation` read one more case).
- 3f607a8: A door for everybody is not a key to every record (the ninth walk, W-201). A kind's derived edit, remove, archive, bring-back and merge ride the declared acts that make or write it — and a beekeeping co-op's "Anybody may join" (`roles: "*"` on the act that makes a member) let a novice remove her mentor from the co-op, and handed somebody browsing the tools to change, archive or merge any member they could see. Riding only a grant to every role, a derived act now reaches the records that seat made, as the store's log says who made each; a role's own grant on the acts it rides, or a grant that names the act or says `*`, reaches the rest as before. Refused, it says so: "Not permitted: “Remove the beekeeper” on a beekeeper — only on what you made; a coordinator can." The graview-permissions skill says it.
  
  Compatibility: derived tools are unchanged. Narrowed: a seat whose only way to a kind's derived `edit-`, `remove-`, `archive-`, `unarchive-` or `merge-<kind>` is a grant with `roles: "*"` naming the declared acts that make (or write) the kind is now refused it, as `forbidden`, on a record it did not make (`store.permits`, `apply`, what a face offers and an agent's call), where it reached every record it could see. Unchanged: a grant of everything to everybody (`{ roles: "*", mutations: "*" }`, which `add-sight` writes on a document with no policy) and any grant naming the derived act or saying `*` reach every record; a role's own grant on the acts it rides reaches every record of the kind. A store with no policy permits everything, as before. `permits` from `@graview/core` takes an optional ninth argument, who made the record. docs/stability.md says it under derived tools.
- 42dbfb7: A guest view is handed the pictures on the records it reads, as addresses the host made from the bytes (FR-175). A record keeps a file as a path on the host (FR-162's `FileValue`), which the host serves to the app's members with their credentials; a worker view and a frame run at an opaque origin, so a view of a job's photographs was handed paths it could not load, and learned the host's paths besides. A worker view whose manifest says `pictures: true`, or `pictures: { width: 400 }` (the widest it draws a picture, in CSS pixels; 1 200 by default), and a frame whose host passes `pictures` to `mountGuestView` or `guestView`, is now handed each file on a record as a `GuestFile` in the record's own field, never the path: `{ name, type, bytes, width?, height?, caption?, src?, left? }`. A picture (`image/*`, not SVG) carries `src`, which the host made from the bytes it fetched on its own origin as the reader (`credentials: "same-origin"`): a `blob:` URL of the page for a worker view (a `data:` image where the page's policy refuses `blob:` images), a `data:` image for a frame, as the brand's logo is (FR-127). It comes without `src` on the first push and the view is pushed again once the host has made it. A document, and an SVG, is its name, type and size alone, and is never fetched. Only files on records the guest is handed are fetched, so a file on a record the seat may not see is never asked for. A picture wider than the view draws one (`width` × the screen's pixel ratio, at most 2) is made smaller on a canvas, kept in its own type where the canvas writes it; one picture is at most `limits.pictureBytes` as handed (1 500 000 by default) and a view's pictures together at most `limits.picturesBytes` (12 000 000); a picture left out says why in `left`, naming the view — "The picture "porch.png" on "Porch repair" is left out: the view "Job photos" is handed at most 12,000,000 bytes of pictures, and the pictures before it took them." — and a worker view's console and `onProblem` hear it once. Pictures past the view's budget are not fetched. A frame that asks is answered once the pictures' host has arrived, and a worker view starts once it has; the pictures' host is a chunk of its own, fetched only for a guest that asks. `checkManifest` says what is wrong with a manifest's `pictures`. Headless (`graview view check`, `runWorkerViewHeadless`), a view that asks is handed each file without its path and without `src`. The graview-worker-view skill says in one line how a view shows a record's picture. `guest-sandbox --transport=pictures` draws a worker view of a crew's jobs as a member on one of them, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: the pictures are `blob:` URLs of the page made from bytes fetched once each with the reader's session, the step's byte for byte and the porch's, 1 600 pixels across, made 400; the PDF and the SVG are said by name, type and size; nothing the view drew names a path on the host; and the roof's photograph, on a job the member is not on, is never requested. A hosted page's first load is 391 bytes larger (587 597 bytes over the vendors document, 587 206 on main; 546 594 handed a compiled app, 546 203 on main), the frame's wait for the pictures' host; the guest host before a worker is drawn is 400 bytes more, registering a worker view 547 more, and a worker view on the open kit 5 353 more, the pictures' host itself, each raised by a file of its own in `scripts/budget-raises/`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-175.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names are unchanged. The guest protocol is additive and `GUEST_PROTOCOL` stays 1: no message is new, and a guest that does not ask for pictures is handed each file as the record keeps it, as before; one that asks is handed a `GuestFile` in that field instead. New: `GuestFile` from `@graview/guest` and `@graview/guest/host`; `WorkerViewManifest.pictures`, `MountGuestViewOptions.pictures`, `GuestViewOptions.pictures`; `GuestLimits.pictureBytes` and `GuestLimits.picturesBytes` (`GuestPictureLimits`), and `GuestPictures`, from `@graview/guest/host` and `/host/views`; `GuestHostOptions.hand`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-175. What Graview Cloud does: nothing to serve, since its files route already serves each file to the reader with their credentials on the page's own origin; a view it stores may say `pictures` in its manifest once `capabilities().shipped` includes FR-175, and a view's author draws `<img src="${photo.src}">` once `src` comes.
- a5c260f: A hosted page loads thirteen chunks before it draws where it loaded twenty-three, and 7.6 KB less (5.0 KB gzipped), so the round's money formats have room after its repeating dates and field-level sight. A bundler splits what a page loads first by which of its doors (each part fetched with `import()`) reach each file, so the frame's files were spread over twenty-three chunks, each with its own list of names in and out and each compressed alone. Every door in `@graview/core`, `@graview/react` and `@graview/primitives` now imports the barrel the page drew first (`@graview/core`, `@graview/react/provider`, `@graview/primitives/frame`) for nothing but that, which makes the files every door reaches one set and costs a door nothing at run time. What draws a notice is fetched with the first notice and prefetched once the page is idle (`notice-stacks.tsx`); the board and the two live regions that say each notice aloud stay up front, so a notice is said at once, and `fetchNotices()` fetches it at will (the embed's `preload` does). What only fetched faces say or do left the barrels a page imports: `edgeWords` and `valueWords` for `@graview/core/words`, and a face's moves (`withShown`, `withQuery`, `withoutSearch`, `withPast`, `withPan`, `withPin`, `edgeSelectionId`) for a new `@graview/layout/view-moves`. Built as Cloud builds it over the vendors document, the page that compiles its document goes from 549 727 bytes (192 368 gzipped) to 541 994 (187 254), and the page handed a compiled app from 507 528 (177 639) to 499 794 (172 530). Their budgets fall to keep about 8 KB of room (3.5 KB gzipped): 537.5 KB (186.4 gzipped) from 538.0 (188.0), and 496.0 KB (172.0 gzipped) from 497.5 (173.5). No face loses anything: the pages face alone is 3.4 KB less, and every face fetched together 4.4 KB less.
  
  Compatibility: BREAKING imports. `edgeWords` and `valueWords` move from `@graview/core` to `@graview/core/words`; `withShown`, `withQuery`, `withoutSearch`, `withPast`, `withPan`, `withPin` and `edgeSelectionId` move from `@graview/layout` and `@graview/layout/view` to `@graview/layout/view-moves`; `FOOT_MOVED`, `FOOT_OBSTACLES`, `NARROW_PICTURE`, `placeAtTheFoot` and `placeAtTheTop` leave `@graview/primitives/frame` (still on `@graview/primitives` and `@graview/primitives/pages`). `withoutMoves` stays on `@graview/layout/view`. New: `@graview/layout/view-moves`, and `fetchNotices` from `@graview/primitives`, `/frame` and `/pages`. Ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: import those names from their new entries; its shell built from these sources loads about 7.5 KB less before the app draws.
- 3cd624d: A lens is listed once, however many kinds it draws. The program's scene bar read "The whole thing · What the talks are about · The timetable · The timetable · A dozen workshops": its one calendar over talks and workshops was registered on each kind, so that either district goes in to it, and every list of places took each registration for a picture of its own. A place now says when it is one picture over several kinds (`Place.over`, given by `ViewMeta.over`, which `registerDeclaredLenses` sets from the lens's bindings), and every list reads it through `onePerPicture`: the scene's bar and its tabs, the Pages bar and its More, the gallery and the pictures' page, Find and `search_graph`, the seat's places, `placesOf` and `placesFrom`, `graview describe`, and the slugs a guest view may link to. A district of either kind still goes in to it, the tab is pressed from either, and choosing it keeps the district in focus when it is one of its kinds. Its page is at `/places/<as>` alone and draws every kind it is over, with each kind's list beside it. Two different lenses that share a title on different kinds are another matter: `graview check` warns `lens-title-repeated`, and the bars and the gallery say whose — "The timetable · talks" (`placeLabel`).
  
  Compatibility: `placesOf` and `placesFrom` list a picture over several kinds once, under the first of its kinds in the arrangement's order, with a new optional `over` naming every kind it is over; its `address` is `/places/<as>` and its `stop` `#view=<as>`, where before each kind had an entry at `/places/<as>?of=<plural>` (those addresses still open the picture). `describe_place` takes the one slug as before. A search's place hit for such a picture comes once. `Place`, `ViewMeta` and `ViewRegistration` gain an optional `over`; `onePerPicture`, `samePicture` and `placeLabel` are new exports of `@graview/core`, and `sharesItsName` no longer counts a picture's own other kinds. Check gains the warning `lens-title-repeated`. Stored formats, the document format and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged.
  
  The embed's budgets are raised by what this adds (`scripts/budget-raises/a-lens-is-listed-once-*.json`): the pages face alone 617 / 260 bytes, every face 1,325 / 529, the embed with the studio handed in 1,312 / 529, and the guest host on the open kit 77 / 43, minified / gzipped. A hosted page's first load holds to its budget unraised.
- 774d39c: The map from altitude reads, and says where things stand (Roadmap f-map-readable, f-map-shows-state). `cityMap` lays the districts out by how the kinds relate: each kind one column past the furthest kind it points at, so the map reads upstream to downstream, left to right — People, Sessions, Questions, Findings, Opportunities, Actions — with the kinds of a column one above the other near what they point at, a cycle broken where the walk meets it, and a kind tied to nothing in a margin under the rest. Turned upright (`upright`), the same map reads top to bottom, which is how a window taller than it is wide draws it. A plot is two cells a side, or three past four records, so the biggest kind no longer takes the map; blocks are four cells apart (`BLOCK`, was five) and the largest side is three (`MAX_SIDE`, was four).
  
  On open the whole city fits the window — what is left of it beside a panel docked by the map — clear of the bar's corner controls and the zoom: the cell is the largest at which every plot and the name under it stand inside the room, and the names keep a fixed size on the screen whatever the zoom. Under each district is its name, its count and how many have a problem, on a solid backing — and, for a kind with a status, a bar of where its records stand ("9 open · 8 doing · 14 done", the words where a segment has room and always on hover). Where two names would collide the less pressing one says less (`LayoutNode.nameplate`: whole, short, over or beside its plot, or nothing until the camera comes closer); the most pressing is always said whole, over the edge of a neighbor's ground if it must, and a chosen record's link labels are placed clear of every name. Counts are said in the app's locale (`numberAsRead`). The lens names left the district: they are in the bar, and come up under the name when the district is hovered or focused. The marks nobody could explain are gone or said: the ◆ after a count and the open chevron's dot are gone from the map, "1 of 17 tied" is not said there, "moved ×" says "Reset view", the altitude control says "Close the map" (its name says where it lands), and the Key opens with what each mark on a block means. "From above", beside the zoom, sees the same ground from straight overhead (`tilt`, `TOP_DOWN`), kept in the browser.
  
  A district's blocks stand in rows by its kind's main choice field — its lifecycle's, else one called status, stage, state or phase — one row per value in declared order (`districtState`, `districtBlocks`): done lies flat and pale, waiting stands gray, active stands darker and taller, and a row cut for room says `+n` where it begins, never under the name. Color carries state, not kind: plots and blocks are the ground's own ink, and the strong colors are a problem or a record past its due date, lit; one somebody else changed since you last left the page, aglow; your own, rimmed. Coming down, the blocks say their records' names. The ground of the city — its plots, blocks, roads and their rules — is fetched the first time the scene rises (`plots.tsx`), with the bar of states and the Key's legend, so a page that never rises carries none of it.
  
  The layout no longer stands a billboard on a plot for a focused kind with a named picture — that picture opens in the panel beside the map — so a focused kind's district keeps all its ground. `@graview/core/city` is a new entry: the map alone (`cityMap`, `districtState`, `toIso`, `sideFor`, `BLOCK`, `MAX_SIDE`, `TILTED`, `TOP_DOWN` and the rest of what lays a city out), which the scene lays out with before it draws; `@graview/core/scene` keeps everything it had and adds `districtState`, `districtBlocks`, `TIER_HEIGHT`, `TILTED` and `TOP_DOWN`. `toIso` takes a tilt. A fitted thumbnail's district fills its whole block.
  
  Compatibility: the map moves. Every kind not hand-laid stands on a new plot, sides cap at three and blocks are four cells apart, so a thumbnail of an app (`sceneThumbnail`, `sceneDistricts`) draws a different picture and two plots declared five cells apart now leave a cell more street between them; a declared `plot` is still used verbatim. `@graview/core/city` is additive. `placeCity` takes the canvas and its insets, no longer a card base. `LayoutOptions.screenHeight` and `SCREEN_LEASH_CELLS` are removed with the billboard.
- 933761a: A Markdown field named in a view never shows its syntax, a row's glance is two lines at most, and a first sentence ends at a code-ish word's stop (FR-180). A declared `{ "text": "{body}" }` block printed the field as written — "## Plan", "**Friday**" — where the record's own page drew it as prose: a text block that is a Markdown field alone is now drawn as Markdown on a page (the resolved block carries `markdown: true` and the text as written) and said by its start in a card or a row; named beside other words, or in a title or a badge, it is said as its words with the syntax taken out, on every face and in `describe_place`. A filter (`{body|excerpt}`, `{body|upper}`) says it as before. The 0.1.28 brief's row ran four lines in the glance column; the glance is now clamped to two, like the row's name. And "capabilities().shipped." was taken for an abbreviation, so an excerpt ran on into the next sentence: only short letters between stops ("e.g.", "U.S.", "Ph.D.") and the usual titles ("Dr.") are abbreviations now, and a word like "graview.dev." ends its sentence.
  
  Compatibility: a resolved text block (`resolveBlocks`, `@graview/core/blocks`) may carry `markdown: true`, and `BlockContext` takes `glance` (set by `blockContextFor` for a card or a row); a block about a field that is not Markdown resolves as before. What a card, a row or a describer says of a Markdown field named bare in a template changes from the text as written to its words. The document format, check finding codes, ops, stored formats, the wire and tool schemas are unchanged.
- 2d3f806: A store `openRemote` opened is trusted to show a field its host judged, and no other store on the page is (Roadmap f-field-sight). Opening one set a switch every store read, so a page that also held a whole store of its own — `openStore`, or a `Store` — showed that store's seats every record a sight with `hide` or `fields` reaches, phone numbers and all, without field sight loaded to keep them. The trust is now the mirror's own (`FIELD_SIGHT.served`, a set `openRemote` adds its store to), and a store without field sight loaded fails closed everywhere it judges: `kindsKeptFrom` keeps a kind only such a sight shows from a seat that may begin none, and whether a seat sees every record of a kind — what an act may mint for it — says no. An agent's tool for an act no longer names a field the seat may not see: an argument that writes a field no sight granting the seat that kind shows is left out of the act's input schema (`argumentsNeverShown` on `@graview/core/json-schema`), as a form leaves it out, while one the seat sees on some records — its own child's phone — stays.
  
  Compatibility: derived tool input schemas narrow for a seat whose sights keep a field on every record of a kind it may write: the argument is no longer listed, so that seat's tool-surface hash moves; every other seat's surface is unchanged, and the store refuses such a write as it did. `FIELD_SIGHT.on` now means only that field sight is loaded; `FIELD_SIGHT.served` is new. `sees` takes an optional sixth argument, whether a sight that shows only some fields may show the record. Ops, stored formats, the wire and the document format are unchanged. docs/stability.md says it under the seat's sight.
- dedefed: A near word finds the record, and says it is near (the Roadmap's f-semantic-search). A typo or another form of a word found nothing, and "the florist" did not find Bloom & Co, tagged flowers. Now, when the words find fewer than five records exactly, `search` (`@graview/core/search`) adds the records a near word finds, ranked after every exact hit: one slip in a word of 4–7 letters or two in a longer one ("tulpis" finds Tulips Galore, "flowr" the start of "flowers"), or another form of the word — a plural, -ing, -ed ("planting" finds "planted"). No model is asked for that, and it is fetched with Find as the matcher is: a near pass over 8,000–12,000 records adds a few milliseconds to a keystroke (6–16 ms in all on the gauntlet's and the discography's records). A host may give a ranker for what no spelling finds — `HostAi.near`, or `rank` on `search` — handed the words and only the records the seat may see, answering scores (0–1) and the record's word that was near; Find shows the exact hits at once and the ranker's when they arrive. Find's row for a near hit reads "Near “florist” — tags: flowers", the count says how many are near, and `search_graph` answers `near: true` with `why.matched`, so an agent knows it was not what it asked for. Going to a record by name and the conversation's "things called …" stay exact.
  
  Compatibility: a search's answer may now include near hits — a node hit's optional `near: true`, a `why` with `strength: "near"` and optional `asked`, `matched` and `by` (`"spelling"`, `"stem"`, `"meaning"`), and `SearchResult.later` when a ranker was asked; `search` takes `near` (false for exact hits only) and `rank`. `search_graph`'s name and input schema are unchanged; its description says what a near hit is, so a seat's tool-surface hash moves, and its answer may hold hits with `near: true`. `HostAi` gains `near`, `createToolRuntime` and `createMcpHttpHandler` take `near`, and `@graview/core` exports the types `NearBy`, `NearCandidate`, `NearScore` and `SearchRanker`. A ranker is handed only records the seat sees, past the kinds searched, the horizon and the conditions, so a hidden record's text never reaches a host's model (docs/stability.md). Ops, stored formats, the wire and the document format are unchanged. The hosted page's first load grows by 121 B (70 B gzipped), the provider waiting for a ranker's answer, within its budget; the pages face, every face and the embed with the studio handed in are raised by the near matcher's measured size (`scripts/budget-raises/a-near-word-finds-the-record-*.json`).
- A phone, a place and a length of time are field types of their own. Templates held all three as strings: a phone that was not a link to call, an address that was not a link to a map, and lengths of time nobody could add up. A document says `{ "type": "phone" }`, `{ "type": "place" }` and `{ "type": "duration" }`; a declaration `phoneField()`, `placeField()` and `durationField()`. A phone is kept as it is entered (a form trims it) and refused only when it cannot be one — fewer than three digits, or letters other than an extension's (`PHONE`: digits, spaces, `+`, `-`, `.`, `/`, parentheses, `ext. 12`, `x12`), at most 40 characters; it is said grouped as "(555) 123-4567" for a reader in the US or Canada where it is a North American number and as entered everywhere else, and drawn as a `tel:` link. A place is a line of words, at most 500 characters, drawn as a link that opens the reader's maps app in a tab of its own (`rel="noopener noreferrer"`): maps.apple.com on Apple's platforms, which opens Maps, and Google's universal Maps address elsewhere, which opens the Google Maps app on Android and the map in the browser otherwise — no one address opens every reader's own app (`geo:` does nothing in a desktop browser), and neither needs a key or fetches anything until it is followed. A duration is a whole number of minutes, none less than none, bounded by the field's `min`, `max` and `step` like a number; it is typed as "1h 30m", "90", "1:30" or "1.5 h" (`parseDuration`), said "1 h 30 min" with the locale's figures (`sayDuration`), and sorted and summed as the number it is: a computed field that sums, adds or subtracts durations — `sum(out('rehearsals'), length)`, `planned - spent` — is said as a duration too, as is one a declaration says `as: "duration"`. Each is taken by `editDocument`'s `add-field` and `retype-field` (a duration keeps its range), kept by a migration where the old value reads as one and cleared where it does not, read from a spreadsheet's cell or refused in words, written by `graview types` (a phone and a place as `string`, a duration as `number`), asked for by a form (a `tel` box; a duration in words, sent as minutes), the workbench and a value changed in place, and described to an agent with `"x-graview-type"` (and `"x-graview-unit": "minutes"` for a duration, which a seat sends as 90, never "1h 30m"). A record's page, the scene's record panel and a document card's field block draw the links, each a target of at least 24 pixels; where the value is changed in place, "Call" and "Map" stand beside it. The studio offers, declares and writes back all three. `@graview/core/words` gains `parseDuration`, `sayDuration`, `sayPhone`, `telHref`, `mapsHref`, `fieldSays`, `typedWords`, `typedHref` and `typedFieldOf`. A hosted page's first load is 392 / 168 bytes larger minified / gzipped (360 / 179 handed a compiled app), under its budgets; the embed's every face carries 7 885 / 3 336 more and with the studio handed in 7 242 / 3 150, each raised by a file of its own. verify-field-types holds it at desk width on both faces in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field types `phone`, `place` and `duration`, and `graview-compiled@1` carries them; a host's build that predates them refuses a document using them by path, and a page that predates them refuses such a compiled app as `compiled-shape` and compiles the document, which refuses the type by path. These carry no FR id, so a host validates a document that uses them against the `document.schema.json` of the oldest build it serves. `ReadableField` gains `href`, `ScalarField` gains `holds`, `ArgShape` gains `holds` (words) and `duration` (a number), and a computed field may say `as: "duration"`. A derived tool's input schema for a declaration with none of these fields is unchanged.
- 8e09c51: A place is rendered to static HTML off the browser (FR-174). `renderPlace(store, declaration, place, { viewer, look, today, base })` on the new entry `@graview/pages/static` answers `{ html, css, title, found }`: a kind's list, a record, a declared place or the home as one self-contained document in the app's look — its brand's tokens, type and shape inlined as CSS, light unless asked or declared otherwise — with no script, no form and nothing to fetch. It says what `describePlace` says the place shows that seat, a record's describe line under its name, its facts in its page's sections and its relations in the declaration's words, each listed record by its card or row (or its glance), and puts each section of a place — a board's column, a roster's week — on a page of its own in print. A record the viewer may not see, a kind kept from them and an address that names nothing are answered alike, `found: false`. The entry imports no React, router or face, so a Worker renders a fixed link — a schedule as a PDF, a roster — on each change without a DOM; nothing on a hosted page imports it. `staticCss(brand, scheme)` is the sheet alone.
  
  `describePlace`'s derived home no longer counts a kind kept from the seat — an administered module's people for a member, a kind the seat sees none of — or a disabled module's, as the routed home never listed them.
  
  Compatibility: additive. `@graview/pages/static` is a new entry, and `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-174. `describe_place` says one line fewer on the home for a seat a kind is kept from.
- 307f2d4: A problem reworded is not mended (the ninth walk, W-191). A rule that is a function says its count in its message — "Meadow 07 has not been opened for 22 days" — and `problemsMoved` knew such a problem by its message, so on a co-op of 299 hives the day turning (`store.tick`) answered 34 problems cleared and 280 appeared, when none had cleared and 246 had appeared; an act that left such a rule broken and changed its count was said to break it in the activity too. Now a problem the same rule says about the same record under the same name, in other words, is paired off with the one it was before anything is called made or mended.
  
  Compatibility: `problemsMoved` (and so `Store.tick`'s `appeared` and `cleared`, `previewLines`' and the activity's `introduces` and `resolves`) no longer lists a problem as both made and mended when only its message changed; its shape is unchanged.
- 11deba2: A host's ranker's word reaches an agent as the host's, and Find asks the ranker only once the typing pauses. The record's word a ranker says a near hit matched ("flowers") was handed to an agent as it came, at any length, as though the app had said it; a ranker that is a model can be steered by what it reads, so `search_graph` now marks it as words somebody else said — `why.matched` is `{ untrusted: true, authoredBy: "the host's ranker", text }`, the shape record text another seat wrote already takes — and a hit keeps at most 80 characters of it (`MATCHED_MOST` on `@graview/core/search`). Find searched on every keystroke and asked the host each time, so "florist" typed a letter at a time asked a model seven times for words nobody would see the answer to; it now waits 300 ms after a keystroke, and an ask a later keystroke overtook answers nothing without reaching the host.
  
  Compatibility: `search_graph`'s name and input schema are unchanged; on a near hit found by a host's ranker (`why.by: "meaning"`), `why.matched` is now an untrusted-text object rather than a string, and on every surface it is cut to 80 characters. Hits found by spelling or stem are unchanged. Ops, stored formats, the wire and the document format are unchanged. docs/stability.md says it beside near hits.
- 15c033b: A record can be archived and brought back, and two records that are one can be merged (FR-173). Every kind is given three more derived acts beside `edit-<kind>` and `remove-<kind>`: `archive-<kind>` and `unarchive-<kind>` ("Bring it back") take `{ id }`, and `merge-<kind>` takes `{ id, into }`. Archive keeps the record — its fields, its links, its history — and takes it out of lists, the Scene, counts, computed fields and rules: every graph read that lists records (`allNodes`, `nodesOfKind`, `count`, `allEdges`, `edgesOfKind`, `out`, `in`, `neighbors`) reaches the live ones unless it is given `{ archived: true }` or `{ archived: "all" }`, and a read by id (`getNode`, `has`, `outEdges`, `inEdges`) finds a record archived or not. A merge moves the duplicate's links to the kept record and archives the duplicate, its op saying `mergedInto`, both histories kept and undone as one. A host archives with `store.apply({ name: "archive-<kind>", args: { id } })`, and a mutation with `ctx.archiveNode`, `ctx.unarchiveNode` and `ctx.mergeNode`; each goes through the policy, the seat's sight and the log like any act, permitted through the acts that make the kind as a remove is, and a grant may name each on its own. The host decides what to archive; the framework keeps the state.
  
  A rule or a computed field asks for archived records explicitly: `all('task', true)` for the archived ones, `all('task', 'all')` for both, and `archived()` says whether a record is. Search still finds an archived record, marked `archived` and after the live ones, and `is:archived` asks for those alone; the agent's `search_graph` and `get_graph` take `archived` and reach the live records unless asked, and `get_node` says `archived: true`. A lens and a list page take `archived` beside `sort`, `filter`, `group` and `q` (`in.archived=all`, `?archived=true`), a list says "+N archived" beneath it, and an archived record's page says so at its head with "Bring it back" where the seat may. A name finds a live record before an archived one, and an archived one when no live one has it. `editDocument` renames and drops the new acts with their kind, `graview check` knows them, and a kind's JSON Schema says `$archived`. The hosted page carries 1.3 KB more up front, under its budget, which is not raised; the embed's budgets for the pages face alone (gzipped), every face and the studio handed in are raised by what they grew (`scripts/budget-raises/a-record-can-be-archived-and-brought-back-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive to ops and primitives — an archived record is `$archived: true` on the record, written by an ordinary `patch-node`, and a merge's op carries the optional `mergedInto`; a log folds to the same graph on an earlier version, where an archived record reads as live. Derived tools: every kind gains `archive-<kind>`, `unarchive-<kind>` and `merge-<kind>`, and `search_graph` and `get_graph` take an optional `archived`; `get_graph` no longer lists archived records unless asked. The rule language gains `archived()` and a second argument to `all`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-173.
- 3b06199: A record can link another app, and the host says what that app is called (FR-171). A relation's `to` names kinds inside one document, so a scenario in one app could not point at a house in another. The first step is a field: `{ "type": "app-link" }` in a document, `appLinkField()` in a declaration, holding another app's address — `@owner/app`, an id its host knows it by, or an absolute http(s) address, at most 500 characters (`isAppAddress`). The store refuses anything else as `invalid`, `editDocument`'s `add-field` and `retype-field` take the type, a migration keeps a value that is an address and clears one that is not, a spreadsheet's cell is read as one or refused in words (FR-177), `graview types` writes it as a string said to be another app's address, a form asks for it with "@owner/app or https://…" as its placeholder, and an act's tool schema gives it the address's `pattern` and `"x-graview-app-link": true` so an agent sets an address rather than a name. A host hands `resolveApp(address, viewer)` to `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` or the routed face's context, answering `{ name, icon?, headline?, href }`, `{ denied: true }` or `null`: the field draws as that app's icon, name and home headline, linked to `href`, on a record's page and in the scene's record panel, and as its icon and name on a list's row and a document card's field block. A viewer the host refuses sees "linked app (no access)" and nothing of the app. Without a resolver, or where the host knows no such app, the address is drawn as it is kept, a link only when it is an absolute http(s) address. The host is asked once per address and viewer, however many records link it, and a throw is taken as `null`. Where an act may write the field, a "Change" control beside the link edits the address. What draws it is a chunk fetched with the first app-link drawn: a hosted page's first load is 509 bytes larger (557 327 bytes over the vendors document, 556 818 on main), 501 handed a compiled app, and the scene before it draws 1 647, all under their budgets; the embed's pages face carries 1 884 / 1 607 bytes more minified / gzipped, every face 7 001 / 3 726, each raised by a file of its own. A relation whose `to` is another app's kind, an expression that walks it and the history of the far value are left for 0.2. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-171.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field type `app-link`, and `graview-compiled@1` carries it; a build that predates it refuses the type by path, and a page that predates it refuses such a compiled app as `compiled-shape` and compiles the document. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` and the routed face's context gain an optional `resolveApp`; `ReadableField` gains `app`, `ScalarField` gains `app`, and a document card's resolved field block gains `app`. A derived tool's input schema for a declaration with no app-link field is unchanged. What a host does: read `capabilities().shipped` for FR-171 before writing an `app-link` field, and pass `resolveApp` to have one drawn as the app rather than as its address.
- 861b0b4: A rule that is a function says it reads the clock without a cast (the ninth walk, W-190). The graview-invariant skill says such a rule "says `readsTheClock: true`", and FR-176 added the key to `InvariantDefinition` but to none of the ways a rule is declared: `bindSchema(schema).defineInvariant`, `defineGraphInvariant` and `defineInvariant` refused it, so the line the skill writes did not compile. All three take `readsTheClock` now.
  
  Compatibility: additive — `defineInvariant`'s, `bindSchema().defineInvariant`'s and `defineGraphInvariant`'s specs take the optional `readsTheClock` that `InvariantDefinition` already had.
- ed76a10: A sheet without a name column is refused in words rather than thrown (the ninth walk, W-196). `importRows` and `previewImport` named each planned record by its kind's label, and a kind declared in TypeScript is labeled `(node) => node.label`, which says nothing where the sheet holds no name — so a beekeeping co-op's sheet of inspections threw "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'trim')" before a row was read. A label that says nothing, or throws, now names the row the way a row with no name is named, and the row is refused: "it has no Name, which every inspection needs".
  
  Compatibility: none of the import's shapes change; a sheet that threw now answers.
- df64e2b: A sight hides a field, not only a kind (Roadmap f-field-sight). A role saw a whole record or none of it, so a roster where parents see every player's name and not their phone number could not be said.
