A sight may now say `hide: ["phone", "medical"]`, or `fields: ["name"]` as an allow list, and sights add up: a field is shown when any sight that shows the seat the record shows it, so `{ roles: ["parent"], kinds: ["player"], own: true }` beside the hiding one gives each parent their own child whole. A field kept from a seat is judged in the seat view, so it never leaves the store for that seat: not on the record it is served (a required one withholds the record, as a hidden required reference does), not in the log — an op that adds or removes such a record, writes such a field or whose sentence read one is withheld, whatever the field held, its primitives served without it — nor in an act's answer, a conflict, a search hit, a name a call resolves, a form, an edit offered in place, a static page or a tool's answer. A computed field and a rule are worked out over what the seat is served, so a phone it may not see reads as none. The seat may not write such a field: an act that would is refused as one given an argument it does not take (`invalid`), the same whatever it held and whatever was written, and an import refuses a column that goes to one as it refuses a column that goes to no field. `graview check` names a field a sight misspells (`sight-names-an-unknown-field`), one a record cannot do without (`sight-hides-a-required-field`) and a label or description that reads a kept field (`sight-hides-a-labeled-field`, a warning). `add-sight` and `remove-sight` take the keys, a field the sight's kinds lack is refused (`sight-field`), and the studio carries, adds, changes and writes them back. Judging a field is `@graview/core/field-sight`, which `@graview/ship`'s server entry loads; a page that holds its whole store (`openStore` from `@graview/ship/browser`, or a `Store` of its own) imports it once, as does a host that serves seats without `@graview/ship`, and a page `openRemote` opened needs none, its host having judged what it sent. Without it, a sight with `hide` or `fields` shows nothing: the store keeps the record rather than serve a field it cannot judge. The new graview-sight skill teaches it; graview-permissions points there.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration gains two optional keys on a sight, `policy.sees[].hide` and `policy.sees[].fields` (field names), and the document schema with them; a declaration without them compiles and serves as before. Four finding codes are new (`sight-names-an-unknown-field`, `sight-hides-a-required-field`, `sight-hides-a-labeled-field`, a document edit's `sight-field`). Ops and the wire are unchanged in shape: a seat with a kept field is served fewer fields and more withheld ops, and an op's `described` may name `<id>#<field>` — a field its sentence read — beside the record ids it named; a build that predates it reads those as ids that name no record. `WIRE_PROTOCOL` does not move. The risk is an older build: a host or a page on a build before this one ignores `hide` and `fields` and SHOWS the field to the role it was hidden from — so a host serves a declaration that says either only on a build that has `@graview/core/field-sight` (`FIELD_SIGHT` on `@graview/core`), and refuses such a declaration otherwise; `capabilities().shipped` names no FR id for it. Stored data is unchanged. docs/stability.md says it under the seat's sight.
- 1076b0c: A spreadsheet comes in as one undoable batch (FR-177). `importRows(store, kind, rows, mapping, { by })` on the new entry `@graview/core/import` takes rows as arrays under a header row or as records keyed by header, and a mapping from columns to the kind's fields and relations — a relation the kind declares, or one declared toward it (`{ relation, direction: "in" }`). Each cell is read by its field's type: a date written as ISO, as `M/D/YYYY` (`dayFirst` for `D/M/YYYY`), as "Sep 10, 2023" or as a sheet's serial day; a number with its currency, unit, thousands separators, a parenthesized loss or a percent; a choice by its option or its words, case aside; a yes or a no as yes/no, true/false, x or ✓. A cell that names another kind's record links it by its label as acts take names — an exact name, or the one label it starts — under the importer's sight. Every row that fits lands as one batch, each record its own op ("Add a vendor: Bloom & Co") under the batch intent "Import 52 vendors", attributed to `by` and recorded `via: "import"`, and `store.undo(result.batch)` takes it all back. A row that does not fit is left out with every reason in words — "“soon” under Booked on is not a date", "“Smith” under Event could be Smith reunion or Smith wedding", "it has no Name, which every vendor needs" — and the rest still land. The importer is held to the acts that create the kind: a policy or an agent's `may` that refuses them refuses every row in its own words, and a host's `admit` is asked with the import as planned before anything is kept. `previewImport` (or `preview: true`) says per row what would be made, linked and refused, and the totals, and keeps nothing. `proposeMapping(app, kind, header)` proposes a mapping from the header row — fields by name, label or name spoken, relations by name or by the noun of the kind at the far end — with the columns nothing answers to and the required fields no column fills. `parseCsv(text)` reads a CSV or a paste: RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF, the byte-order mark, and a tab or semicolon delimiter read off the first line. The import is a library function; no agent tool is derived for it. The graview-seed skill says when to reach for it.
  
  Compatibility: additive. `@graview/core/import` is a new entry, nothing on a hosted page imports it, and `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-177.
- c893321: A store's tick never outlives the day it was made on (FR-176). `store.tick(now)` set the day the rules are judged on until the next tick, so a store whose timer slept through midnight — a laptop's server, a process paused — judged "overdue" on yesterday until the timer woke, and `store.today()` said yesterday to every page that asked. A tick now holds for the rest of the day the clock read when it was made; past it, the store judges on the clock again until it is ticked, and a day a host ticks to on purpose (a replay, a test) holds as before for that day. `describePlace`, and so `describe_place` and a place rendered to static HTML, says "due tomorrow" against the store's day — the one it was ticked or pinned to — where it read the clock.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged. `Tick.was` is the day the store judged on before the tick, as it was.
- ee15ae3: A store hands itself to a watching harness, and the watch reads its words (the ninth walk, W-193 and the hosted page's budget). `@graview/core` worked out the declaration's names, what a seat may not see and who had signed what, and told them to a harness's watch — 2.4 KB that every reader of a hosted page downloaded for a harness's sake, and the room the ninth walk's fixes needed under the hosted page's budget, which is not raised. The store now hands itself over (`__graviewWatch.store`, and `__graviewWatch.seat` with the seat at a surface) and the watch (`scripts/lib/watch.mjs`) reads the same words from it. On the way it learns that a yes or a no a kind words itself is its own word: a hive's `queenSeen`, labeled "Queen" and said "Queen seen" when true, was reported on every page that drew an inspection as a key spoken where the declaration says otherwise.
  
  Compatibility: nothing a person sees, stores or sends changes. `tellTheWatchItsAuthors` is removed from `@graview/core`, and `tellTheWatchWhatIsUnseen(store, principal, from?)` hands the store and the seat to the watch's `seat` rather than computing the words; a page's `globalThis.__graviewWatch` is called with `store` and `seat`, no longer with the names' `learn` nor `unseen`.
- 488aba6: An excerpt is not cut short at an abbreviation's stop. `{summary|excerpt}` said the first sentence of "Dr. Okafor reviews every request…" as "Dr.", and of "E.g. a garden…" as "E.g.": any stop followed by a space ended the first sentence. A stop that ends an abbreviation ("e.g.", "U.S.", "Dr.", "Mr.") is passed over, and so is one before three words, unless it ends the text; a short first sentence that is a sentence ("Ship it on Friday.") still ends the excerpt.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged. What a card says of a text that opens with an abbreviation changes.
- 4dbb23e: An id a seat mints for itself does not spell who the seat is, under FR-55. A seat that may not see every record of a kind minted `room:green.person-gus`, and every seat that saw the room read Gus's id in it — a seat the policy keeps people's records from among them, whose served log withholds an op by an author it may not see. The mark is now `seatMark(<the seat's id>)`, seven letters and digits that are the same for the seat on any store (so a page's guess and its host's answer agree) and that no other seat's label mints: `room:green.k3j9x2a`. `seatMark` is exported from `@graview/core`, and `mintId` is the one rule an act's `freshId` and an import mint by.
  
  Compatibility: ops unchanged in shape. An id minted for a seat that may not see every record of a kind is `<kind>:<slug>.<seatMark>` where it was `<kind>:<slug>.<seat slug>`; ids already minted are kept as they are. A seat that sees every record of the kind mints as before.
- d8dc4c6: An import can change the records a sheet already holds instead of making them twice (FR-177). Imported a second time — corrected, a quote raised, an email fixed — the vendors sheet made every vendor again, numbered `-2`. `importRows` and `previewImport` take `match`: `"label"` finds a live record of the kind called what the row would call it, `{ column, field }` one whose field holds what the row has under that column, both under the importer's sight. A row that finds one changes only the fields it has whose value differs, through the kind's `edit-<kind>` so the policy answers as a press on "Change" would, and links what it names that the record lacks (a relation to one replaces the link it had); a row that finds none makes a record as before. Each row says which — "Row 2 would change Bloom & Co (Quote: 1000 → 1200).", "Row 3 would leave Petal Press as is.", "Row 4 would make New Florist." — and a row is refused in words when two records answer to it, when the one that answers is archived, when a row above already makes or changes it, or when it would change a field the edit act does not. The changes, the records made and the links are one batch and one undo. `proposeMapping` suggests a `match` (a key column such as an email or a code, else the column the name is read from) with `matchSaid`, and never applies it.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. `@graview/core/import` gains the `match` option and the `ImportMatch` and `ImportChange` types; `ImportResult` gains `changed` and `unchanged`, `ImportRowOutcome` gains `action` (`make`, `change`, `leave` or `refuse`) and `changes`, and `ProposedMapping` gains `match` and `matchSaid`. Without `match` an import makes, says and keeps exactly what it did.
- a047696: An import makes only the links its importer may make, and mints ids that say nothing of what it may not see (FR-177, FR-55). `importRows` asked only whether the importer may add the kind, so a helper who may add a vendor and not book one booked fifty from a sheet's Event column, and an own-end grant ("you, on your own end") was never asked of a link at all. Each link a row makes is now asked of the acts that make links of its relation (their `connects`) as a press on one would be, with the record at the far end handed to the argument that takes it — a grant's kinds, a `self` or own-end grant and an agent's `may` answer as they would — and a row with a link its importer may not make is refused in the policy's words ("Not permitted: “Book for an event” on a vendor — a planner can."). Where a policy is declared, a relation no act makes is linked by nobody; where none is, a person links what they name, as before. A record's id is minted as the importer's own act would mint it — its own, for a kind they may not see every record of — where it stepped past a record they could not see (`vendor:bloom-co-2` with no Bloom & Co in sight). A one-to-one link a row gives a record the importer sees is judged by what they see: one a hidden record held is taken as an act's `setSingleSource` takes it, where the row was refused "already has its one".
  
  Compatibility: unchanged on every surface. An import under a policy may refuse a row it made before: one whose link no act the importer may run makes.
- b61f093: Cards you can read at a glance (the Roadmap's f-glanceable-cards). On the Graview Roadmap's "Up next", each card printed its whole thirty-line summary, so a row of cards was a wall of text, every card stretched to the tallest beside it, in a box of its own to scroll under the page's scroll. A long text on a card is now three lines at most and on a row one, cut with an ellipsis; the record's page says it whole. A template says a long text's start on purpose: `{summary|excerpt}` is its first sentence, or about 140 characters cut at a word, and `{summary|excerpt: 80}` says at most 80. Cards side by side keep their own heights. A place drawn from blocks flows with the page, never a box that scrolls inside itself; a list cut by its `limit` says "See all 120 features" on the routed face and goes to the kind's list (or "24 more · all features" when it listed some of them), where the scene still says "and 24 more" of a list without a limit. `graview check` warns where a card or a row puts a `text` field whole in a text block (`view-whole-text`: "feature card shows summary whole; it will be clamped. Use {summary|excerpt} or move it to the page."). `excerpt` is in `@graview/core` too, and the stylesheet the blocks are drawn with is written without the spaces it does not need, to make room for this under the hosted page's budget.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and wire messages are unchanged. The document format's templates take the formatter `excerpt` (`excerpt: <20–1000>`); a template that names it is refused by an older build. New check finding code `view-whole-text`, a warning. `ResolvedList` carries `total`, how many its source named before the limit. `SpecLinkTo.list` is `SpecLinkTo.more`, a face's way to draw the rest of a list cut short. `SPEC_VIEW_CSS` says the same rules without spaces (`.graview-spec [data-graview-tone="good"]{--graview-spec-tone:var(--graview-good)}`), with `.graview-spec-card .graview-spec-text` clamped to three lines and `.graview-spec-row .graview-spec-text` to one. On the routed face a place drawn from blocks has no fixed height (`[data-testid="place-lens"][data-flows]`). What a host does: a card that should say more than three lines of a text moves it to the page.
- 0b722e4: Days, times and numbers are said in the app's locale, en-US first (FR-178). The eighth walk read "10 Sep 2023" in an app written for the United States: every day was said day first, in an order fixed in code, and no face knew a locale. Now every day, time and figure a person reads is said through `Intl` — a record's facts and its glance, a template's `{due}`, `{due | date}` and a moment's `{at}`, a block's field and figure, a rule's line and a rule's message, a creating act's line, an arrangement's headings ("Week of Aug 24, 2026"), the calendar's titles and labels, a declared timeline's hours, and the seat's lines ("On Thursday, Sep 17: …") — in en-US unless the app says otherwise: "Sep 10, 2023", "3:30 PM", "1,250". A moment (`2023-09-10T15:30`) is said with its time where it was said as it is kept. The app says its locale where it says its currency, `brand.locale` ("en-GB" keeps the day first, "de-DE" writes "1.250"), and a host that knows its reader passes theirs as `locale` on the embed (both faces), `GraviewProvider` and a pages `PageContext`, which wins over the app's. `describeApp` says what the locale makes of a day, a time and a figure; `editDocument`'s `set-brand` and `diffDocuments` say "Days, times, numbers and money are written for de-DE." The framework's own words stay English. `@graview/core` exports `dayAsRead`, `momentAsRead`, `timeAsRead`, `numberAsRead`, `weekdayAsRead`, `dayOfMonthAsRead`, `monthAsRead`, `weekdayName`, `minutesAsRead`, `whenAsRead`, `localeFor`, `sayIn` and `DEFAULT_LOCALE`; each takes an optional locale. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-178.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and derived tool schemas are unchanged; `brand.locale` is the key it was, and now says days, times and numbers as well as money. The words a person reads change: a day is said "Aug 28, 2026" where it was said "28 Aug 2026", a moment with its time where it was said as stored, a number in a block or a rule's line with the locale's separators, a week's heading with its day in words, and the seat's day "Thursday, Sep 17" where it was "Thursday 17 Sep". `editDocument`'s sentence for `set-brand`'s `locale` and `diffDocuments`' for a changed locale say "Days, times, numbers and money are written for …"; a changed currency is its own sentence. What a host does: pass the reader's locale as `locale` on `mount`, and match words a test reads in en-US.
- b61f093: Filters that stay usable as an app grows (the Roadmap's f-filters-that-scale). On the Graview Roadmap's Requests, "Filter" opened one long menu listing every record at the far end of every relation — "The feature it became" alone was ninety feature names, wrapping, in a popover that ran off the screen — and a row's "1 vote" was cut off at a desk's right edge while "0 votes" was drawn as a badge. A list's choice fields now stand on its arranging line as chips with counts ("Considering 12"), always in view and on a phone a rail of their own beside "Arrange": one press keeps that value, another value of the same field takes its place, pressed again it lets go, and each counts among the rest of what is asked. What is on is a removable chip beside the count. Filter opens short: every heading starts closed and says how many of its own are on; a box at the top, which stays as the list scrolls, finds any filter or any record at a relation's far end by a few letters; a relation with more than eight records there lists only "anything" and "nothing" and is found by typing, eight matches at most; the list is opaque, at most 520 pixels tall with its own scroll, and a sheet along the foot of a phone, where Filter is now a word of its own beside "Arrange" (which holds the sort and the grouping). A list reaches through a relation to what is at its far end — `became.stage:building`, said "The feature it became · Stage: Building" — and `pages.lists.<kind>.filters` names what a kind's list offers, in order (a field, a relation, `is`, or `<relation>.<field>`); unsaid, a list offers every way but a relation to a kind with more than forty records, which it offers by the far kind's choices instead, and its first two choice fields as chips. `graview check` says a filter the kind cannot be filtered by (`pages-filter-unknown`) and a kind `pages.lists` names that is not there (`pages-kind-unknown`). A count of nothing ("0 votes", "0") is no badge, and a list's glance says a record's prose by its start.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and wire messages are unchanged. The document format takes `pages.lists` (`{ "<kind>": { "filters": [...] } }`) and `arrange-pages` takes `filters: { "<kind>": [...] | null }`, which it refuses for a key the kind cannot be filtered by; `DocumentDiff`'s sentences may say "The request list filters by status, became.stage.". A filter's grammar takes `<edge>.<field>:<value>`, judged by `conditionHolds` (and so by search's `key:value`). New check finding code `pages-filter-unknown`. `@graview/core/arrange` exports `throughEdges`; `ArrangeOffer.about` may be `"via"`. `@graview/primitives` exports `offeredFilters`, `chipOffers`, `farCount`, `farCounts`, `MANY_AT_THE_FAR_END`, `TYPED_FROM` and `CHIP_FIELDS`; `ArrangeBar` takes `filters`, `chips` and `offers`. On a narrow line "Arrange" (`arrange-all`) holds the sort and the grouping and Filter (`arrange-add`) stands beside it; the Filter list's headings are `[data-testid$="-section"][data-key="<offer key>"]`, closed until pressed, and its box is `-filter-find`. What a host does: a harness that picked a Filter entry by `[data-value]` opens its heading first.
- 3c16f27: "Keep this link" is permitted per relation (FR-172). A seat that could run any act that made or broke links, or only made records, could keep any provisional link it saw: a clerk who could add a property could accept a link a model drew between two people. `keep-link` is now permitted, as the act that connects would be, to a seat that may run a declared act whose `connects` or `severs` names the link's relation, or that a grant names `keep-link` to; a link of a relation no act makes or breaks is kept by nobody a policy names. A keep whose either end the seat may not see is still refused as `missing`.
  
  Compatibility: derived tools and the wire are unchanged in shape. Under a policy, a `keep-link` from a seat that may make records or links of other relations but not this one is refused as `forbidden` where it landed. With no policy nothing changes.
- 4dbb23e: "Keep this link" tells a seat nothing of a record it may not see (FR-172, FR-55). `keep-link` names the link's ends as plain ids, so the store's check that a call names no hidden record never read them: a seat that guessed an id kept a hidden record's link when one was there and was refused "missing edge" when none was — an answer that said which guess was real — and could make a link it could not see ordinary. A keep whose either end the seat may not see is now refused as `missing`, in the graph's own words, alike whether the link is there or not.
  
  Compatibility: the wire's refusals are unchanged in shape; a `keep-link` naming a record the seat may not see is refused `missing` where it could land.
- 93a7636: A text field may be written in Markdown, and every list says a text field by its start (FR-180). Graview Cloud's Roadmap keeps its briefs as Markdown in a text field, and the Briefs list drew each body whole with its syntax showing, and the record page too. A document says `{"type": "text", "format": "markdown"}` and a declaration `markdownField()`; the value is kept as written. The record's page — the routed face, the scene's record, the scaffolded page and a static page from `renderPlace` — draws it as prose: headings demoted under the page's own, paragraphs with their line breaks kept, lists (nested, and tasks as ☐ and ☑), emphasis, code and code blocks, quotations, rules and tables, and links only to `http:`, `https:` and `mailto:`, each `rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow"` in a new tab. No HTML passes through: a tag, a handler or a `javascript:` or `data:` link is its words, an entity its character, and a picture `![…](…)` is its words — a record's pictures are its image fields' (FR-162). The reader is a small one of the framework's own, `@graview/core/markdown` (`markdownBlocks`, `markdownHtml`, `safeHref`), linear in what it reads, fetched with the first Markdown a page draws. Editing it opens the text area, with a "Preview" that shows it as the record will; the form says "Markdown" beside the field.
  
  A row, a card, a describe line, a kind stop's table cell, a search hit and a seat's line about a record say a text field by its start (`excerptOf`, on `@graview/core/words`): its first paragraph, heading or list item, or about 160 characters cut at a word, with an ellipsis where words were left out and Markdown's syntax taken out — "## Heading **bold**" is "Heading bold". A template's `{body|excerpt}` takes out Markdown's commonest marks too, and a derived edit's history line says a long string by its start rather than whole. How many lines each draws is still the surface's clamp. Plain text fields keep their paragraphs and line breaks on the record's page, as before.
  
  The checker refuses `markdown` on any type but `text`, and a number's format on a `text`, as `field-format`. `retype-field` takes `"format": "markdown"` and drops it when left out, saying "brief's body is now written in Markdown" or "is drawn as written, not as Markdown"; `diffDocuments` says the change too. A Markdown field's tool argument carries `"contentMediaType": "text/markdown"` and "Written in Markdown." `graview types` notes it. The studio reads it as a text field that says Markdown (`markdown` on its field node, set by `add-field` and `retype-field`), writes it back as `markdownField()` and hands a document back with `"format": "markdown"` kept. `pnpm verify long-text` now also asks it of the Roadmap's real briefs, on a desk and a phone in both schemes: a Markdown body renders as prose with no syntax showing, a list row says a text field as one excerpt line with no Markdown syntax, and hostile Markdown runs nothing. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-180.
  
  A hosted page's first load is 171 bytes (0.2 KB) heavier, 114 gzipped: the mark on a compiled text field, the format's name, the template excerpt's marks and the history line's start. Nothing else is up front; the reader and the renderer are fetched with the record that draws one. Fetched all together, every face is 16_333 / 6_776 bytes more and the embed with the studio handed in 5_888 / 2_421, each raised by a file of its own (scripts/budget-raises/markdown-in-a-text-field-every-face.json, -studio.json).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. The document format gains `"markdown"` among a field's `format` values within `graview-document@1`, refused by a build that predates it, and the checker a finding code, `field-format`. A derived tool's input schema for a Markdown field gains `contentMediaType` and the words "Written in Markdown."; for a declaration without one it is unchanged. A derived edit's intent, which the log keeps, says a string of more than 60 characters by its start. `excerptOf` and `isMarkdownField` are on `@graview/core/words`, `markdownField` on `@graview/core`, and the new entry `@graview/core/markdown`. What Graview Cloud does: offer `"format": "markdown"` on a text field when `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-180, and send `retype-field` with `{"type": "text", "format": "markdown"}` to turn it on.
- 73a9750: A number field marked `"format": "money"` is said as money on every face. Found on a Graview Cloud app: a deal's amount, in an app whose currency is USD, read a bare 10000 on its record page, its list, its board's cards, the scene's record, the kind's table, a static page and its form; only a `{amount | money}` template and a lens block said "$10,000". A document's number field now carries what it is said as on its schema (`markAmount`), and every face that reads a record says it there, in the app's currency (`brand.currency`, or the field's own `"unit": "EUR"`) and the reader's locale (FR-178): money "$10,000", a percent kept as a fraction "13%", a measure with its unit "12.5 kg"; a number shown as a duration (`"format": "duration"`) now means exactly the `duration` type — whole minutes, said "1 h 30 min", typed as "1h 30m" and told to a model as `x-graview-type: "duration"` — and the checker notes that the type says it once. A field named bare in a label, a description, a rule's sentence or a refusal (`{amount}`) is said as `{amount | money}` is, and a change's line says "amount → $12,500". A form asks for an amount as it is said: the pages face's form, a record's edit in place and the strip's prompt show the currency's symbol (or "%", the unit) beside a text box with a suggestion in the reader's figures, and read "$1,250.50", "1.250,50 €" and "15%" as typed (`amountBox`, `amountInBox` and `readAmount` on `@graview/core/words`). An act's declared argument that sets a formatted field and says no format of its own is asked for as the field is. `describeApp` says each amount under "## Amounts", and an act's tool schema marks one with `"x-graview-format"` (`money`, `percent` or `unit`), `"x-graview-currency"` and `"x-graview-unit"`, and a description saying the number is kept plain. `editDocument` gains `set-format` (`{ kind, field, format?, unit? }`, `null` clears either), which changes only how a field is said — a number's `money`, `percent`, `duration` and `unit`, or a text field's `markdown` (FR-180) — and says it in words — "deal's amount is said as money in USD — 10000 reads $10,000; its values are kept." — as `add-field` and `retype-field` now say a format they set, and refuse one on a field it does not fit; `diffDocuments` says a format or a unit that changed. `graview check` warns `money-currency` where a money field has no currency to be said in; `field-format`, one code with FR-180's, says in one sentence a format on a field it does not fit — an error for `markdown`, a warning for a number's format — and notes a number shown as a duration. What a hosted page loads first grows by the mark on a number's schema and the change line's money: 476 bytes gzipped over a document, 301 over a compiled app, under both budgets; what reads an amount, asks for one or says one in words is fetched with the faces and forms that do. The embed's every-face and studio budgets are raised by files of their own (10_726 / 3_888 and 10_092 / 3_734 bytes).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged; stored values are kept as the numbers they were. The document format is unchanged; the edit vocabulary gains `set-format`, which a build that predates it refuses as an edit it does not know, and `add-field` or `retype-field` with a format on a field that is not a number or an integer is now refused where it was taken silently. A new check finding code, `money-currency`, a warning; `field-format` (FR-180) also warns of a number's format on any field that is not a number, where it refused one only on `text`, and notes a number shown as a duration. Derived tool input schemas change for an act whose argument is a number field with a `format` or a `unit`: the property gains `x-graview-format`, `x-graview-currency` or `x-graview-unit` and a description (announced on the conformance kit's `document:vendors`, 0.1.29); tool names are unchanged. The words a person reads change wherever a formatted number was said bare, and a percent field's facts are said to the whole percent, as `{x | percent}` says them.
- 4c545b7: The city from altitude is laid out along the ground's own axes, and a relation's ribbon runs on the ground: a straight street beside each row of districts, and at most a bend or two between rows.
  
  The map of #263 stood upstream to downstream left to right across the screen, which on the tilted ground is the lattice's diagonal: every row of districts lay across the grain of the ground, so no street could run along one, and a ribbon between two districts of a row had either to climb a large V past the row or zigzag through the gutters behind each district between. Now `cityMap` lays the chain upstream to downstream along one of the lattice's axes — up and to the right on the screen, as a street in an isometric city runs — each kind one place past the furthest kind it points at, the kinds of one place side by side along the other axis, and the kinds tied to nothing in a row of their own parallel to the rest. Districts stand a block and a cell apart along both axes. In a tall window the chain runs down and to the right, folded into bands, where that stands the city larger. The window is still fitted to the whole city, clear of the chrome and of a panel docked beside the map.
  
  A district's name stands under its own plot, whole or short, and nowhere else: over a plot or beside it, it read as the neighbor's. Where names under their districts would meet, the districts stand further apart along the same axes (`cityMap`'s `pitch`), rather than a name moving. Where no spacing says every name inside the window — a phone, whose names are as wide as its city — the camera starts closer, at the spacing and closeness that name them all with the smallest city, and the city runs past the window's edges, with the signs on those edges saying what is there; a district the person chose is kept in sight. On a phone the panel beside the map is a sheet from the foot, and the city is laid out above it rather than under it (`--graview-sheet`), so what was chosen can still be pressed and asked for its acts; a lens's sheet stands at three fifths of the picture.
  
  A ribbon is routed on the lattice (`routeOnTheGround`): along the ground's axes through the gutters, a cell clear of every curb, never across a district; out of the middle of one district's curb square on and into the other's square on; bending only where it must, so a row's ribbons run its street straight; round the districts' name cards, measured where they were drawn — or, where the cards leave no way at all or only a long way round, under them as a street runs under a sign; keeping to the streets by the districts rather than the open ground beyond them. A stretch another ribbon already runs costs less, so relations that can share a street do, and each keeps one lane along the whole of a street, its own width from the next. The ribbons lie under the districts and their buildings, and follow the tilt the city is seen at, from above as from the side. A district chosen that the app has not got lists nothing beside the map. The router rides in the ribbons' own chunk, fetched when the city is first drawn; the embed's every-face budget is raised by 4_783 / 2_256 bytes for it and the phone's closer camera (scripts/budget-raises/ribbons-run-on-the-ground-every-face.json), and a hosted page's first load is no larger.
  
  Compatibility: every district the declaration does not place by hand stands at a new address once (a declared `plot` stands where it always did), so `graview describe`'s map and a scene thumbnail draw the new layout. `cityMap` takes a `pitch` hint and `@graview/core/city` exports `PITCH`; `placeCity` takes `keep`, the kinds whose districts stay in sight. `routeRibbons`, `tileOf` and `crossesAny` give way to `routeOnTheGround` in `@graview/react`'s ribbon module; none was exported from a package entry.
- 0d585ba: Rules that read the clock are judged again when the day turns (FR-176). A rule was judged on every change and once on load, so "overdue" stayed unsaid in a room that stayed open past midnight. `store.tick(now)` judges the store's rules on `now`'s day (UTC, as `today()` reads the clock, or a day given as `YYYY-MM-DD`) and answers `{ day, was?, appeared, cleared }`: the problems the day turning made and mended. Every subscriber hears it with an empty diff and no ops, so the bar, the problems page and the counts read again; nothing is written or logged, and a store pinned to a day (`invariantOptions.today`) stays on it. The day a store judges on is handed to every rule as its `today` — a rule in the rule language reads it before its own clock, a rule that is a function reads it from its arguments and says `readsTheClock: true` — and a broken rule's line is said on it too. `@graview/ship`'s `openStore` (and so `serve`) and `openRemote` tick their store just after each midnight until `close()`; `openStore({ tick: false })` leaves it to a host that sleeps through timers, which calls `store.tick(now)` from its own alarm. `tickEachDay(store)`, `untilTheDayTurns()` and `dayOf(now)` are on `@graview/core`.
  
  `clockReaders(schema, rules)` on `@graview/core/check` says which rules and computed fields read the clock — `today()`, `now()`, a computed field or fact that does, a kind whose records retire on a date — and `graview check` notes them (`rules-read-the-clock`), saying what a host owes them. The `graview-invariant` skill says how a rule that is a function reads the day and how a host ticks.
  
  Compatibility: additive. `Store.tick`, the `today` an invariant is handed, `InvariantDefinition.readsTheClock` and `openStore`'s `tick` option are new; a new check finding code, `rules-read-the-clock`, is a note and never fails a check. A store whose `invariantOptions.today` is pinned now has its document rules judged on that day too, where they read the clock. `openStore` and `openRemote` hold an unref'd timer until `close()`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-176.
- 5197748: Archive and merge are offered after a record's own acts and its edit, just ahead of remove, never first (FR-173). Settled to one press, `archive-<kind>` and `merge-<kind>` led every strip: on the studio's selected kind, "Archive the kind" came first and pushed "Add a field" past the rows shown before "Show more". "Bring it back" keeps its place, since it is only offered on an archived record. A kind can now say `archives: false` on `defineNode`, and is then given no `archive-<kind>`, `unarchive-<kind>` or `merge-<kind>` (its `remove-<kind>` stays): the studio's meta-schema says it on every kind, because a kind, a field or a relation is in the declaration or not, never set aside.
  
  Compatibility: derived tools — the studio's meta-schema loses the archive, unarchive and merge tools it gained in 0.1.29 for its kinds, and a kind declaring `archives: false` has none. Every other kind's tools are unchanged; only the order acts are offered in moves.
- 952e526: Several records are chosen and acted on at once, as one change. Every act on a page was on one record, so marking six tasks done was six rounds and six undos. A kind's list on the pages, a kind's stop in the scene and a status board now choose records: a box at each row's start (on a desk, shown as the pointer or the keyboard reaches the row and always once one is chosen), Shift for a range, Cmd/Ctrl-A for the list, Space on a row, Escape to put the choice down, and on a phone a long press, after which a tap chooses rather than opens. A bar over the face, fetched with the first one chosen, says "6 tasks chosen" to a screen reader as it changes and offers the acts every chosen record shares — the derivation's own, in its order, with "Move to Declined" for the kind's state field and archive, merge and remove behind More — asks an act's questions once for all of them, and runs it as one batch with one undo, attributed as any act is. A record the act may not run on — the policy, the seat's sight, the act's own refusal — is named with why and stays chosen, and the rest are done: each record is judged first, without being told to anybody, and the batch is all-or-nothing only when the store refuses it whole after that (a served store's server), which the bar says ("None of it was kept"). `actsForSeveral` and `actOnSeveral` are on `@graview/tools/several` (and `@graview/tools`), and a seat does the same with `act_on_several` (`{ act, records, args }`), which answers the batch, the records changed, those already so and those refused. The hosted page carries 33 bytes more up front (22 gzipped): the names the first chunk now exports to the bar's chunk.
  
  Compatibility: derived tool names and input schemas — one tool is added, `act_on_several`, listed for every seat that is not read-only, so the surface's hash moves; every act's tool and every other tool is unchanged. `@graview/tools` gains the `./several` entry; `AnswerArgs` takes an optional `store`.
- 4b12e16: A hosted page has room again before the app draws: 20.7 KB less up front (6.4 KB gzipped). The wave before 0.1.29 left Graview Cloud's shell 94 bytes gzipped under its budget, and 20.6 KB of what it loaded first was zod's JSON-Schema writer, which only the seat calls, to write an act's tool, and the seat is fetched when first asked. It rode up front because a bundler places a whole file in the first chunk when the page can reach it, and every zod barrel a page imports to build an app's fields reaches the writer. A tool's JSON Schema is now written by graview from the schema's own definition (`core/src/schema/to-json-schema.ts`, fetched with the seat), so nothing on a page calls zod's writer and no chunk carries it. Built as Cloud builds it over the vendors document, the page that compiles its document goes from 564 089 bytes (191.4 KB gzipped) to 542 940 (185.0 KB gzipped), and the page handed a compiled app from 522 312 (177.0 KB gzipped) to 501 163 (170.6 KB gzipped): 21 149 bytes fewer each. Their budgets fall to keep about 8 KB of that as room: 538.0 KB (188.0 gzipped) from 554.0 (191.5), and 497.5 KB (173.5 gzipped) from 513.5 (177.5). Every face fetched together, and the embed with the studio handed in, are 10.6 KB more, since the studio's classic schemas keep zod's writer beside graview's; each is raised by a file of its own in `scripts/budget-raises/`. The pages face alone is 21.5 KB less. A test holds what graview writes equal to zod's, key for key, over every example app's acts and kinds, every document fixture, and every kind of schema zod has.
  
  Compatibility: every name stays where it was, and `@graview/core/json-schema`'s `toJsonSchema`, `nodeJsonSchema`, `mutationToolSchema` and `schemaJson` write the same JSON as before, so derived tool names, input schemas and a tool surface's hash are unchanged; the one difference is that the object returned no longer carries zod's hidden, non-enumerable `~standard` property. Ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged. What a host does: nothing; Graview Cloud's shell built from these sources loads about 20 KB less before the app draws.
- 8da8189: A hosted page's first load is 31 KB lighter, and a change that spends more of it has to say so (FR-179). Graview Cloud's shell measured 562 KB of its 570, the framework's hosted page 573.8 KB of 579.7, and every release had spent a little of it. A bundler places a whole file in a page's first chunk when the first chunk can reach it and any fetched chunk uses it, so a name a barrel the page imports re-exports rode up front as soon as a face, a form or the seat used it. Those names left the barrels for entries of their own: the matcher (`search`, `actsOn`, `touchWeights`, `parseQuery`, `matchNode`, `fold`, `squeeze`, `strengthOf`, `fragmentOf`, `searchableFields`, `describeSearched`) is `@graview/core/search`, handed to the provider by the scene and the Find box that search (`matchWith`, from `@graview/react/provider`); a record read in words and an argument's shape (`readableFields`, `pageSections`, `isLongText`, `describeNode`, `tellApart`, `argShape`, `describeArg`, `argumentWords`, `failureWords`, `viaSaid`) are `@graview/core/words`; an act's answer to its seat, every place an app has and the order things are made in (`answerSeenBy`, `placesOf`, `placesFrom`, `beginning`) are `@graview/core/seat`; judging the arrangement (`arrangementFindings`) is `@graview/core/check`'s; a card's sizes and a line's estimated width (`rosterRows`, `MARQUEE_WIDTH`, `estimateWidth` and the rest) are `@graview/layout/sizes`; and an address's arrangement is read apart from what a kind can be arranged by. A frame guest's host — `mountGuestView`, `createGuestHost`, `createGuestLimiter`, `readAcross`, `readTheme` and the protocol's constants — is `@graview/guest/host/frame`, and `guestView` fetches it as the view is first drawn, the frame's height held meanwhile, asked for again when it did not arrive. The studio's door, the seat's panel and the lenses were already fetched with the face that draws them; a test now holds each of them, the frame guest's host and the matcher off the first load.
  
  `pnpm verify hosted` measures a hosted page at 556 818 bytes up front (543.8 KB, 187.4 KB gzipped), where main measured 589 206 (575.4 KB, 199.9 KB gzipped): 31.6 KB less, 12.5 KB gzipped. Handed a compiled app (FR-123) it is 515 126 bytes (503.1 KB, 173.1 KB gzipped), where main measured 547 516 (534.7 KB, 185.6 KB gzipped): 31.6 KB less. Before a face draws, with what it fetches as it is drawn, the scene is 876.3 KB and the pages 806.2 KB, each smaller than before. The budgets fall to 554.0 KB (191.5 KB gzipped) and 513.5 KB handed a compiled app (177.5 KB gzipped), which keeps 10 KB of room for the five features landing beside this one (FR-171, FR-172, FR-173, FR-176, FR-178, about 8 KB together), and a base is never edited upward again: a change that spends more files a raise in `scripts/budget-raises/` naming `"a hosted page"` or `"a hosted page handed a compiled app"`, the bytes it adds and the changeset that ships it, and that changeset says the kilobytes it adds up front ("1.2 KB"), or the test and the pull request's changeset check fail. `pnpm verify hosted` says the gzipped weight beside the minified, and docs/hosted-page.md carries both. Fetched all together, every face's chunks are 4 063 / 3 665 bytes more for the chunks the moved files split into, the studio handed in 1 816 / 2 926, the pages face alone 411 more gzipped (2 637 fewer minified), the guest host before a worker 282 / 288 and registering a worker view 223 / 639, each raised by a file of its own. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-179.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document and compiled formats, check codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Breaking for an import: the names above are no longer exported from `@graview/core`, `@graview/layout/view` or `@graview/guest/host`, and are imported from the entries named (their types — `ReadableField`, `FieldSection`, `ArgShape`, `AppPlace`, `Beginning`, `Hit`, `SearchResult` and the rest — stay where they were). What Graview Cloud does: import `placesOf` from `@graview/core/seat`, and `mountGuestView`, `createGuestHost` or `readTheme` from `@graview/guest/host/frame`, if it uses them; its shell's `guestView` import is unchanged and now carries the frame's host only when a guest view is drawn.
- 7da616c: The store's own bookkeeping tells a seat nothing of a record it may not see, the last three known limits of FR-55. An act ran under its caller's sight, but three of its reads still read the whole store and told a seat a hidden record was there; each is now held by a world-with and world-without comparison. An id minted from a label for a kind the seat may not see every record of is the seat's own — `room:green.person-gus`, which no other seat's label mints — stepping only past what it sees, where `room:green-2` used to step past a room it could not see; one that would land on a hidden record anyway takes a suffix no guess collides with. A seat that may not see every record may not ask for the id of one it makes: it is refused alike, reason `invalid` ("… makes its own id here"), whether a hidden record holds the id or nothing does, where it was refused "already taken" for a hidden one and made for an absent one. An act that sets a single-source link (`setSingleSource`) writes the new link before taking the old ones, so one that takes a hidden record's link is served to the seat whole, with what it may see, as it would be with no such link. A seat that sees every record of a kind mints and asks for ids as before.
  
  Compatibility: ops unchanged in shape; `setSingleSource` emits its `add-edge` before the `remove-edge`s it replaces, so a new op lists its primitives in that order. A create by a seat that may not see every record of the kind mints `<kind>:<slug>.<seat slug>` instead of `<kind>:<slug>`, and its `id` argument is refused as `invalid` whatever holds it; `docs/stability.md` no longer lists the three as known limits.

## 0.1.28

### Patch Changes

- 085670c: Named edits for the people kind, grants and sights (FR-170). `add-kind` and `add-act` passed `people: true` and `$me` through, but nothing set `people` on a kind already there and no edit touched the policy, so an own-end grant or a sight via a relation could only be made by a raw JSON patch, which a builder's form or a chat's `propose_change` cannot offer as a named change previewed in words. `editDocument` takes five more ops. `set-people` (`{ "kind": "participant", "people": true }`) says which kind is the people who sign in, and refuses a second one with a sentence as `people-ambiguous`, so a move is `false` on the one, then `true` on the other, in one change. `add-grant` and `add-sight` take a grant or a sight as the policy holds one (`roles`, `mutations` and `kinds`, `self`, `ownEnd`, `describe`; `roles`, `kinds` and `own`, `via`): a role the document's `roles` does not list joins it, a first grant says that from now on only what a grant gives is allowed, and a first sight on an app with no policy comes with a grant to everyone of every act, since a sight alone would take every act away. `remove-grant` and `remove-sight` name the one to remove by what it says, never by its place in the list: a grant by its roles and acts, a sight by its roles and kinds, each in any order, any other key given matching too; two that match are refused, asking for the key that tells them apart, and one that matches nothing is refused with the ones there are, in words. Each says what it did in the declaration's words — "A participant may "Change the participant" on participants, only on their own record", "A participant sees matches, only those "givenBy" joins to their own participant" — and `diffDocuments` previews the change in the same sentences, where a policy changed was "Who may do what changes.". What the change leaves the people and the policy saying together is judged once it is whole, by the checker's own codes at its last such edit: `people-unsaid`, `sight-via-unknown-edge`, `sight-via-misses-people` and `grant-own-end-without-link`. The Secret Santa's and the idea board's people, grants and sights are each made from nothing by these edits alone, equal to their fixtures. The studio says a grant, a sight and the people kind it changed as these ops, where a grant or a sight was a `studio-unsaid` finding and a kind made the people was not said at all. And a creating act's line leaves out a sentence held in a string, as it leaves out a long text: "Add a feature: Named edits for the people kind, grants and sights (framework, building, 0.1.28)" where the feature's `layerWhy` stood in the parentheses; a value said in over 40 characters, or words ending in a stop, is not said again. A hosted page's first load is 103 bytes smaller (587 155 bytes over the vendors document, 587 258 on main), and 104 smaller handed a compiled app: the new line is paid for by saying a list in words in one place (`listed`). The studio and every face carry about 9 kB more minified, the edits and their sentences. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-170.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-170. The edit vocabulary (`EDIT_OPS`) gains `set-people`, `add-grant`, `remove-grant`, `add-sight` and `remove-sight`, and an edit's refusal may now carry `people-ambiguous`, `people-unsaid`, `sight-via-unknown-edge`, `sight-via-misses-people`, `grant-own-end-without-link`, `grant-act` or `sight-kind` as its code where every refusal was `edit`. `DocumentDiff`'s sentences say a grant, a sight, a role and the people kind in words where a change to the policy was "Who may do what changes." (still said of one they do not cover). `documentEdits` in the studio returns these ops where it returned a `studio-unsaid` finding for a grant or a sight. The line a creating document act is logged with leaves out a value said in over 40 characters or as words ending in a stop. What a host does: a builder's form or a chat offers the five ops by name, and reads `capabilities().shipped` for FR-170 before it sends them to a build that may predate them.
- 10bac84: What FR-170's edits left the older ones doing to a policy, and what the studio and a creating act's line said, reviewed before 0.1.28. `remove-kind` took the kind out of every sight and dropped a sight left showing nothing, so removing the kind the last sights showed took the sights away, and every other kind came to be seen by everyone, which the edit never said: it is now refused, saying "every sight shows only matches, so with them gone everyone would see everything", and `remove-sight` first, in the same change, says it in words. It also took a grant on the kind's derived edit or remove with it no more, so removing the people kind of the Secret Santa left a grant naming "edit-participant" and a change the check refused (`grant-unknown-mutation`): those acts now go from every grant, and a grant left naming no act, or no kind where it named some, goes, as a grant whose last act is removed does. A grant on no kind (`kinds: []`) gives nothing, since the policy matches no kind against it, and was said as a grant on every kind: `add-grant` refuses one, and a document that holds one is said "on no kind, so on nothing". `diffDocuments` says a role taken away ("A participant is no longer a role."), which was unsaid beside any other change to the policy, and a new policy with no grant says nobody but the system may do anything. The studio could not revoke a grant on a kind's derived edit: it has no act for one, so the grant read as naming no act, and revoking it was no edit at all; a grant the document had reads those acts from the document now. And a removal the studio says names what the grant leaves out — `kinds: "*"`, `self: false`, `ownEnd: false`, a sight's `own: false` — so revoking a grant to every kind beside one held to a person's own record no longer means both and is refused. What goes with a removed kind or field is named as a person reads it: "The match kind is removed, and every match with it; so is the act "Draw a match"", where it said "so are act draw", and a rule by its title ("the rule "A booked vendor has a quote"", and a repair of it), where it said the rule's id. A creating act's line said "Acme Holdings Inc." as a sentence and left it out: a stop after an abbreviation ("Inc.", "U.S.") is not a sentence's.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged. `editDocument` refuses `remove-kind` while every sight shows only that kind, and `add-grant` with `kinds: []`, each as `edit`; `remove-kind` takes the kind's `edit-<kind>` and `remove-<kind>` out of every grant, and a grant left with no act or no kind goes. What `remove-kind` and `remove-field` say names an act or a rule that goes with them by its title, where it named its id. `DocumentDiff`'s sentences may say a role taken away, and "on no kind, so on nothing". The studio's `documentEdits` says a removal with `kinds`, `self` and `ownEnd` (a sight's `own`) given. A creating act's line keeps a value ending in an abbreviation's stop. What a host does: a chat removing a kind its last sights show sends `remove-sight` first, in the same change.

## 0.1.27

### Patch Changes

- e404dd1: A document act that creates a record is logged by the record it made. With no subject, the line the log keeps took the first string argument the caller sent, so "Add a feature" was logged as "Add a feature: framework" when the feature's layer came before its name; it now names the new record by its kind's own label.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged. The document format, the compiled format, ops and check finding codes are as they were; only the sentence an op is logged with changes, for a creating act with no subject.
- b228859: A creating act's line in the log says the record as the act sets it. The label it is logged by was read off the arguments alone, so a field left to its default said "—" ("Add a fund: Rainy day (—)"), a literal `$$5 fund` said both dollar signs, and a title worked out by an expression said "—" where the record was called "Holiday fund". The new record's label is now read off its defaults and what the act would write, `$$` as the one `$` it writes and an expression worked out.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged. The document format, the compiled format, ops and check finding codes are as they were; only the sentence an op is logged with changes, for a creating act with no subject whose label reads a default, a `$$` literal or an expression.
- 649dc1f: A document act can say `$me`, so "Vote for it" always votes as you (the Graview Roadmap's f-act-as-me). An act that linked the acting person took them as an argument, so anyone could vote as anyone. `$me` in a `connect`, a `sever` or a `set` is the acting principal's own record of the people kind — the person, for an agent acting for one — and the act's tool no longer asks for it: `{ "connect": "votedBy", "from": "$subject", "to": "$me" }` takes the idea and nothing else. A principal with no record of the people kind is refused with a sentence, reason `refused`: "\"Vote for it\" is done as you, and you have no record here". A TypeScript act reads the same id as `ctx.me`. The checker refuses `$me` in a document that says no people kind (`people-unsaid`, at the act) and `$me` linked where the relation never takes one (`act-me`).
  
  Compatibility: `$me` is no longer an argument's name: a document whose act named an argument `$me` now means the acting person by it, and its tool no longer takes `me`. Every other document compiles and runs as before. The compiled format carries it within `graview-compiled@1`. Check finding codes: `act-me` is new, and `people-unsaid` is said of an act too. Tool names are unchanged; an act's input schema loses an argument only where it said `$me`. Ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. New: `MutationContext.me`. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing, once its member records are of the people kind; an act that took the voter as an argument may say `$me` instead.
- 649dc1f: A document names the kind that stands for its people (the Graview Roadmap's f-people-kind). `"people": true` on one kind says that a record of it whose id is a principal's id is that person — the record Graview Cloud makes for each member under their principal id. A declaration says the same with `people: true` on `defineNode`, and the installation's `user` says it. `self` grants and `own` sights read a principal's id as before; the people kind is what `$me`, a grant held to your own end of a link and a sight via a relation read it as. `describeApp` says it ("The people are the participants: a participant whose id is a member's principal id is that member."), a kind's JSON Schema says `"x-graview-people": true`, and `toDocument` and the studio carry it. `graview check` refuses two kinds that both say it (`people-ambiguous`) and a policy that reads a principal's own record with no kind saying it (`people-unsaid`).
  
  The hosted page pays for what it carries of these four changes up front by reading a grant's and a sight's roles one way, a mutation's subject kinds one way, and a call's named records once in the store: 586,874 bytes up front where main is 586,913, and 545,887 handed a compiled app where main is 545,948.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains `kinds.<kind>.people` within `graview-document@1`; a build that predates it refuses it by path. Check finding codes: `people-ambiguous` and `people-unsaid` are new, errors only about declarations that say something that could not be said before. A kind's JSON Schema gains `x-graview-people` on the people kind. Ops, stored formats, the wire and tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `NodeDefinitionSpec.people`. What Graview Cloud changes: say `"people": true` on the kind its member records are (f-member-records), with each record's id the member's principal id.
- 649dc1f: A grant can hold to your own end of a link (the Graview Roadmap's f-own-end-grants). `self` holds a grant to the act's subject being the principal's own record, and the act that takes back a vote is on the idea, so a voter could take back anyone's. `ownEnd: true` on a grant holds a connect or sever to every record the call names at the far end of the link being the principal's own. The store refuses another with a sentence — "Not permitted: “Take back a vote” on an idea — only on your own end of the link." and the grant's own words — the seat keeps the tool, and the strip offers the act on an idea you voted for with you already chosen, and not at all on one only somebody else voted for. `graview check` refuses the condition on an act that connects and severs nothing, or names no record at its far end (`grant-own-end-without-link`), and asks for a people kind (`people-unsaid`).
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains `policy.grants[].ownEnd` within `graview-document@1`; a build that predates it refuses it by path. Check finding codes: `grant-own-end-without-link` is new, an error only about a grant that could not be written before. A refusal's `reason` is `forbidden`, as any grant's. Tool names and schemas are unchanged; the affordances of an act refused for want of a far end now narrow to the seat's own as they did for a subject. Ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. New: `Grant.ownEnd`, and `permits`'s eighth argument, the records the call names at the far end of what it links.
- 41c0ebc: A proposal to add a record names it by its label, then the rest, and the seat tells the model what the person saw (FR-169). On Graview Cloud's staging, "add a new revenue stream for Graview Cloud SaaS" was offered as "Add a revenue stream: nascent": the line took the first string it was handed, the person read the stream's maturity as its title and said so, and the model, which never saw the line, agreed and asked for another. A document act that creates a record, the `add-<kind>` act the studio writes for a new kind among them, now says the record by its kind's label and then what else it was given, in parentheses, as a record says it: "Add a revenue stream: Graview Cloud SaaS (nascent)", money as money, a yes by the field's words, and neither a no, a long text nor what the label already says. The offer under an answer (`describeProposal`) and the line the log keeps are that one sentence, and a creating act with no label to say is said by its title, never by the first string it was sent. The conversation a seat hands the model carries each offer as the person was shown it, and what became of it: "Here is a plot for it. [Offered beneath this answer: Stake out Back bed — applied]". "Answered with AI" is one quiet mark at the end of the answer's own line, not a line of its own under every answer. A hosted page's first load is 39 bytes smaller (587 280 bytes over the vendors document, 587 319 on main), and 10 bytes smaller handed a compiled app: the new sentence is paid for by a kind's defaults kept once, `isoToday` from one place, and one test for a document too large. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-169.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-169. Only the sentence an op is logged with changes, for a creating document act given more than its label says. `useSeatConversation` takes `said`, how the surface shows one proposal, and the history it hands `answer` says "[Offered beneath this answer: …]" where it said "[applied: …]". What a host does: a surface that calls `useSeatConversation` passes `said: (proposal) => describeProposal(store, proposal)`.
- b228859: A document edit that renames a relation carries the sight that reads it. `rename-relation` moved every act, rule and view that named the relation but not a sight `via` it, so Secret Santa's `givenBy` renamed left the participants' sight reaching a relation nobody declares, and the document no longer compiled. The sight now follows the new name. `remove-relation` is refused while a sight reads the relation, as it is while a lens draws by it — `a sight shows what "givenBy" joins to each seat's own record` — since dropping the sight could show everybody what it kept to each seat's own, and the policy is not an edit's to change.
  
  Compatibility: the document format, the compiled format, ops, stored formats, the wire, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. `editDocument` refuses `remove-relation` on a relation a sight reads, where it used to leave the sight naming a relation that is gone.
- af180f4: A seat's read is bounded, and a read it already made is not sent again (FR-167). One turn in an app of 30 components, 28 skills and 84 strengths ran seven steps whose input grew from 5.6k to 91k tokens: `get_graph` answered the whole graph, about 28,000 tokens, the model asked for it three more times, and every step resent all of it, $0.60 for one question. Now no read answers more than `READ_CHARS` (8,000) characters of JSON, for a seat's turn and an MCP client alike, since both call the same runtime. `get_graph` answers a page of at most `READ_PAGE` (25) records with the edges out of them, `total`, and `more`: "and 117 more nodes: call get_graph with offset 25 for the next page." `get_node` answers a record's fields and its links as `{ kind, id, label }`, `READ_LINKS` (20) each way a page, never the linked records' own fields, with "and 24 more links in: call get_node with id "component-1" and offset 20 for the next page." `search_graph` answers at most `READ_HITS` (20) hits whatever `limit` asked, without every match's id, and "and 65 more records match: narrow the words, or add kind:<kind>." `get_violations` answers `READ_PROBLEMS` (20) a page. A text longer than `READ_TEXT` (600) characters is cut and says "… (cut at 600 of 5000 characters)", and any other read too long to fit, a place described in full, answers `{ cut, partial }`, the cut saying how long it was and to ask for less. Within one turn of a host's model, a call with the same name and input as an earlier one, in any order of its keys, is answered `{ already: "Already read: see the answer to call 1 of this turn, which is above." }` and counts toward the turn's twelve calls, so a model asking the same thing over and over is stopped as before. The bounds are exported from `@graview/tools` as named constants. The reads are a chunk of their own, fetched with the first read: a page whose seat is never asked carries none of them, the scene face before it draws is 742 bytes smaller and a hosted page's first load is unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-167.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-167. Tool names are unchanged; the read tools' answers, titles, descriptions and three input schemas moved, so a surface's hash moves with them. `get_graph` takes `offset`, is titled "Read the graph", and answers `{ nodes, edges, total, more? }` a page at a time where it answered every node and edge. `get_node` takes `offset` and answers `out` and `in` as `{ kind, id, label }` links where it answered whole edges, with `more` when there are more links or problems. `get_violations` takes `offset` and answers `{ violations, total, more? }` where it answered an array. `search_graph` answers at most 20 hits, with `more`, and no longer `matched`. What a host or an agent does: read `get_violations`' `violations`, follow `more` with `offset`, and read a link's `id` where it read an edge's `to` or `from`.
- 649dc1f: A sight can reach the records that link to you (the Graview Roadmap's open request "Visibility scoped by relation"). `sees` kept whole kinds, and `own` showed a seat every record any relation joins to its own — so a Secret Santa match, joined to its giver and its receiver, was shown to both. `{ "roles": ["participant"], "kinds": ["match"], "via": "givenBy" }` shows a seat a match only where `givenBy` joins it, at either end, to the seat's own record of the people kind: each giver sees their own match and not the one they receive in. It holds wherever sight is honored — a seat's graph and its counts, names, search, what a place says, the served log, the wire's state, polls and export, an answer to a seat, a seat's problems and its tools — and moves as the link does. A declaration says it as `Sight.via`; the studio carries it. `graview check` refuses a relation no kind declares (`sight-via-unknown-edge`), one that never joins the kind to the people (`sight-via-misses-people`) and a policy that says one with no people kind (`people-unsaid`). `packages/core/tests/document/fixtures/secret-santa.gdd.json` is the worked example.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains `policy.sees[].via` within `graview-document@1`; a build that predates it refuses it by path. Check finding codes: `sight-via-unknown-edge` and `sight-via-misses-people` are new, errors only about a sight that could not be written before. A seat is served what it was before under every sight a declaration could already say. Ops, stored formats, the wire's shapes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `Sight.via`.
- b228859: An act done as you is undone only by you. An undo is judged as the act it takes back, asked again for whoever undoes it, and "Vote for it" says `$me`, so everybody may run it: Bo could take back Ann's vote by undoing it, which the idea board's own grant ("A vote is taken back only by whoever cast it") refuses him directly. An op whose act wrote its author's own record where its call never named it — a document act's `$me`, or `ctx.me` in TypeScript — is now undone only by that person, their agent or the system; anybody else is refused with a sentence: `Not permitted to undo "Vote for it: A picnic": it was done as somebody else.`
  
  Compatibility: an undo by another seat of an op made as its author is now refused; every other undo is judged as before. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document and compiled formats, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged.
- af180f4: A model's last step that answers nothing says the app's own reading, as a failed turn does (FR-168). A turn whose last step answered one output token was shown "The answer came back in a shape I could not read. Ask again in other words." though the app had read the ask and had a proposal ready. Now an answer that is empty, only whitespace, JSON that does not parse, or JSON that says nothing and proposes nothing is no answer: the seat says what the app read, its proposals with it, and the failed turn's aside, "(AI didn't answer just now, so this is from the app alone.)", and nothing is marked as answered with AI. A model's prose is still its answer. `llmResponder` on its own, with no floor to fall back on, still says the shape sentence, and now marks it `unsure`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-168.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-168. A reply `llmResponder` could not read carries `unsure: true`. What a host does: nothing.
- b228859: A grant held to your own end of a link holds every link the act touches, not only the one it names. `ownEnd` read the records a call names at the far end of its link, and a connect on a relation of one replaces the link that was there, so "Lead it" with yourself named took the idea from whoever led it — breaking their end of a link nothing granted you. The store now holds what the act would do as well: every link it makes or breaks has the seat's own record at one end, or the call is refused with the grant's sentence, "Not permitted: “Lead it” on an idea — only on your own end of the link.", and the strip no longer offers it. A seat with no id of its own is no longer offered an act only an own-end grant allows. `graview check`'s `sight-via-misses-people` says its sentence in fewer words, and `grant-own-end-without-link`'s fix for an act with no record at its far end says to take one as an argument or say `$me` there.
  
  Compatibility: a call an own-end grant allowed that replaced or broke somebody else's link is now refused; every call that touched only the seat's own is allowed as before. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document and compiled formats, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged.

## 0.1.26

### Patch Changes

- cf22161: A host's turn that fails says why in the host's own words (FR-166). The seat caught a failed model step and wrote "(AI didn't answer just now, so this is from the app alone.)" whatever had happened, so a refused key, a rate limit and a step the host would not read all read the same, and the sentence a host threw to explain it never reached the reader. Now a plain `Error` a host's `turn` or `complete` throws is carried in the aside: "(AI didn't answer just now: The model is busy right now; try again in a moment. This is from the app alone.)". Only a plain `Error` is said: a `TypeError` from a fetch that never left, and every other kind, is the machine's and keeps the old aside. The sentence is one line of at most 160 characters, with its brackets taken out so the aside stays whole. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-166.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-166. What a host does: throw an `Error` whose message is a sentence a reader may see when its turn or completion cannot answer; throw anything else to keep it to yourself.
- 8b7862d: A file field's editor adds to what the field holds when the host's upload answers, not to what it held when the file was sent, so a picture somebody else put on the record meanwhile is kept (FR-163). A file the field already holds is said ("“porch.jpg” is already here.") and not held twice; several files given to a field that holds one say which were left out ("Photos holds one picture, so “b.png” was left out."); and when a list's file is moved earlier or removed, the keyboard stays in the editor — on the moved file, the next file's Remove, or Choose pictures — rather than on the page. Removing a record counts each of its files once, however many of its fields hold it ("Remove Fix the roof and its 2 files").
  
  Compatibility: unchanged. A derived `remove-<kind>`'s sentence counts a file two fields hold once, where it counted it twice; tool names and input schemas are unchanged.
- 26dbe52: A picture or a document is put on a record from the page, through the host's own storage (FR-163). A host hands `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` or the Shell `files: { upload(file: Blob, name: string): Promise<FileValue>; maxBytes: number; kinds: readonly string[] }` (`HostFiles`), and `PagesApp` takes it in its context; `upload` stores the bytes and answers the value the record keeps. A file field's editor is drawn on every face where the record's values are changed — the scene's record, the routed record page, the routed face's forms and the strip's ask — and a field that holds nothing yet is drawn there too when an act may write it. It takes files picked, a photo taken (`<input capture>`, offered where the pointer is a finger), files dropped and files pasted, several at once for a list. A camera's photograph, and a JPEG or WebP bigger than a page needs, is drawn again in the page no larger than 2,048 pixels before it is sent (`resample`, which `downscale` now uses too); a HEIC the browser can read is sent as a JPEG, one it cannot read is sent as it is where the host takes HEIC and refused in words where it does not. What the host takes is said before anything is sent ("or drop or paste them here · pictures and PDF files, up to 10 MB"), and what it refuses is said in the editor's words: "“big.png” is 14 MB; this host takes files up to 10 MB.", "“quote.pdf” is a PDF file; this host takes pictures.", or the message `upload` threw ("“porch.png” wasn’t kept: the disk is full"). What is kept becomes the ordinary act that sets the field, which the room records and undo takes back, and a list's files may be given a caption, moved earlier and removed. Without `files` the editor says "This host doesn’t take files, so none can be added here." and offers no way to add one.
  
  For development and the harnesses, `@graview/ship/dev` has a door that keeps files in memory and serves them back (`devFiles()`, a Vite plugin, and `devFilesHandler`; `/__graview/files`, same-origin only, never in a build), and `@graview/ship/browser` has `filesAt(url, { maxBytes, kinds })`, the `files` a page hands that sends to such a door, or to a host's route that answers the same. The todo example's tasks take attachments through them under `pnpm dev`. `scripts/verify-files.mjs` drives it on both faces in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged by the seam itself. New: `HostFiles` from `@graview/core`; `files` on `FrameOptions` (so `mount` and `<Embed>`), `GraviewProviderProps`, `ShellProps` and `PageContext`; `HostFilesContext` and `useHostFiles` from `@graview/react` and `/provider`; `filesAt` from `@graview/ship` and `/browser`; `devFiles`, `devFilesHandler`, `DEV_FILES_PATH`, `DEV_FILES_KINDS` and `DEV_FILES_MAX_BYTES` from `@graview/ship/dev`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-163. What Graview Cloud changes: it passes the `files` its route already answers to `mount`.
- 26dbe52: A form, a field read as a document says it, and what can be done are entries of their own, so a page that has drawn none of them carries none of them. A bundler places a whole file in a page's first chunk when the first chunk can reach it and any chunk uses it, and three did: `formFields` and the rest of a form's reading rode in every hosted page's first load through `@graview/core`, though only a drawn form, the strip's ask and a seat read them; a field read in a document's words (`fieldSpecOf`) rode in it through `@graview/core/document`, though only a face's blocks and the checker read it; and the routed face reached `deriveAffordances` through `@graview/tools`, which carried the chat, the agent's tool surface and zod's JSON Schema into the pages face's first load. Now:
  
  - `formFields`, `formField`, `formComplete`, `formArgs`, `undecidableArguments` and the types `FormField`, `ScalarField` and `UndecidableArgument` are `@graview/core/forms`', and no longer `@graview/core`'s;
  - `fieldSpecOf` is `@graview/core/blocks`', beside `fieldSpecsOf`, and no longer `@graview/core/document`'s; `validateViewSpecs`, the checker's reading of a declaration's view specs, has a module of its own and is still `/document`'s;
  - `@graview/tools/derive` carries `deriveAffordances`, `applyAffordance`, `previewAffordance` and `defaultProviders`, which `@graview/tools` still exports too, and the routed face reads them there.
  
  What that buys, with a record's files (FR-162, FR-163) in the same pages: the hosted page carries 1,609 bytes fewer up front (587,604 where main is 589,213) and 280 fewer handed a compiled app (546,632 where main is 546,912); the pages face alone loads 26,338 bytes fewer first (505,306 where main is 531,644; 10,782 fewer gzipped), and the hosted page's routed face 25.7 KB fewer before it draws (834,347). The embed with the studio handed in loads 4,965 more first (1,571,334; the value's check, the file field's door on every face and the checker reading a field as a document says it), and every face 18,725 more in all, 12,787 of it the files' own chunk, fetched with the first file field drawn; both budgets are raised by that, measured.
  
  Compatibility: breaking for an import, and nothing else.
