Ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged, and so is what each name does. What a host does: import `formFields` and the rest of a form from `@graview/core/forms`, and `fieldSpecOf` from `@graview/core/blocks`; a bundler alias list that names `@graview/core`'s subpaths names `@graview/core/forms` and `@graview/tools/derive` before the bare names (`graview create` writes both). Graview Cloud imports none of the moved names.
- 26dbe52: A record can hold a picture or a document (FR-162). A document's field may be `{"type": "image"}`, `{"type": "file"}`, or a `list` with `"of": "image"` or `"of": "file"`; a declaration says the same with `imageField()` and `fileField()` from `@graview/core` (`photo: imageField().optional()`, `papers: z.array(fileField())`), which `toDocument` writes as those types. The value is what the host stored, `FileValue`: `{ href, name, type, bytes, width?, height?, caption? }`, its `href` a path on the host under its base (`/graview/files/3f9a….jpg`, as Graview Cloud answers, or one relative to the page). A value whose `href` has a scheme of any sort (`javascript:`, `data:`, `https:`), names another host (`//…`), holds a backslash or climbs (`..`, `.`, `%2e`) is refused, by the store, a form, a seat's call and an import alike (`isFileValue`, `safeFileHref`): a record is written by acts, seats and imports as well as by an upload, and a `data:` picture would put the bytes in the log, where they do not belong.
  
  A file field is drawn wherever the record is drawn. On a card in the scene and a row of a list on the routed face, the record's first picture is drawn small; on its page and in the scene's record, its pictures are a gallery, each opening full size in a dialog that gives the keyboard back to the picture it opened from; a document is said by its name, its type and its size, a PDF shown inline and anything else a download. Every picture says what it is: its caption, else its name. A view's blocks gained `{ "image": "photos", "as": "thumb" | "gallery", "label"? }` for a card, a row, a page or a blocks lens; the check refuses one that names no image or file field (`view-image`) and an `as` it does not know (`view-format`). What draws them is fetched with the first file field drawn, never in the first paint.
  
  A file's value is a value like any other: set by an act and said in the history by its names ("Change Fix the roof: photos → porch.jpg, gutter.jpg"), undone, exported and remixed as any field, found by its name and its caption, and written by `graview types` as `FileValue` (or `readonly FileValue[]`). A change that leaves files no record holds says which: removing a record says "Remove Fix the roof and its 3 files", and a migration's plan lists them (`MigrationPlan.files`) and names them in its words. What becomes of the bytes is the host's to decide. An agent's tools describe a file argument as the object it is, and a seat may set one to a value the host gave.
  
  The hosted page carries none of it up front that it did not pay for: 587,604 bytes up front where main is 589,213, and 546,632 handed a compiled app where main is 546,912 (see the changeset that moved the forms and the field reading to entries of their own).
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field types `image` and `file`, `of: "image" | "file"` on a list, and the `image` block, within `graview-document@1`; a build that predates them refuses them by path. The compiled format carries such a field within `graview-compiled@1`; a page that predates it refuses the compiled app (`compiled-shape`) and compiles the document, which refuses the type by path. Check finding codes: `view-image` is new, an error only about a block that could not be written before. Tool names are unchanged; the input schema of an `edit-<kind>` (or any act) whose kind has a file field describes it as an object. Ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. New: `FileValue`, `imageField`, `fileField`, `isFileValue`, `safeFileHref`, `filesOf`, `isImage`, `fileKindOf` from `@graview/core`; `fileFieldsOf`, `firstPicture` and `fileWords` from `@graview/core/blocks`; `ReadableField.files`; `ArgShape`'s `{ type: "file", image? }` and `FormField`'s `control: "file"`; `MigrationPlan.files`; `Files` from `@graview/primitives` and `/pages`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-162. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing until it writes a file field; then it reads `capabilities().shipped` for FR-162 first, and serves each `href` it answers to whoever may read the record.

## 0.1.25

### Patch Changes

- d0eb455: A phone's seat is put away by its ✕, a tap on its grab line or a pull down, and the keyboard goes down with it (FR-164). On a phone the open seat is a bottom sheet whose grab line read as something to pull or tap, and neither seemed to do anything: there was no ✕ (only a desk had one), nothing followed a finger, and a tap did close the sheet but put the keyboard back in the ask field, so on a real phone the on-screen keyboard stayed up and the next letter opened the sheet again. The field's 15px text also made iOS Safari zoom the page when it was focused. Now the phone's sheet has the desk's ✕ beside its grab line; its top row follows a finger down and puts the sheet away past 64px, or on a flick of 0.5px a millisecond over 16px, settling back short of both; closed on a phone, the keyboard goes back to where it was only if that is not a field, and otherwise to the seat's region, never the field and never `<body>`; the field's text is never under 16px; and the words typed and not sent, and where the talk was scrolled to, are still there when it is opened again. An open sheet still comes back open after a reload, since the conversation is the tab's, but with no field focused and its ✕ in sight it no longer traps the reader. The swipe is in the seat's lazy chunk: what the hosted page loads up front does not change. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-164.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; the hosted page's first load is the same size.
- 21365f6: A kind's JSON Schema and an act's tool schema are `@graview/core/json-schema`'s. `mutationToolSchema`, `toJsonSchema`, `nodeJsonSchema`, `schemaJson` and their types were exported from `@graview/core`, and a bundler places a whole module in every chunk that can reach it: only a seat's tools and the CLI derive a tool schema, yet every hosted page carried the module in its first load. They are gone from `@graview/core`; import them from `@graview/core/json-schema`. The hosted page's first load is 1,278 bytes smaller, which pays for the measured screen height (FR-165).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. The four functions and two types leave `@graview/core` for `@graview/core/json-schema`.
- 21365f6: An app fills the phone after it turns (FR-165). The embed took the height it was mounted with once, the whole-page Shell was `100vh`, the pages' seat stood in a fixed `100dvh`, and the scene passed over a box reported as nothing; on a phone turned to landscape and back, the app was drawn in the top half of the screen and stayed so, on both faces. `mount(element, { height: "viewport" })` now measures the screen itself: `innerHeight`, read again on `resize`, `orientationchange` and the visual viewport's `resize`, and once more a beat later for the phones that report a turn late. A height in viewport units — `100dvh`, `calc(100svh - 48px)`, the fallback inside a `var()` — is measured the same way, each `vh`, `dvh`, `svh` and `lvh` read as a hundredth of that figure. The Shell's height and the pages' seat follow the same figure (`useViewportHeight`, from `@graview/primitives`, `/frame` and `/pages`), and the scene reads its box again on every `resize`, so a box that comes back from nothing is followed. The on-screen keyboard does not shrink the app: the figure is the layout viewport's, which the keyboard and a pinch leave alone, so a lens is not laid out again for half a screen while somebody types; the browser scrolls the field into view. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-165.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. `mount`'s `height` takes `"viewport"`, and a viewport unit in it is now measured rather than left to the browser.

## 0.1.24

### Patch Changes

- 6dbad14: A box nobody ticked is a no. A form that makes something with a required yes-or-no — "Induction first" on a tool — sent nothing for the box when it was left unticked, and the act refused "expected boolean, received undefined"; `formArgs` now sends it as `false`, as it sends a list nobody added to as `[]`. The derived form no longer marks a box "*", as if it had to be ticked. (W-177, the eighth walk.)
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged. Tool names and input schemas are unchanged; what a derived form sends for an untouched required box is now `false` where it was nothing.
- e23f631: A fact in a rule's line is said by the words it is shown by. A rule that read `fact('proposed')` said it as "fact (proposed)" — "Nothing proposed — fact (proposed) 3 ≥ 1". Now a fact is said by its `label`, or, with none, by its name in words, as a field is: "proposals in play 3 ≥ 1", "open work 4 ≥ 2".
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; the hosted page's first load is the same (the words are `@graview/core/lines`'). A rule's line, and the message of a rule with no sentence of its own, read differently where they say a fact.
- 9fe145e: A host's turn knows the app the seat is mounted with, so `describe_place` describes a lens place. Inside `HostAi.turn` the seat built its read tools from the store alone, and the chat's own runtime did the same, so a model asking `describe_place` for a lens or the declared home was told "is not a place this app has": the app never reached the seat. `GraviewProvider` now takes `app` and holds it on its context (`useGraview().app`), inheriting the app of a provider it is drawn inside; `mount` and `<Embed>` pass theirs on both faces, and `PagesApp`'s `context.app` reaches the provider under the pages. The chat hands it to its own tool runtime, to the workbench's agent seat and, as `ChatContext.app`, to a host's turn, whose `describe_place` now answers exactly as the runtime `graview mcp` serves does, for the seat that asked. With `app` given, a provider's reader `settings` default to `app.settings`. `graview create` writes `app` on the provider and on the pages' context. The hosted page carries 132 bytes fewer up front (589 625; 589 757 before), and as many fewer handed a compiled app (547 324; 547 456): the embed now hands the provider each option as given, which the provider's props accept as `undefined`, rather than spreading each one in only when it is set.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `app` on `GraviewProviderProps`, `GraviewContextValue`, `PageContext` and `ChatContext`. Changed: `GraviewProviderProps`' `principal`, `seats`, `people`, `memory`, `presenceTtlMs`, `presence`, `brand`, `onKeepLens` and `ai` also accept `undefined`, which means the same as leaving them out. What a host does: one that mounts its own `GraviewProvider` passes `app={yourApp}` (and `app` in `PagesApp`'s context) so its seat and its turn can describe the app's lenses and home; `mount` and `<Embed>` need nothing.
- 56f203f: A record deleted here that the remote edited is a conflict, not a delete that wins. Under `conflicts: "report"` the sync engine held a field both sides changed, but a record deleted here was sent as a delete whatever the remote had done to it since they last agreed, so somebody's edit there was thrown away without a word. Now an inbound change to a record deleted here that changes what both sides agreed is a conflict like a field's, and goes where the policy says. `report` holds it — `{ localId, deleted: true, theirs, base }`, with `theirs` the remote's record in our field names — and the delete is not sent until `resolve` chooses: `"ours"` sends the delete on the next run; `"theirs"` makes the record again with the remote's values through `createInbound`, authored by the system, and links what it made to the remote's record at the version of that edit. `theirs` does the same in the pass that hears the edit, and `ours` sends the delete in it; the report's `resolved` says each. Without `createInbound`, taking theirs lets the remote keep its record and leaves this one deleted and unlinked, sending nothing. A record already held in conflict and then deleted here is held as a deleted record; one whose delete is taken back is held field by field against the remote's record, never sent over it; one the remote then deletes too is nobody's to choose and is not said `gone`. A record projected with a version and deleted here before the engine was made is linked as it was projected, so its delete is sent (or held), where it was never sent.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; the hosted page's first load is the same to the byte. Changed in `@graview/core/sync`: `SyncConflict` is `FieldConflict | DeletedConflict` (both new exports), so `field` and `ours` are absent on a deleted record's conflict; `SettledConflict` is that union with `took`; `SyncConflictOptions.argsFor` is handed a `FieldConflict`, and `syncConflictInvariant` passes over a deleted record's conflict; `SyncState.conflicts` may carry a deleted record's; `createInbound` is also called to make a record again.
- cfe3b7a: A name is called a name. A kind's `label` field is said "Name" wherever a field is said — `fieldWords`, the derived edit's line in the log ("Change Woodworking: name → “Woodshop”", which said `label`) and its refusal — as the form already asked for it. (W-184, the eighth walk.)
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; a derived edit's intent says "name" where it said "label".
- e23f631: A removal whose cascade takes a link to a record its seat may not see is still the seat's own. Removing a room that a hidden hold pointed at took the hold's link with it, the link named the hold, and the seat was served its own op withheld — "A change you cannot see" — and could not undo it, which told it a hidden link had been there. Now a link the seat is not served is no part of an op that did anything else: it is left out of the op's primitives and of its inverse, and the op is judged without it. So the seat is served its removal whole, exactly as it would be with no such link; it may undo it, the undo puts the hidden link back and is served to it the same way, and so is the redo, or somebody else's undo of it. An op that did nothing but such links is withheld as before. `docs/stability.md` now says so, and names the three things a hidden record still changes for an act, as known limits: a create asking for an id a hidden record holds is refused "Id … is already taken", a minted id steps past a hidden record's (`room:green-2`), and an act that sets a single-source link replaces a hidden record's link and is served withheld. The hosted page loads 32 bytes less up front (589,213 where main is 589,245), and 26 less handed a compiled app (546,912 where it is 546,938).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. What a seat is served changes: an op whose only hidden part is a link the seat is not served arrives whole, with that link left out of its primitives and inverse, where it arrived withheld. Its log still folds to exactly the snapshot it is served.
- 108721d: A rule that reads a fact is judged, and worded, over what the seat sees. A document's fact (`fact('proposed')`) counts the whole graph, and a rule that read one was judged over the whole graph and its sentence said there, so a guest who sees one proposal was told "3 proposed, and at most two may be". A seat's problems are now judged over its sighted view, its lines and its facts with it, wherever it reads them: `seenBy(principal).violations()`, and so a seat's tools (`get_violations`, `get_node`), the store's reading for a served seat and a page's seat, which now agree with a page served only what its seat sees. A record the seat may not see never makes, mends or words one of its problems. An act's answer to a seat (`answerSeenBy`) gives what the act broke, and what stands after it, as the seat judges them once it is made; what it mended, and what a preview would do, were judged over the whole graph, and a rule there that reads beyond its subject (a fact, a sweep, a relation) is said by its title. `store.violations(context, over)` judges over another reading of the store's graph. `graview check` warns of a rule whose message says a fact over a kind some role told of the rule may not see in full (`rule-says-hidden-fact`), and the `graview-permissions` skill says what it means. The hosted page loads 30 bytes less up front (589,727), the compiled entry 36 less (547,420); the every-face budget is raised for chunks fetched later only.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Check finding codes: additive, `rule-says-hidden-fact` is a new warning, so a declaration that passed still passes. What a seat is told changes: where a policy keeps records from a seat, its problems are those its own view breaks, so a rule broken only by records it may not see is no longer reported to it, and one its view breaks that the records it may not see keep is; a preview's problems from a rule that reads beyond its subject are said by the rule's title. A store with no sights, and the system's seat, read exactly what they read before.
- e23f631: What an act broke and mended is told to a seat as the seat judges it, over its own world. `answerSeenBy` took the whole graph's judgment of a change and, where a rule reads beyond its subject (a fact, a sweep, a relation), said what it mended, and a preview's problems, by the rule's title: a guest who previews proposing its draft was told "too many proposed", and one who withdraws its one proposal was told it mended that, though by what it sees there were never too many — so each was told about records it may not see. Now all three of a change's lists are judged over the seat's own world: what it is served now, and that world with the change as it is served taken back (a change made, or undone) or put on (a preview). What it broke and mended is the difference, each in the seat's own words and lines, and nothing in them moves for a record it may not see. The judging is `@graview/core/lines`', which a seat's tools load before they answer; without it an answer to a seat says no problems at all. `answeredTo` is gone from `@graview/core/lines` (`judgedForASeat` does this), and the engine's lines hook no longer takes a fourth argument.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; the hosted page's first load is smaller (see the removal's changeset). What a seat is told changes: where a policy keeps records from a seat, a preview's problems and what a change mended are the seat's own judgment, with lines, never a rule's title over the whole graph.
- 129f2fa: A type lives where it is said first, so six of the import cycles that were left only in types are gone. The kit's types (`Kit`, `ConnectorKit`, `KitOverrides` and the rest) are the theme's, beside the `Brand` that carries them; `PagesArrangement` is the view registry's, which arranges by it; `Finding` and `Severity` are the check context's, where a finding is added; `validateScaffoldOptions` asks only the options a name can be wrong in; `settleAt` asks only the embed's options it reads; the seat's `SeatMove` and `SeatOffer` are the conversation's, whose replies carry them (`@graview/tools/go` still says both); and `KeptLens` is the reader's lenses'. Every name is exported from the same entry as before. Three are left, each for a reason: a `Store` is declared beside the store it describes; the document's views and its description of a place are being changed by this round's other work.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, and tool names and input schemas are unchanged — only where a type is declared moved, and every entry exports what it did; the hosted page's first load is the same to the byte.
- 5b7e4c8: A yes or no the kind words itself is said whole. A member's `inducted`, declared as "Inducted" or "Not yet inducted", read "Inducted: not yet inducted" on every card and row; a boolean with a declared `format` is now said in those words alone, and a bare one still with its label ("Explicit: yes"). (W-187, the eighth walk.)
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged.
- b68d1cc: An act runs under its caller's sight. The store refused a call naming a record its seat may not see exactly as one naming nothing, but an act's own body read the whole store: a guard that counted holds on a room refused a guest when a hold it could not see was there and let the booking through when there was none, so the guest learned the hold existed — and a page on a served app, which had shown the booking from the guest's view, took it back after the host's second answer. Now an act's body, its condition, its refusal and its sentence read the store as its author is served it (`seenBy`), so a hidden record is to the act what an absent one is: whether it runs, what it refuses with and why, what it writes and what problems it answers with are the same for both, on the tool runtime, `graview serve` and a page alike, and the page's guess is the host's. What it writes still lands on the whole store, and the store's own bookkeeping reads the whole store — a removed record's links and fields, a patch's prior values, a single source's other links, an id already taken — so nothing hidden is lost or overwritten. A call naming a record withheld from its seat whole (for a required field that names one it may not see) is now refused as missing too, before the act runs, and a seat's view's `wouldChange` and `preview` ask as that seat. The sentence "“<act>” could not be done as asked.", which hid an act's refusal worded from a hidden record, is gone: no act can word one now. `graview check`'s `act-reads-hidden-kind` says what the act does instead: run by that role, it judges as though there were none. docs/stability.md §3 no longer lists an act's own logic as what sight cannot hide. The hosted page's first load is 472 B smaller.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. What changes is what an act sees when a seat with a sight runs it: an act whose body reads records its author may not see — a TypeScript `apply`, a document act's `allowedWhen`, `refusal`, `sets` or effects — now reads none of them for that author, and so may run where it refused before, or write a count or copy without them. A rule that must hold across records a seat may not see (no double booking) belongs in an invariant, which the store still judges over everything; `graview check` names each act this touches with `act-reads-hidden-kind`. The system, and any seat from whom nothing is kept, is unchanged. `Store#wouldChange` takes the author as an optional second argument.
- 32c54f1: An act that makes something is not guessed to change its subject. A TypeScript act that declares no `writes` is read as writing each argument named like a field of its subject; "Report a fault" acts on a tool and is told the fault's `label`, so it was taken for the act that renames tools — a tool's name changed in place reported a fault by that name, the tool's derived edit left the name out, and every role allowed to report a fault rode that derived edit to change any of the tool's other fields. `fieldsWrittenBy` now reads an act that `creates` something and declares no `writes` as writing nothing on its subject; one that does write its subject says so in `writes`. (W-180, the eighth walk.)
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged. Tool names and input schemas: `edit-<kind>` may take a field it left out before, where the only act told that field's name makes something else and declares no `writes`; such an act no longer permits `edit-<kind>`. What a host does: nothing, unless an act that creates a record also writes its subject without saying so — give it `writes`.
- 56f203f: `mcpClient` reads the reply to its own request. It read the last `data:` line of an event stream as the answer, so a server that sent a notification after the reply (a log message, progress) or a request of its own handed a sync that notification instead, and a reply whose JSON was split across `data:` lines was not read at all. Now the stream is read as the SSE spec reads it — an event ends at a blank line, its `data:` lines join with a newline, one space after the colon is dropped, a line starting `:` is a comment, and `\r\n`, `\r` and `\n` each end a line — and the answer is the message whose JSON-RPC `id` is the request's, with a `result` or an `error`; notifications, the server's own requests and replies to other ids are passed over, and a JSON body that is a batch is read the same way. An answer with no reply to the request is said as such ("The MCP server sent no reply to get_item."), where it was read as an empty result.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; the hosted page's first load is the same to the byte. Changed: `mcpClient` throws for an answer that carries no reply to its request.
- e23f631: A field only the remote changed lands on the next sync, rather than being held as a conflict. Once a delete held against the remote's edit was taken back, each field was held field by field — even one this side never changed, where ours was what both last agreed — so a title the remote edited waited on somebody's `resolve`, a conflict with nothing to choose between. Now `run` lands a held field whose value here is back at what both last agreed: theirs is applied through `applyInbound`, authored by the system, agreed, and said in the report's `applied`, and nothing is sent for it. A field both sides changed is held as before.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; the hosted page's first load is the same (`@graview/core/sync` is an entry of its own). A `SyncState` that holds such a field from an earlier run lands it on the next.
- 8e1f679: The app bar says which kinds hold anything without walking every record. A kind with no records folds into "More" (`barPlaces`), and the bar asked by gathering the kind of every record in the graph each time it drew — on every page's bar, on every change. The graph now keeps how many records of each kind it holds as they come and go — added, removed, loaded, a batch that failed put back, a patch that names the kind — and `Graph.count(kind)` reads it without a walk; a seat's view of the graph (`seenBy`) counts only what that seat is served. Measured on apps/discography's 2,229 records, the bar's places took 0.044 ms a change and take 0.007; at 22,290 records, 0.42 ms and 0.009. Paid for in the same bytes: the hosted page's first load is 22 bytes lighter.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the declaration, the compiled format and tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `Graph.count(kind)`. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing.
- fe08229: The upgrade says why it is not taken back. Asked to undo a migration, the store said it "was done before the declaration changed (migration 1→2 …)", naming the upgrade as what stood in its own way; it now says the upgrade changed the declaration, and that undo does not reach back across that change. (W-185, the eighth walk.)
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives are unchanged — what may be undone is as FR-27 decided, and only the sentence for the upgrade itself is new; stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged.

## 0.1.23

### Patch Changes

- 8f7e998: A host wires its turn from the loop alone: `@graview/tools/turn` (FR-161). `turnThroughMessages` was exported only from the root of `@graview/tools`, and a host that imported it there, as the notes for FR-160 said to, carried the root's agent, providers and the rest in its first paint: Graview Cloud's shell went from 562 KB to 646 KB for forty bytes of wiring. The new entry `@graview/tools/turn` carries `turnThroughMessages`, `TURN_STEPS` and the `MessagesBlock`, `MessagesStepRequest` and `MessagesStepReply` types and nothing else; bundled on its own it reads one module and weighs under 2 KB. The root still exports them. The scaffolder's link-mode aliases and the example apps' name the new entry, and the seat skill says where to import it from.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-161. New: the `@graview/tools/turn` entry. What a host does: import `turnThroughMessages` from `@graview/tools/turn`; a host that imported it from `@graview/tools` on the first ask, to keep it out of the first paint, can import it up front instead.

## 0.1.22

### Patch Changes

- 9df7ef3: A declared block has a stable key, and a face claims a block by it. n-dx's face drew a change's rewrites and the tasks it works on in its own hand and let the declared blocks draw the rest, choosing which by a title's words (`title === "Rewrites"`), so a title said differently dropped a section without a word. Any block in a view — a kind's card, row or page, the home, a blocks lens — may now say `"key": "rewrites"`; one that says none has a key derived from its sort and its place among the blocks of that sort beside it (`list.2`, the second list; `group.1.text.1` inside the first group), never from what it says, and counted whether a sibling names its own or not, so naming one never moves another's. `compileBlocks` keeps each key and `resolveBlocks` carries it on every resolved block, nested ones too. A face claims blocks by key, view by view, with `BlockSlots` from `@graview/primitives`: `<BlockSlots.Provider value={{ "change.page": { rewrites: Timeline } }}>` draws `Timeline` for that block, handed the block worked out, its record and the framework's own drawing of it to wrap or leave out, and the framework draws every other block as declared. A view is named as `describe_place` names it — `home`, `<kind>.page`, `<kind>.card`, `<kind>.row` — so a record a list draws inside a page answers to its own view's claims. A claim may be `lazy`: the framework's drawing stands in until it arrives. `graview check` refuses two blocks of one view under one key, nested or not, saying where the first is, and a key that is not a word or a few joined by hyphens (`view-key`); a seat drafting a view may give its blocks keys too.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the compiled format and tool names and schemas are unchanged. The document format takes `key` on any block, where it was refused as an unknown key, and a declaration's `ViewBlock` types it. New: the check finding code `view-key`, an error; `key` on `SpecBlock` and `ResolvedBlock`; `viewKeys` beside `viewBraces`; `BlockSlots`, `ClaimedSlots`, `BlockSlotProps` and `BlockSlotComponent` on `@graview/primitives`, and `SpecBlocks` takes the `view` it draws. A document that compiled still compiles. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing.
- 0f051ef: A declaration document says its shape to an editor, and a document written in TypeScript is checked as it is typed. n-dx's projection is a 2,020-line JSON document that only `graview check` read: a key the format does not have (`fieldRoles` on a kind) was refused at run time, never underlined where it was written. Now every version of `@graview/core` ships `@graview/core/document.schema.json` (`dist/document.schema.json`), the document format as JSON Schema, which the build writes from the shape the compiler reads (`DocumentSpec`), never by hand. Its `$id` is the version's own published file (`https://unpkg.com/@graview/core@<version>/dist/document.schema.json`). A document that names it, `"$schema": "./node_modules/@graview/core/dist/document.schema.json"`, is checked in the editor as it is typed: every key, type, name pattern and range the compiler reads, every object closed so an unknown key is flagged, and the refinements said again in JSON Schema's words (at least one kind and at most 40, an enum's options and only an enum's, no field called `id` or `kind`). The compiler reads `$schema` and passes over it. A test holds the two together: every document in the tree that the compiler reads, n-dx's among them, validates, and an unknown key on a kind, a field, a relation, an act, a policy or the document fails both.
  
  `defineDocument` from the new `@graview/core/typed` is the same document in TypeScript: it returns the JSON document unchanged, and a key the format does not have, an edge to a kind the document does not declare, a glance, page or lifecycle field its kind lacks, a relation an act connects or severs that no kind declares, a kind an act is on or creates, a kind a rule is over, and an act a repair or a grant names are type errors, each said as the names the document does declare. Expressions and templates are strings still, for `graview check`. n-dx's whole document checks under it in about a second.
  
  The hosted page measures 34 bytes fewer up front (593 573, from 593 607, under its 593 613) and the same handed a compiled app (548 004): the `$schema` key is paid for by the pages arrangement's names saying their lengths through the schema's own helpers.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. The document format gains an optional top-level `$schema` string, which the compiler reads past; every document that compiled still compiles, and one carrying `$schema` no longer refuses it as an unknown key. New: `@graview/core/document.schema.json`, and `defineDocument`, `CheckedDocument` and `DocumentDraft` on `@graview/core/typed`.
- f25b52c: A document is tested from where it lives. n-dx's face tested the document n-dx emits by copying it into `tests/fixtures` and holding the copy to the original with a drift test of its own. `compileDocumentFiles(document, { seed })` from `@graview/core/testing` reads both from their paths (relative to the working directory, or a `file:` URL), compiles the document with the checker — a refusal throws, saying every finding — and hands back the app, the document and seed as read, the checker's findings, and `store()`, a fresh store over the seed with the app's own acts, rules and policy each time it is called.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool schemas are unchanged. Added: `compileDocumentFiles` and `DocumentFiles` on `@graview/core/testing`, which now reads files with `node:fs`; it was, and is, an entry for tests in Node.
- 9154faf: A document can declare facts of the whole graph. `computed` was a kind's only, so n-dx decided "the whole product map is proposed" in its own face with `every(n => n.proposed)`, and the scene, the pages and an agent could not say the same thing. A document's top-level `computed` is a name and a rule-language expression with no record as its subject — `"mapProposed": { "expr": "every(all('area') | all('capability'), proposed == true)", "label": "The map is proposed" }` — read anywhere the language is as `fact('mapProposed')`: a block on the home or a lens, a template, a rule over the graph, a kind's computed field, another fact. It is worked out over the reader's own graph, so what a seat may not see adds nothing to it, from the budget of the expression that reads it, and a fact that cannot be worked out is said where it is read, never thrown. `factValues(schema, graph)` and `factNames(schema)` on `@graview/core/blocks` work them out for a face of a product's own; `describePlace` (and so `describe_place` on the home, over MCP) lists each with its value for the seat; `graview describe` lists each with its expression; a compiled app carries them, `resolveFor` and `blockContextFor` read them as every face does, and the document's JSON Schema and `defineDocument` take them; a TypeScript app says them as `createSchema(kinds, computed)`. `graview check` judges a fact as a computed field — a bare name (no record is there to read it from), an expression that does not parse, a kind or relation nobody declares, work that grows with the square of the graph (a warning) or its cube (refused) — and refuses `fact('<name>')` naming no fact wherever a document reads it (`fact-unknown`). A diff says a fact added, changed or taken away; renaming a kind, a field or a relation renames it in a fact, and removing one removes the facts that read it.
  
  To pay for it on a page, a page that compiles a document its host has already judged (`compileDocumentWithoutCheck`) no longer judges its kinds' computed fields: `compileDocument` and `graview check` do, as they judge an act's ranges and a brand. The hosted page loads 589 821 bytes up front (593 247 before, budget 593 613), 547 520 handed a compiled app (547 742 before, budget 548 045), and the scene 901 241 before it draws (904 615 before), with no hosted-page budget raised. The every-face and studio-handed embed budgets are raised by the checker's growth, and the pages face alone by 145 bytes gzipped for `fact()` and the roles it draws with, as `scripts/lib/bundle-budget.mjs` notes.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: the document's top-level `computed`, the `fact()` function of the rule language, `Schema.computed` and `createSchema`'s second argument, `factValues` and `factNames` on `@graview/core/blocks`, `GRAPH_FACTS` on `@graview/core/document`, `PlaceDescription.facts`, the finding code `fact-unknown`. Changed: `graview check` now refuses facts that read each other in a circle (`computed-cycle` at `computed.<name>`); `computedValues` takes `null` for the whole graph; `compileDocumentWithoutCheck` builds an app whose kind's computed field `compileDocument` would refuse (a page refuses that value where it reads it), so a host that compiles strangers' documents in the page without the checker judges them with `compileDocument` first, as it already must for everything else the checker says.
- 9154faf: A kind can say which field is its natural time and which its author. `fieldRoles` was not a document key at 0.1.20, so n-dx could not declare a commit's `committedAt` its time nor its `author` who made it: commits had no declared order, and the Runs timeline bound its date in the lens. A document's kind says `fieldRoles` as `defineNode` does — `"fieldRoles": { "when": "committedAt", "who": "author" }`, and any role a lens binds by name — and the framework reads two of them. `when`: a list of the kind opens newest first by it at any length, indexed by year when it is large (it was a large list only, by a `start` role, which a kind that wants it now says as `when`); a timeline or calendar that binds no `start` for the kind draws each record there (`withNaturalTime`, exported for a lens of an app's own). `who` and `when`: an unchosen glance leads with them, before the fields as declared. `graview check` refuses a role naming no field (`field-role-missing-field`, now at `kinds.<kind>.fieldRoles.<role>` for a document) and a `when` that is no date or a `who` that is no name (`field-role-ill-typed`); a lens that binds no start passes where the kind says `when`. `graview describe` lists each kind's roles; a diff says a role that moves; renaming a field moves its role, and removing one lets it go; `toDocument` carries a kind's roles; the document's JSON Schema takes them, and `defineDocument` holds each to a field the kind has.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the compiled format, tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `kinds.<kind>.fieldRoles` in the document, the `when` and `who` roles, the finding code `field-role-ill-typed`, `withNaturalTime` on `@graview/primitives`. Changed: a large list no longer opens newest first by a kind's `start` role — say `when` (Discography's albums do); a list of a kind with `when` opens newest first at any length; an unchosen glance leads with `who` and `when`.
- 9154faf: A list over a relation several kinds declare sorts by a choice field of the kinds it reaches. n-dx declares `under` on six kinds — a task under a change, a zone under a zone — and a change's page lists `in('under')` by status in its declared order. The checker held that sort, and a group, to every kind that declares `under`, so a zone, which has no status and is never under a change, refused it. A walk is now judged by the kinds it reaches from where it starts: `in('under')` on a change's page, the kinds that declare `under` to a change; `in(all('zone'), 'under')`, those that declare it to a zone. A choice field one of those kinds lacks is still refused, by name.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the compiled format, check finding codes, tool names and schemas are unchanged. Changed: a list's `sort` and `group` over `in(…)` are judged against the kinds whose relation leads to the walk's start, so a document refused `list-sort` or `list-group` for a kind its list never reaches now compiles.
- 0e1bafe: A snapshot says when it was made and what it holds, and can be read a kind at a time. A snapshot file was one JSON object, `{ nodes, edges }`: to learn how old a projection was, or whether it held any tasks, a reader parsed every record, and n-dx's face loaded a 3.3 MB snapshot whole into a memory adapter on every page load. `@graview/core/snapshot` writes and reads one format for every snapshot file: `writeSnapshot(graph, { generatedAt })` puts a header line first — `"format": 2`, `generatedAt`, records per kind, links per kind and where each kind's lines are, by line and by byte — then one record per line, each kind's together, then the links. `readSnapshotHeader(text)` reads the first line alone; `readSnapshot(text, { kinds })` parses only those kinds' lines and the links among them; `fetchSnapshot(url, { kinds })` fetches the header and then only those kinds' bytes by range, reading the whole answer from a server that serves no ranges. An old `{ nodes, edges }` file reads everywhere as format 1, and a newer format is refused by its number. `graview serve --seed`, `graview sync-seed --seed` and `graview describe --place --seed` read either.
  
  Compatibility: ops, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged, and so is what an adapter stores. New: the snapshot file's line format (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT`, 2), on the stored-formats surface, and `@graview/core/snapshot` (`writeSnapshot`, `readSnapshot`, `readSnapshotHeader`, `fetchSnapshot` and their types). A seed is read as either format. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing.
- 3399521: A kind with no records folds into More until it has one. n-dx's projection of a v1 tree has no constraints, and the bar still said "Constraints" beside the work, whether `pages.primary` named it or the bar ranked it so. Now the bar does not stand an empty kind's list, or its pictures, on the row, declared and derived alike; they are in More, and the kind's first record brings them back. A graph with nothing in any kind folds none, since nothing yet says which kinds matter. The place you are on stands whatever it holds, and a list that is open keeps the row it opened over (FR-158). The embed's bar is drawn again as the graph changes, so the first record shows on the row without waiting for some other change. A kind's list on the bar is named the way every other surface names it (`pluralLabel`), so a kind with no declared plural reads "Shelf items", not "ShelfItems". The bar costs nothing more up front: the hosted page's first load is 24 bytes lighter than before.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged.
- 9154faf: Each kind words its own end of a relation several kinds declare. An edge's name was one relation to every surface, so a record listed everything that reached it by `by` under the first declaring kind's words, and `graview check` refused two kinds declaring one name in two sets of words (`edge-name-shared`). n-dx's `under` sits on six kinds: a change wants "the tasks inside it", a zone "the zones inside it", and they could not both be said. Now a record's page, a record's connections in the scene, and the cards around a focus list what reaches a record by each reading: kinds that word a relation alike together, kinds that word it their own way apart, each under its own words (an artist's page says "Their songs" and "Their releases"), and a band of a kind is captioned in that kind's own words. So the refusal is gone: a kind may word its own end of a shared relation.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, tool names and schemas are unchanged. Changed: the finding code `edge-name-shared` is no longer reported; a document or app it refused now compiles. A record's relation groups (`recordFacts(...).links`, the scene's connections) are one per relation, direction and reading, where they were one per relation and direction.
- f25b52c: `graview doctor` says whether a product is on one Graview release. One release is named five times in a product — the `@graview/*` pins, `graview` beside them, the lockfile, what is installed, and each document's `formatVersion` — and React's pair makes a sixth: n-dx's face met `react` 19.2.8 beside `react-dom` 19.3.0 as a blank page, since React 19.3 refuses a mismatched pair at startup. `graview doctor [<dir>] [--document <file>] [--json]` reads the product's package.json, the nearest lockfile (pnpm, npm, yarn or bun), its node_modules and every `.json` document in it that says it is a Graview declaration, and says each problem as a sentence with its fix: two releases among the pins ("pin them all to 0.1.20"), a pin the install or the lockfile does not follow, a lockfile holding a second copy, a document of a format the installed core does not read, and the React pair to pin. A pin that is a range, a package named by a path (a checkout, a workspace or a tarball), a document of an older format and a `graview` run at another release than the one pinned are said as notes. It exits 1 on any problem. `diagnose` is exported from `@graview/core/cli` for a host that wants the findings as data. The getting-started page and the `graview` README say the npx trap: inside a pnpm workspace that already holds a release in its hidden hoist, `npx -y graview@<version>` skips the install and finds no bin (`sh: graview: command not found`) — run the project's own `node_modules/.bin/graview`.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool schemas are unchanged. Added: a `doctor` command and `diagnose`, `doctor` and `DOCTOR_USAGE` on `@graview/core/cli`.
- 9df7ef3: `resolveFor(store, principal, node, options?)` resolves a record's declared blocks — or the home's, for no record — over what that seat may see, exactly as the scene, the pages face and `describe_place` resolve them, and a record's page is read under a page's budget. n-dx's face built the context blocks are read in by hand — the seat's graph, the schema, every kind's shape, the kind's fields, a budget and a heading — with ten casts, and the promise that its face, the scene and `describe_place` agreed was a comment; and a record page was judged under a card's allowance of 500 steps, so a change with 98 tasks said "—" for every list on its page. `resolveFor` is on `@graview/core/describe`, beside the describer that goes through it: `resolveFor(store, principal, node, { app })` reads the kind's declared page (`slot: "card"` or `"row"` for the others, `blocks` for a caller's own), and the context it reads them in is `blockContextFor` on `@graview/core/blocks`, which the faces read their blocks in. The budget is an option, `budget`; by default a page, the home and a blocks lens — drawn once — take a page's (`PAGE_BUDGET`, 20 000 steps), and a card and a row — drawn many times a frame — keep a card's (`CARD_BUDGET`, 500). The scene's record and the pages face's record page now list a change's 98 tasks where they said "—", and a home or a blocks lens may sweep four times more than it could (5 000 steps before).
  
  The hosted page measures 593_305 bytes up front, 243 fewer than before, and handed a compiled app 547_761, 184 fewer: the compiler, the evaluator and the declaration ask whether an object has a name with `Object.hasOwn`. The scene face measures 904_673 bytes before it draws, 364 fewer, and the pages face alone 535_469 / 191_047, 287 fewer minified and 75 more gzipped, under its budget.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Changed: a declared page, the home and a blocks lens are judged under 20 000 steps where they were judged under 500 and 5 000, so an expression that said "—" for want of steps now says its value. New: `resolveFor`, `ResolveForOptions` and `DeclaredBlocks` on `@graview/core/describe`; `blockContextFor`, `BlockContextOptions`, `BlockSource`, `PAGE_BUDGET` and `CARD_BUDGET` on `@graview/core/blocks`; `useSpecContext` takes the slot it reads for. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing.
- 0e1bafe: The sync engine decides a conflict as it is told, refuses what the remote owns, and is its own entry. A product that projects another system into Graview and syncs back (n-dx's face, over rex) found the engine pushed our value for a field in the same pass it reported that field as a conflict, so the remote was overwritten before anyone could choose; it pre-empted every run by hand. `SyncEngine` now takes `conflicts: "report" | "theirs" | "ours"`. `report`, the default, holds a field both sides changed and sends neither value until `resolve` says which stands, and a later pass that hears nothing new still reports it; `theirs` lands the remote's value through `applyInbound`, authored by the system like any inbound change; `ours` sends ours in the same pass. The report's new `resolved` says each conflict the policy settled and which side it took, and `conflicts` is every one still held.
  
  A mapping's resource may be `readonly`, with a `because` sentence: inbound changes land, nothing is sent, and once `sync.guard()` holds the store, an act that would write a record of it is refused with that sentence as a `PermissionDeniedError` (`sync.admit` is the same judgment as an `ApplyOptions.admit`; `sync.refuses(kind)` says it before an act is offered). A resource's `version` names our field that holds the remote's version, and every record that carries one is linked at it when the engine is made, so a projected graph's first run sends nothing. A local delete the remote refuses, or of a record the remote owns, is refused once with its reason and the link let go, where it was refused again on every pass. A field the mapping only sends (`ours`) is agreed after a push, where it was sent again every pass.
  
  A remote that speaks MCP is `mcpRemote({ name, call, pull, push })`, one write at a time, each refused alone. `call` is `mcpClient({ url, allow, headers })`, which opens a streamable-HTTP session, reads a JSON or event-stream answer, opens another session once when the server forgot it, and refuses a tool not in `allow` before sending it; or, in a page, `doorClient()`, posting to `mcpDoor({ call, allow })` on the server, which holds the token and passes only a POST of `application/json` from its own origin (`sameOrigin`) naming an allowed tool (`allowTools`), saying which it refused. None imports a `node:` builtin.
  
  A store attributes what its reader does: `new Store({ principal })`, or `openStore({ storeOptions: { principal } })`, is the author of every apply and undo that names none, where it was always a person with no name. `rememberedPrincipal()` on `@graview/ship` and `@graview/ship/browser` reads the name a page keeps under `graview:principal` (`PRINCIPAL_KEY`), "reader" when it keeps none or storage is locked away. The hosted page measures 593_548 bytes up front, 59 fewer than before: the store's constructor builds its graph from one `validate` it already held.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Changed: the sync engine, its mappings, its conflict invariant and the Google Calendar transport left `@graview/core` for `@graview/core/sync` (`SyncEngine`, `googleCalendar`, `googleCalendarMapping`, `syncConflictInvariant`, `SYNC_CONFLICTS`, `EMPTY_SYNC_STATE`, `systemAuthor` and their types); a conflict is held and not sent unless `conflicts` says otherwise; `SyncReport.conflicts` is every conflict held, and `SyncState` may carry `conflicts`; `resolve(conflict, "ours")` re-bases on their value. New: `conflicts`, `resolved`, `guard`, `admit` and `refuses` on the engine; `readonly`, `because`, `version` and `remoteId` on a `ResourceMapping`; `mcpClient`, `mcpDoor`, `doorClient`, `mcpRemote`, `allowTools` and `sameOrigin` on `@graview/core/sync`; `principal` on `StoreOptions`; `rememberedPrincipal` and `PRINCIPAL_KEY` on `@graview/ship`. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing.
- 6384676: What the review after 0.1.21 found, each fixed with a test that failed first.
  
  A seat is read only what it sees, whatever it is handed. `resolveFor(store, principal, node)` resolved the record it was given as it was given, so a node read from the whole graph put its fields on a page for a seat that may not see it; it now reads the record through `store.seenBy(principal)`, and one the seat may not see resolves to nothing. `blockContextFor` with a principal and a bare `{ graph, schema }` says that a seat is read from a store, where it threw a `TypeError` about `seenBy`.
  
  The doors answer only this machine's own pages. `sameOrigin` (`@graview/core/sync`) is now the one rule every door keeps — `mcpDoor` and the dev server's model, decision and studio doors alike (`@graview/ship/dev` re-exports it) — and it also refuses a Host that is not this machine (`ownHost`: `localhost`, a `*.localhost` name or an IP address, or one of the `hosts` a door is told), so a page that rebinds its own name to this machine can no longer spend the dev server's model key or call a sync's tools. `mcpDoor` takes only the media type `application/json` (not a type that merely mentions it, which a page on another site may send unasked), refuses a body past `limit` (1 MB) with a 413, and decodes it as UTF-8 across chunks.
  
  `graview preview` keeps to its own page. It believes the seat its page names, so `serveStore`'s new `ownOrigin` refuses every request and live socket not from the preview's own origin on this machine; `servePreview` on a host other machines reach refuses unless `trustSeatHeaders` says the network is trusted (the CLI's `--trust-seat-headers`); `graview preview` takes a free port unless `--port` says one, where it took `graview serve`'s 5196; and a seed that does not read is said as `graview preview:` or `graview shots:` and a sentence, not a stack. `compileDocumentFiles({ seed })` and `graview view check --seed` read a seed in the snapshot's line format as well as an old `{ nodes, edges }` file.
  
  The sync engine keeps to its policy at the edges. A write queued while offline is worked out again rather than replayed, so a field the next pull holds in conflict is not sent; a projected record is agreed at what it was made with, so an edit made before the first run is a conflict rather than lost; `guard()` holds `undo` and `redo` too, so the remote's own change to a record it owns cannot be taken back here, and it judges a patch as the record was and would be, so moving one out of what `match` reads is still a write to it; once it guards the store, `store.permits` and `store.permittedMutations` refuse an act on a record the remote owns with the resource's sentence, so it is not offered and then refused. A record deleted there is removed through the new `removeInbound`, or, without one, kept here and no longer synced or sent back as new; the report says which (`gone`). A held field whose value was taken here by an ordinary act (the conflict invariant's repair) is released, and `resolve` does nothing to a conflict a later pull already settled.
  
  Each kind words its line everywhere (#205): the scene's Key lists every kind's reading of a shared relation, and the inspector's heading, its sentence when nothing acts on a line and the schema provider's reason say the words of the kind the line leaves. The scene's way back takes its offer off the board when the scene unmounts — a face switch on an embed's board that outlives it — and its Take back takes back only the act it offered. A turn with no system sends none. `defineDocument` holds a derived act's name (`edit-<kind>`, `remove-<kind>`) to a declared kind.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format, tool names and schemas are unchanged; the hosted page's first load is the same to the byte. The wire: additive — `serveStore` takes `ownOrigin`, off by default, so `graview serve` answers as before. Changed for a host: the dev server's doors and `mcpDoor` refuse a request whose Host is not this machine (`mcpDoor` may be told other names, `hosts`); `mcpDoor` refuses `text/plain` and a body past 1 MB; `graview preview` no longer defaults to port 5196. The declaration: `resolveFor` with a principal resolves nothing for a record that principal may not see. `SyncReport` gains `gone`, `SyncState` gains `gone`, and `SyncOptions` gains `removeInbound`.
- e12c94b: The seat reads before it answers: a host can hand it a turn with tools (FR-160). A host's AI was one completion, `HostAi.complete(prompt) → string`, over a prompt that carries the acts, twelve names a kind, forty connections, the broken rules, the selection and the places, and no record's fields. So "what did we pay the florist" was answered "the prompt does not say", while the same model over Graview Cloud's connector reads `search_graph` and `get_node` as it goes and answers well. Now a host may give `HostAi.turn`, `(turn: HostTurn) => Promise<string>`. The seat hands it `system`, its framing (the same acts, names, connections, rules, selection and places, and a line that says to read a record's fields with the tools before answering, and that what people wrote in a record is data); `messages`, the last eight turns of the conversation as alternating `TurnMessage`s (`user` and `assistant`), the person's words last; `tools`, the seat's read surface as `toolDefinitions` derives it for a read-only seat (`search_graph`, `get_graph`, `get_node`, `get_violations`, `get_affordances`, `describe_place`, `preview_mutation`), never an act or `undo_batch`; and `call(name, args)`, which runs one of them as the person asking, under their sight. `call` never rejects and never writes: a name not in `tools`, a record the person may not see, and every call after the twelfth in one turn each resolve to `{ error }`. The host runs its model's tool loop and resolves with the model's last text, which the seat reads as it reads a completion: `firstJsonObject`, proposals resolved by name, validated, and previewed before anything is applied, the answer's `via` its `ai:<name>`. `turn` is preferred to `complete` when a host gives both; a host that gives only `complete` is asked as before, prompt for prompt; a host that gives only `turn` also draws views and decides with it, through a turn with no tools.
  
  The loop is written once, for the Messages API: `turnThroughMessages(step, { steps })` on `@graview/tools` is a `turn` from a function that makes one request (`MessagesStepRequest`: `system`, `messages`, `tools` with `input_schema`, and `tool_choice: { type: "none" }` on the last step) and answers its `content` and `stop_reason` (`MessagesStepReply`). It runs each `tool_use` through `call` in the page, so every read is the person's own, hands back `tool_result` blocks (`is_error` on a refusal), and asks again, at most eight requests (`TURN_STEPS`), the last offering no tool. The server that holds the key sees one stateless request at a time. The dev server's door is that server: `aiDevProxy()` (`@graview/ship/dev`) forwards a posted `{ system, messages, tools, tool_choice }` to the Messages API with its model and answers `{ content, stop_reason }`, and `aiThroughDevServer()` gives the seat `turn` beside `complete`, so `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=… pnpm dev` reads before it answers. A build carries no door, and a harness starts its servers with no key.
  
  The seat's model left the face: its framing, the turn and the reading of what came back are `@graview/tools`' `seat-model` module, fetched with the first ask a model answers, and `aiVia` left `./ai.ts`, which every page carries up front, for the seat's own module (it is no longer on `@graview/tools/frame`). Measured on the hosted page: 593 183 bytes up front (593 247 before, budget 593 613), 547 678 handed a compiled app (547 742 before, budget 548 045), the scene face 900 694 before it draws (904 615) and the pages 863 114 (867 034), with the seat's model 48.6 KB fetched when a model first answers; no hosted-page budget is raised. The embed's "every face", which counts every chunk, is one chunk more and the turn's code: 1 703 993 / 537 215 bytes minified and gzipped (1 701 975 / 535 578 before), its budget raised to 1 704 400 / 537 400. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-160.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-160. Changed: `llmResponder` takes `complete` or `turn` (one of the two) and fetches its model's part on its first answer; `aiVia` is on `@graview/tools` and no longer on `@graview/tools/frame`; the dev door answers a posted turn step with `{ content, stop_reason }`. New: `turn` on `HostAi`; `HostTurn`, `TurnMessage`, `turnThroughMessages`, `TURN_STEPS`, `MessagesBlock`, `MessagesStepRequest` and `MessagesStepReply` on `@graview/tools`. What a host does: to let its seat read, give `ai.turn`. A host whose server makes Messages API calls gives `turn: turnThroughMessages((request) => post(request))`, where its route adds the model and the key to the posted `{ system, messages, tools, tool_choice }`, makes one Messages call, and answers `{ content, stop_reason }`; it may keep `complete` or drop it, since a turn with no tools draws views and decides. A server-side tool loop of its own, and the framing it repeated from the seat's prompt, can go: the reads now run in the page, as the person, with the selection and the conversation the seat holds. A host that keeps only `complete` does nothing.
- 0f051ef: Types flow from a document to a face. n-dx's face read every record as `{ id, kind } & Record<string, unknown>`, its fields with `typeof n.status === "string"` and its relations by bare strings, so a renamed relation was a blank section and not a compile error. `graview types <document> [--out <file.d.ts>]` now writes a document's kinds, fields and relations as TypeScript: one interface per kind, with each field's own type (an enum's options as a union, a number as a number, a list as a list; a required field required and the rest optional), `Kinds`, `Edges` (the kinds each relation of each kind reaches), `Types`, `AnyRecord` and `Reader`. It refuses a document that does not compile, with the findings. `typedReader<Types>(store)` from the new `@graview/core/typed` reads a store, or its graph, through those types — `nodes(kind)`, `node(id, kind?)`, `is(node, kind)`, `fieldOf(node, field)`, `edgesOf(node, relation)` and `inwardOf(node, relation)` — so a field or relation renamed in the document, or an option taken out of an enum, fails `tsc` in the product that reads it. A test compiles a face over the generated types as a product's `tsc` would and renames each. `TypesOf<typeof document>` gives the same types from a `defineDocument`, with nothing generated. The reader is its own entry: a page that does not import it pays nothing, and one that does pays for six one-line functions.
  
  A product can delete its interim: n-dx's `Node = { id, kind } & Record<string, unknown>` and the `typeof` reads in `packages/graview-face/src/model/graph.ts`, and the hand-written `DocumentField`, `DocumentKind` and `GraviewDocumentData` in `packages/graview/src/document.ts` (`DocumentDraft` and `defineDocument` say the format).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `graview types`; `typedReader`, `TypedReader`, `DocumentTypes`, `TypesOf`, `RecordOfKind`, `ValueOf` and the name helpers on `@graview/core/typed`.

## 0.1.21

### Patch Changes

- 5f2f3aa: A role that only reads is not warned. `graview check` warned `role-may-do-nothing` on every Graview Cloud template's `viewer`, a role declared to run nothing, because it may run nothing. A role that may only read is the point of a viewer. Now the warning is kept for a role that can neither run a mutation nor see a kind. A role reads every kind where the policy keeps none from anybody (no `sees`), and where it does, a sight that names the role (or every role), even for its own records only, is its reading. The warning now says so: `"guest" may run no mutation and see no kind, so anyone holding it can do nothing at all`, at `policy (role "guest")`. The vendors conformance fixture's viewer no longer draws it, announced for 0.1.21. The permissions skill says what the warning means.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format and tool schemas are unchanged, and so is the finding code. Changed: `role-may-do-nothing` is reported only for a role that can neither act nor read, where it was reported for every role that may run no mutation; its message, fix and `where` say what it now means. A host that counts warnings counts one fewer for each reader role.
- 615f368: A broken rule says its values to a host that reads only its message (FR-159). 0.1.20's notes said a rule shows its shape everywhere. That was true of the framework's own surfaces and not of a host's: `Violation.line` was set only where a surface called `withLines` from `@graview/core/lines`, and a violation's `message` was still the rule's title. Graview Cloud's connector hands a chat the message, and authors had shortened their titles to let the shape speak, so since 0.1.20 a chat read "A wedding planner on Small: Margin above target" with no values. Now, once `@graview/core/lines` is loaded, the engine gives every judgment its line: `evaluate`, so `store.violations()`, `preview`'s `violationsAfter` and every surface over them carry `line` on each broken rule written in the rule language, and a rule that wrote no sentence of its own (`says`) says the line in its `message` after its title: "A wedding planner on Small: Margin above target — margin 31% < target 40%". A rule's own sentence is left as its author wrote it, its line beside it, and a rule that is a function is as it was. `@graview/ship`'s store handler (`createStoreHandler`, `serveStore`, `graview serve`) loads the words when it is made, and a seat's tools (`createToolRuntime`, so `graview mcp` and a connector's runtime) fetch them before `get_violations` or `get_node` answers, so those answer the values in the message, as an act's `introduces` and `resolves` do from then on, still without the parts a page draws the line with. A page still carries none of the words up front: it fetches them when it first draws a problem, as before, and its judgments carry lines from then on; the engine's part is 68 bytes, measured on the hosted page at 593 446 bytes up front (593 378 before, budget 593 613) and 547 843 handed a compiled app (547 775 before, budget 548 045), and the door the seat's tools fetch the words through 88 more before either face draws, with no budget raised. An act that changes a broken rule's values and leaves it broken neither resolves nor introduces it: a violation with a line is the rule and its record (`violationKey`). `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-159.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-159. Changed: the `message` of a broken rule written in the rule language with no `says` gains " — " and its line once `@graview/core/lines` is loaded, which a store served by `@graview/ship` and a seat's tools now always have; a host that matched such a message by its exact words matches its start. New: `judgeWithLines()` on `@graview/core/lines`. What a host with its own problems surface does: nothing, where it judges through a store `@graview/ship` serves, through a seat's tools, or on a page that has drawn a problem. A host that judges on a server with none of those, in a bundle that may drop an import nothing names, calls `judgeWithLines()` from `@graview/core/lines` once at start; `withLines(store, violations)` still adds the line, and the values in the message, to violations judged before.
- eaffce3: The places list renders once as it opens (FR-158), and who else is here is on the bar, on both faces and a phone (FR-155). Graview Cloud, after 0.1.20: the bar ranks its places and folds the rest into More, and the row is weighed again whenever its room may change — a face arriving, a count on the standing, a brand's face loading — so a list already open could be drawn again with a new ranking, entries moving between the row and the list under a press already on its way; Playwright said "element is not attached", and on a slow phone a tap did nothing or hit the entry that moved there. While the list is open, the places now stay as they stood when it opened, every entry the element it was; the new ranking comes when it closes. `verify-chrome-quiet` holds an entry from before the open and presses it at once after, on Pages and on the scene, at a desk and on a phone, the scene also reached by the switch from Pages as Cloud reaches it.
  
  Presence was drawn as figures on the scene and nowhere else, so on Pages and on a phone a reader could not tell who was on the app with them, and Cloud stood a stack of its own under the bar. The one bar draws it now, wherever it is drawn — the Shell, the routed face's own bar, an embed's — from the presence the provider already holds: each other person a letter in a circle ringed in their own hue, as the person's own tool is drawn; an agent a letter in a square; three marks on a desk, two on a phone's bar, the rest counted, viewers not signed in among them. Pressed, it lists each by name — "Claude, for Ada" for an agent — and where each one is in the declaration's words (a picture by its title, a record by its label, a kind by its plural, "the whole thing" from above), never an id and nothing this reader may not see; on the scene a name goes where they are, as their figure does. It is a polite status to assistive technology ("Ada Lovelace and 2 viewers are here"), one mark per person however many tabs they have open, and nothing at all when nobody else is here, your own other tabs included. The marks and the list are fetched with the bar's panes once somebody else is here, so a reader alone carries none of it: the hosted page measures 593_425 bytes up front, 88 fewer than before, and the pages face alone 536_675, 118 fewer. `usePopover` takes `"here"` (`here-open`, `here-list`). `verify-who` holds it on the scene, on Pages and at a phone's width, and that the bar draws nobody once the others have gone. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-155 and FR-158.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format, wire messages, check finding codes and tool schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` names two more requirements. What Graview Cloud changes: once it takes this release it can delete `hereStack` in its shell (INTERIM(FR-155)) and its style, which the bar now draws in the bar's own row; and its views harness can press a place it held from before the list opened.
- 25019d4: The overview's address alone is the overview (FR-154). Graview Cloud: the framework wrote the overview at altitude as `/places/overview#overview=1` and read the altitude from the fragment alone. A fragment never reaches a server, so the same address back through any hop — the sign-in door, a stored link, a bookmark, or typed — came back as `/places/overview` and opened the overview descended on nothing: an empty scene whose control said "Up". Now `/places/overview` with no fragment, or with one that says nothing (`#`), opens the overview at altitude, on the Graview's face, under address routing in an embed and on a hosted page, and on any page that keeps its stop in the fragment (`UrlSync`, the whole-page Shell's `syncUrl`). The framework writes the overview at altitude over nothing in particular as the bare `/places/overview`, so the address it writes means the overview by itself: the bar's switch, Back to it, and "The whole thing" among the scene's places (`data-place-path="/places/overview"`). A fragment that says where it stands is read as it says: `#focus=t1` is the scene descended on that record, and `#overview=1&focus=…` the overview over it; a link written before, `/places/overview#overview=1`, still opens the overview and is tidied to the bare address in place, with no step Back would have to undo. Arriving at an address that says something no longer writes `#` and then pushes the stop: the page adopts the address with no entry. New in `@graview/layout/view`: `overviewStop` (the stop the overview's address holds) and `overviewFragment` (the fragment it carries for a view, none at altitude over nothing). `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-154.
  
  A host can delete its interim: Graview Cloud's `restoreOverview` in `packages/client/src/shell.ts`, which put `#overview=1` back on a bare `/places/overview` before the framework read the window, does nothing the framework does not do now.
  
  The hosted page measures 120 bytes more up front (593 498, under its 579.7 KB) and 120 more handed a compiled app (547 895, under its 535.2 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-154. Changed: the overview at altitude is written as `/places/overview` with no fragment where it was `/places/overview#overview=1` (an embed's address bar, the scene's "The whole thing" `data-place-path`); a bare `/places/overview`, or `/places/overview#`, opens on the Graview's face at altitude, whatever face the host names, where it opened the scene descended on nothing. `where()` still reports the overview's stop as `#overview=1`.
- f062004: What the review after 0.1.20 found, mended. A reader on Pages is said where they are on the others' bars (FR-155): the routed face said nothing of its own, so a reader on Pages stood at the scene's last stop on everybody else's bar, or nowhere, and inside an embed the face's own provider and the embed's both said where one tab was, two places by turns. Now the routed face says its page as the stop that page is on the scene — a record, its kind's group, a picture, the overview, nothing for the home, Find or the problems — through the one provider that says it (`sayWhere` on `useGraview()`), so the bar says "On Pay the deposit" for a reader on that task's page, by its label and never its id, and their figure on the scene stands at it. In the list of who is here a kind is said by its plural as the declaration says it, never its name as written, and the status a screen reader hears is written once it is on the page, so the first person to come in is said.
  
  Pages has Activity (FR-152). Once its ten seconds were up the notice that offered a change back went, and a person on Pages with a mouse had no way to the change at all: only ⌘Z, or Activity on the scene. The routed face's bar now draws the scene's Activity — each change with its own way back — fetched with the bar's panes once anything has happened, so a face nobody has changed carries none of it (`BarActivity` on `@graview/primitives/pages`). A popover closed by Escape gives the keyboard to its trigger as it is in the document now, by its `aria-controls`, and again a frame later if it was left on `<body>`, which WebKit did after the bar's Activity. A notice on Pages says a new offer after a change was taken back, where it went on saying "Took back …". On the scene, pressing an offer's Take back left the keyboard on the notice's ×, so every offer after, said in the same place, was held by a keyboard nobody had put there and never went; the keyboard lands on the app's name now, as it lands on the page's title on Pages.
  
  A seat's first act says a broken rule's values (FR-159): the words were fetched by a problems call alone, so an act answered before one said `introduces` and `resolves` without them; the seat's tools now fetch them before any answer, and a preview and an undo answer without the parts a page draws a line with, as an act does. A seat that may not see the records a rule's line reads is told the line over what it sees: a line worked out over the whole graph could name up to three of them, or sum their values, in a problem the seat may see, on Pages and through the seat alike (FR-55); an act's answer to such a seat carries no line. A problem judged before the words arrived is the same problem after (`problemsMoved`, `shapedRules`), so the scene's activity no longer marked every broken rule new once they came. A long text in a line is said as its first 60 characters.
